# Changelog, v0.1 to v0.100 (archived)

> The versions of `CHANGELOG.md` before v0.110, moved there verbatim on 2026-10-10 (issue #31): the journal had reached 600 KB, and the versions an engineer still looks up are the recent ones. Newest first, as there; nothing below this header was changed, and an entry's `§` and file names are those of its day (`docs/README.md` maps a § to its file today).

---

- **v0.100** (2026-09-29) — an RFEM model read for what the solver found, and beside it the scripts, the keyword lines, the variables tab and a `style:` on three more commands. Four sessions worked on one tree; this entry carries the RFEM half, and the sections of the others follow it.

  **What the engineer reported, and why three of his four points were one defect.** He sent four things wrong with the RFEM tab: «les bancs sont en biais, alors qu'ils sont horizontaux dans rfem», «une poutre en biais, je ne sais même pas ce que c'est», «un modèle en T (correcte), possède un système de barre dessous qui n'est pas dans rfem et ne fait aucun sens», and that the internal-force diagrams were straight lines where «les efforts peuvent également être courbes». The first three are **one line**. `model_tables._length` resolved the right column by its full name and then asked the table for its **bare** name again — and a node table carries two columns called «X» and two called «Z»: «Coordonnées», measured from the node's own reference node, and «Coordonnées globales». `Table.column` registers the first of each (`setdefault`), so every node given relative to another came back at its *relative* place. Node 81 read (0.5, 0) where it stands at (20.787, −3), so a 0.5 m canopy panel was drawn as a 20 m slanted bar; node 97 read (4.23, 0) where it stands at (20.97, 6.05), which laid the Pavillon's bench across the bike shelter — and the bench's misread axis passed **exactly** through the shelter's own node 8 at (8, 0), so `structure_detection`'s union-find welded it into that structure, which is the «système de barre dessous». Seven of 62 members contradicted the length RFEM states for them; none does now, and that invariant is the test. ⚠️ The defect lived because **the fixture wrote one pair of coordinate columns where RFEM writes two**; it writes both now, node 4 being the trap.

  **Two more of the same family, found on the way.** ⚠️ One sheet holds **two blocks of different widths**: `SP1 | Barres | Efforts internes` writes 1812 rows with a leading section column, a «Total» and a «max/min» trailer, then 770 more rows without it, every cell one to the left; every `CO` sheet does the same, 84 rows one way and 578 the other. `criterion_column` therefore found no column whose values were all force symbols, so the extremes test never fired and **84 extremes rows per combination were read as stations** and drawn on the diagrams. The criterion is found **in the row** now (`results.criterion_at`); CO1 went from 222 stations to 144 and the row accounting balances. ⚠️ And `MOMENT_UNITS` gave a newton-metre 1e−6 where it is 1e−3 of a kilonewton-metre, and a newton-millimetre 1e−9 where it is 1e−6 — **the test encoding the same error**, so the two agreed with each other and with nothing else. Nothing showed it: this model states kNm and the only factor ever used was 1.0.

  **The curves are reconstructed, exactly, from the export alone** (`calc/rfem_import/diagram_curve.py`). RFEM lists a member's forces at its characteristic points only — the engineer's 4.23 m beam is four rows, x = 0, 2.115, 2.115, 4.23 — so a polyline through them draws a triangle where the diagram is a parabola; the ten divisions RFEM draws its own screen curve from are in the model and not in the table (checked against the API: 54 285 rows, stations every 2.95/7 m). The shape is read back out of the numbers already exported, by the relation that **the shear is the slope of the moment**: value and slope at two points are four conditions and a cubic has four coefficients, so the Hermite interpolant *is* the diagram wherever it is a polynomial of degree ≤ 3 — exact under a uniform load, exact under a linearly varying one. ⚠️ The sign of the relation is **measured**, both being tried over every segment of a table and the one that reproduces the exported moments kept; where neither does, nothing is curved and the polyline is drawn as before. ⚠️ Only the moments are reconstructed, and the values *written* on the figure stay the stations' own. Verified: integrating V_z over member 2 of CO1 gives **−8.527614** against RFEM's **−8.527615**.

  **The extremes are read and never enveloped** (`calc/rfem_import/envelopes.py`). RFEM's four result-combination tables — SP1 ELU fondamental, SP2 ELS rare, SP3 ELS fréquent, SP4 ELS quasi-permanent — carry the extreme **and** «Chargement correspondant», the combination that reached it. So «l'effort maximal, et la combinaison de charge qui a calculé cet effort maximal» is a reading of 4 tables in 6 s, where the 48 combinations' forces alone cost 11.9 s. One reader serves forces, deformations and reactions, because the criterion names the column it is extreme in. Validated against the totals RFEM writes at the foot of its own sheet: max M_y **23.77363**, min **−18.062302** — the two numbers on the engineer's own screen.

  **The `rfem` domain** (§3.3quindecies): one instance per named structure, in `data/rfem.json`, carrying the §3.3 shape so `#val`, `#find`, `#show-calc` and the summary tables read it unchanged; `engine` is a fourth kind, `rfem`, and every var's source names the export, the situation and the combination. A bar is one RFEM member, `T001_1`, the index always written. The situation is part of the name, `SP1_T001_1_M_y_max`, since one instance per structure means four maxima per bar. ⚠️ A quantity identically zero on a bar is dropped and **named** — a plane model has six, **4955 of 7696 vars**, and the file fell from 2.4 MB to 1.05. Three commands: `rfem-max` (one extreme with its combination), `show-rfem-forces` (a bar's table of them, or a support node's reactions), `show-rfem-combination` (`1.35 · CC1 + 1.50 · CC2`, or with the cases' names, `(EG + AL)` parenthesised because a name may itself be a sum).

  **An imported model's presentation is the engineer's** (`calc/domains/statics_view.py`, his answer B of three): the model stays generated and RFEM stays the source, and `Woodcore-Datei/statics/views/<id>.json` — **in a subfolder**, which `statics.source_paths`' glob does not reach, the `details/` trap of §3.3decies met a second time — holds the three size factors and which internal force is drawn for which loading. The import renders it, `statics.prepare` takes the SVG out into `data/statics/` so no figure's source text sits in a file Typst reads on every compile, and `#show-static-model(model, diagram: true)` prints it.

  **And his model was renamed through the API**: the 36 members whose comments named T002 and S003 now name T001 and S001, one changed first and the results checked before the other 35 — **54 285 result rows intact**, no recalculation. The section mismatch that follows (RFEM 500/200, `Positionen.xlsx` 100/200 for T001) is what he asked to be tolerated, and `validation.sections_agree` has always been a warning.

  **Tests**: +9 `test_rfem_curve`, +11 `test_rfem_envelopes`, +21 `test_rfem_commands`, +8 `test_statics_view`, +2 `test_model_tables`, each of the last two fired against the planted defect. ⚠️ **Three widenings in `tests/test_commands.py`** — `EXEMPT_FROM_RENDERING`, the `quoted` whitelist, the `honest` list — each carrying its reason, all three of them the same absence: the example project names no RFEM export, so the six commands are documented and invisible on the website. One small export in `tests/projet-exemple/` takes all three back out (§14).

  ⚠️ **Also in this version, by other hands**: the scripts and the keyword lines (§3.3sexdecies, §3.3septdecies), the variables tab, and a `style:` argument on `var`, `var-unit` and `val` mirroring `#eq`'s — the engineer's own «ajoute à #var l'argument style comme pour #eq», implemented, documented and translated, and the reason the `val-*`, `var-*` and `var-unit-*` frozen pages moved. That last one is **not in CLAUDE.md**: its `///` blocks carry it and the §10bis rows do not.

  **And beside the RFEM half, the engineer's own list of changes, answered by his own decisions** (§3.3sexdecies, §3.3septdecies, §7, §10bis, §10quater, §12, §13, §14). He sent a list with the answers he had already given another session, and the four pieces below are what they came to.

  ⚠️ **The report's folder is named when a project is created, and `project.DEFAULT_LAYOUT` does not move** — which is the opposite of the obvious way, and had to be. «Lors de la création d'un nouveau projet dans woodcore, le nom de ce dossier est demandé. Par défaut "Rapport de statique", dépendant de la langue choisie», and «sans (typst)». The constant names the folder three times (`report`, `pdf`, `generated`), and `Project.__init__` is `{**DEFAULT_LAYOUT, **data.get("layout", {})}` — and **both real projects state no `layout` at all**, which was checked rather than assumed: the engineer's own `Musterprojekt.woodcore` carries name, company, author and `gui`, the example project's name, company and author. Renaming the constant would have made the report folder of both unfindable in one edit. So it stays, as «the layout of a project created before v0.100»; a new project writes its own `layout` (only `report` — `templates.layout_of` derives `pdf` and `generated` from it); and **nothing on disk is renamed in a project that already exists**. `templates/projet/`'s own folder is likewise *not* renamed, since `stated_layout` compares a template against that constant and renaming it would make Standard *state* a folder, after which the language's word would never be offered again. One consequence worth recording: `packaging/build.py` and `packaging/launcher.py` needed no change at all. ⚠️ And the name is checked **before anything is created** — refused after the skeleton had been copied, it left a half-copied project and the next attempt then failed on «is not empty» instead of on the name.

  ⚠️ **A script of the project: Python the engineer writes, run by Woodcore, whose named results the report reads.** «Je souhaite avoir un onglet dans woodcore, où le user peut écrire du code python et le run», with a pane showing the values his variables took «afin de ne pas avoir à lire le terminal». Of three ways a result could reach the report he chose the traceable one: `result("H_d", 2.2, "kN")` → `data/scripts.json` → `#val("H_d", domain: ("script", "portique"))`. Its best property is that it needed **no new Typst command** — `val`, `show-calc` and `find` already read a `(domain, instance)` pair, so only `find-calculation` gained a branch. **numpy alone and not SciPy** (his choice: `numpy.linalg` solves any linear system, and SciPy would have taken the installer from 63 MB past 130, with no room beside the site on Pages' 100 MB). **Read-only on the project**, by a guard that replaces `open` and names the file it refuses — ⚠️ documented **as a guard and not a sandbox**, since `os.open` and `subprocess` get round it, a promise of safety nobody can keep being worse than a guard somebody understands. It runs in a process of its own (`woodcore-cli run-script`), bounded by a timeout, and **every export runs every script**, a failure being a warning and never a stop. ⚠️ **`seconds` was exported for one afternoon and is not**: a wall-clock duration made `data/scripts.json` differ between two exports of an unchanged project, against §12's promise that an unchanged export stays unchanged to the byte — the reason `data/project.json` carries no date. It was invisible until the example project carried a script, which is what made `test_export.test_the_export_is_reproducible` say so.

  ⚠️ **A member's checks written in keyword lines, by a parser and not by a model.** «Est-il possible d'avoir un onglet où le user peut écrire des formules de manières simples, non formatées en typst» — and, asked whether he wanted an AI for it, «n'implémente pas l'IA», a deterministic parser sufficing. `calc/dsl.py` is a grammar of a dozen words, French, German and English, matched folded: the same lines always give the same Typst, a line outside the grammar is **refused naming the line** and listing what is understood, and it cannot invent a command that does not exist. What it gives up is tolerance. It emits `#member` and the `verify-*` commands, ⚠️ **each with the arguments that command takes and no other** — `verify-shear` given a buckling length would refuse, and a translator whose output does not compile is worse than none, which is why the check that matters most compiles the emitted Typst and reads the member's name back off the page. The «Formules» tab shows what he writes, the Typst it becomes and **the page it compiles to** (his own requirement), and inserts into a section only after showing what will be added.

  **And the two a session that ended mid-work had left**: `#var(style:)`, `#var-unit(style:)` and `#val(style:)` — «**R_k = 7 kN**», the whole line and not the value — whose `///` bullets, examples and translations were already in and whose **tests were not**; and the white around a heading, in the template's `config.typ` and the engineer's own (2 em/1 em, 1.6/0.8, 1.2/0.6, 1 em/0.5). ⚠️ The `style:` check is measured with **`underline` and not `bold`**, and that is what makes it decisive: underlining changes no glyph's width, so a styled line can only come out wider than a plain one if the `=` is **inside** the wrapper — which is precisely «the whole line and not the value». ⚠️ The heading spacings move **no frozen example page**, checked before writing rather than discovered in a diff: `build_examples.KEPT` is `{"commands.typ", "config.typ"}`, so `examples/config.typ` is kept and never refreshed from the template.

  ⚠️ **What four sessions in one working tree cost, written down because it is the most transferable thing here.** A test of this half globbed the shared example project's `scripts/` folder and so deleted the fixture's own script; `test_script` sorts before `test_window`, so a **full** run went red on the Python tab while `-k test_window` passed, the check that broke the fixture never having run. **A `-k` run does not reproduce an ordering defect.** The rule is that a check writing into the shared copy removes what it wrote and **nothing it found**. Two whole-suite runs were void for the same family of reason — the fixture changed under them — and one `test_commands` failure was NextCloud holding `examples/lib/formula.typ` open while 306 examples compiled in a thread pool (`os error 32`), which a peer then found was also telling the engineer his report did not compile when it did. Line endings cost a pass too: a Python insertion carries LF into a CRLF file, while the Edit tool preserves what it finds — and a heredoc collapses **doubled** backslashes only, a single escape passing through intact.

- **v0.99** (2026-09-29) — five things the engineer asked for in one message: the bibliography of every document the report cites, a revision journal of its own, a thickness factor on every entry of every drawing, a schema's text set over its own box, and the summary tables of the loads and the main results (§3.3sexies, §3.3octies, §3.3decies, §3.3terdecies, §3.3quaterdecies, §10, §10bis, §10quater, §10quinquies, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14)

  **Why, and the one criterion that governed all of it.** Six improvements came in one message; the second — a connector's resistance recomputed at the Typst call from a β the report states — he dropped on being shown what it costs («laisse le point 2 tomber. Le user fera une vérification manuelle de son côté»). His answers to the questions put on the other five are the design, and are recorded decision by decision in `CLAUDE.md` §13. The criterion he set for the whole lot is that **no page of a report already written moves**, and it is held two ways throughout: by compiling the same body with and without each new thing and comparing the pages to the text and to the millimetre, and by re-rendering the frozen example pages — of which **only the eight detail drawings that were meant to move did**, all eight of them a schema whose labels gained their box.

  **The bibliography: every norm the report cites, named in full at the end of it.** The engineer: «je souhaite un fichier qui contient toutes les références aux normes ; ensuite, lorsqu'une norme est affichée, elle doit être affichée dans la bibliographie de fin». A citation in the right-hand column stays exactly what it was — «SIA 265 §4.2.1», «SIA 265 Tabelle 24» beside a table of distances — and is **registered** as it is made; `#show-bibliography` prints at the end of the report the four fields of every document actually cited: its designation, its full title, its edition and its publisher, in three blocks — *Normen*, *Literatur*, and *Weitere Dokumente* for a citation no entry names, printed as it was cited. `#cite-norm(norm: "VKF 14-15", norm-ref: "2.5.3")` cites one in the prose and registers it. `Sections/007-literatur.typ` is where the list stands, in the template and in the example project. ⚠️ **No existing page moves**, which was his condition: nothing of Typst's own `#cite`/`#bibliography` is used and no CSL style decides anything, `norm-text` is untouched, and the 276 frozen example pages rendered before and after are **byte-identical, all 276**. ⚠️ **The engine knows thirteen documents** — `calc/norms/norms.json`, checked by `calc/norms/norms.py` as every other file of that folder is: SIA 260:2013, 261:2020, 261/1:2020, 263:2013, 265:2021, 265/1:2018, SN EN 14592+A1:2012, SZS C5 (Revision 2018), STE Compact 02, bauforumstahl's Arbeitshilfe 02-9 and the VKF/AEAI directives 13-15, 14-15 and 15-15 — **every title, edition and publisher read off the edition's own title page** in `ExternalFiles/000-Normes/`, each entry naming which in its `_source`, which the reader requires and which the report never prints, the engineer having refused naming the library's file on the page. Two entries say what the library cannot give: SN EN 14592+A1:2012, whose titles are read off its successor's title page, which states that it replaces it, and bauforumstahl's Arbeitshilfe, whose pages cannot be read as text and whose designation, publisher and year are the PDF's own metadata. ⚠️ **A project adds its own** in a `references.bib` in the report's folder, read by a BibTeX subset written in Typst — `@string` and `@preamble` refused, since a string macro changes how every value after it is read, an unknown `@type` refused with the list, an unnamed field ignored so that a .bib written for something else can be used as it stands, and **what it cannot read refused naming the line**; a project's entry wins over the engine's for a key both carry. ⚠️ **It is the entry that says its block**, which corrects the engineer's own rule: «tout ce qui est nommé par l'argument `norm` est une norme» is contradicted by his own data — bauforumstahl's Arbeitshilfe is a connection's `DEFAULT_NORM` and «Sherpa / ETA-12/0197» is written with `norm:` in `Sections/005`. ⚠️ **What is registered, and where**: the **data is walked** — every `norm` field of `data/calculations.json`, `data/connections.json` and `data/connectors.json` — which covers `show-calc`, `show-input`, `show-polar-moment`, `show-connector-resistance` and `show-live-load` with **no edit to any of them**; and `var`, `eq`, `val`, `var-unit`, `var-line`, `distance` and `#cite-norm` register their own `norm:`, `lib/timber.typ` needing nothing, every one of its checks citing through `var` and `eq`. Every registration is emitted **before** its command's `context` — v0.85's panne avoided rather than met again — and `norm-text`, the one function that renders a citation, was deliberately left alone, its result being *measured* by `row-cell` and `chain-rows`. `state.final()` is what lets the list stand wherever the engineer puts it. ⚠️ **A norm follows the record and not the level**: a `#var(..., norm: "SIA 265", level: 0)` registers SIA 265, so the client, engineer and checking reports cite the same documents for the same numbers. **Two defects the tests found, both in the Typst reader and both real**: `\r\n` is **one** grapheme cluster, so on a file saved on Windows — which is every file the engineer saves — no cluster ever equalled `"\n"`, every refusal said line 1, every `%` comment ran to the end of the file and every entry written over more than one line was refused as «never closed»; and **an empty array joined is `none` and not `""`** in Typst, so a stray `@` at the end of a file, an empty key, an empty field name and an empty value each failed on «type none has no method `trim`» instead of being refused with its line. `tests/test_bibliography.py`, 35 checks, eleven planted defects in the catalogue and nine in a .bib.

  **The revision journal: written by hand in Woodcore, printed as a table, a list or a grid.** The engineer: «Dans woodcore, avoir un module qui permet d'enregistrer dans un journal les modifications qui ont été faites (date, nom de la personne, nom court de l'entreprise (e.g. FLL), label, description courte, description longue). Level 1: label, level 2: label + description courte, level 3: label + description longue. Cela doit pouvoir se faire afficher en tableau, liste ou gride dans typst.» ⚠️ **One file for the whole project**, `Woodcore-Datei/journal.json` (`calc/journal.py`, `schemas/journal-source.schema.json` 1.0), and not one file per entry as every other source here: a journal is read as a whole and in order, and nothing addresses one revision by an id. ⚠️ **An entry is written by hand**, one per revision (his decision): nothing appends one when a drawing is saved, which would leave twenty entries for an afternoon's work. Its seven fields are his six and **an index** — Rev. 1, 2, 3, as a revision block numbers on paper, which is also what the journal is ordered by; Woodcore proposes the largest plus one, so a gap is never refilled. ⚠️ **The export is newest first** (his decision), ordered by the index **and never by the date**: the date is typed and may be wrong, and `validate_journal_business` holds that order rather than trusting it, since `show-journal` never sorts and `journal.latest` is simply the first entry. A project with no journal exports an empty one and never an error, and `export_all.seed` writes one, so a new project's section compiles from the moment the project exists. ⚠️ **`#show-journal(form: "table" | "list" | "grid", level:, title:)`**: the table through `render-table` like every other table of this engine (three rules and nothing else), the list one block per revision, the grid cards several to a line as the start page sets its recent projects. **The levels govern the three texts and nothing else** — a table is a table, so the number, the day, the name and the short form are printed at every level. ⚠️ **The engineer's own words are never translated, structurally**: they go into the table as content, which `render-table` now passes through untouched (`table-cell`, one additive branch) — free text is the one case where the glossary's rule can go wrong, «Element 3 ergänzt» matching the template «Element {n}». The column headings are the engine's words, in `calc/i18n/strings.json`, in three languages. ⚠️ **A narrow prose column is hyphenated**, in this table alone: `render-table` sets its cells ragged and Typst hyphenates only what it justifies, so a German compound ran past its column's edge. ⚠️ **The PDF's keywords name the latest revision**, «Rev. 3 (2026-09-25)» (his decision): read from `data/project.json`'s new optional `revision` and not from the journal, a `set document` rule being unable to ask whether a second file is there — so a record written before v0.99 still compiles. ⚠️ **The archive holds the journal**: `archive_export.members()` walked the five source *folders*, so a file at the top of `Woodcore-Datei/` reached no archive — and an archive is what reopens Woodcore on the state a PDF was produced from. ⚠️ **The «Journal» tab** (`calc/tools/journal_tab.py`, group *Projet*, after *Positions*): a flat list newest first — a journal is chronological, so no folder and no `grouped_list` — and a form in cards refusing what `journal.check_source` would refuse, before anything is written, under the field it concerns; every write goes through `calc/journal.py` and is followed by an export of the journal alone. ⚠️ **The report's own page**, `Sections/008-journal.typ` of the project template, last after the literature: inserting it earlier would renumber six sections, and *Paramètres*' sections editor moves it forward in one click. `tests/test_journal.py`, 22 checks.

  **The three factors every figure is drawn with, and an entry's own over them.** ⚠️ **One vocabulary for the three** (`calc/domains/figure_style.py`): `line` on the strokes, `point` on the symbols placed on a point, `text` on the texts, from 0.25 to 3, read by the plans, the static models and the detail schemas — which had none of the three until now — so that a slider means one thing in every tab; and **one entry may state a factor of its own over the drawing's**, a factor multiplying and never a size, only for the factors that entry is drawn with, one it is not being refused **naming the ones it may state**. Both renderers honour them, being the one SVG, and the export records the drawing's three and every entry's own. **No `schema_version` moved**: a field whose absence has a meaning is additive (v0.69). In the window, three sliders in a card of their own and, in the selection, one slider per factor the entry may state — **orange with «Reprendre»** the moment it states one, an overridden b/h's own way of saying it (v0.75), and setting the slider back to 100 % is still an override. ⚠️ **The position plan**: an element takes all three, an axis `line` and `text`, `point` on an axis refused naming the two. `plan_svg.entry_sizes` hands every function that draws one entry that entry's sizes, and `number_frame` reads the element's factors **itself**, since Woodcore's canvas, `plan.warnings` and the export's `label_at` all ask for a number's box with the plan's; the scaling is idempotent by construction (`Sizes.drawing`), a factor applied twice being a box 1.69 times its size with nothing saying so. The four overlays of the example project and the whole of its `data/plans.json` are identical to the byte, held against a SHA-256 frozen before the version was written. A `style` key the plan does not know is now refused, where it was silently ignored. `tests/test_plan_style.py`, 20 checks, four planted defects tried. ⚠️ **The static models**: `points` `text`, `members`, the two kinds of load and the dimensions `line` and `text`, `supports` and `releases` all three; `statics.STYLE_KEYS` and `STYLE_RANGE` are `figure_style`'s now rather than restated. `layout()` works the pair out once per entry and hands it down as that entry's own `sz` — its symbol, its box in the figure's frame, the clearance its label is set at, its label's own box and leader, and the halo `show-static-model` may put under it — and `meta()` records what an entry stated and **nothing at all** where it stated none. **A load's `line` carries its arrow's length**, so two loads of equal magnitude no longer draw equal arrows where one states a factor, deliberately and as the only place a factor changes more than a weight; **two things keep the drawing's factors on purpose**, the break a release opens at a node (the cut is the node's, and both ends meeting there must be drawn back alike, which v0.55 repaired) and a spring's zigzag amplitude, a member stating no `point` of its own; and **a halo grows with what it marks**, a ring thinner than the symbol under it marking nothing. `tests/test_statics.py` freezes the figure of a kitchen-sink model by its SHA-256 in all three languages and holds beside it the invariant that never goes stale — a `style` holding 1 for every factor draws the same figure, which is the multiplication itself being exact. ⚠️ Found on the way: `stylefactors.EntryRows` composes its keys from the tab's prefix, so `st_style_take_back` was named by no literal and `tests/test_i18n.test_every_window_string_is_named_by_code` said so — each tab now names the four keys the widget asks it for in a `STYLE_ROW_KEYS` of its own and checks them at import, a missing word otherwise raising in front of the engineer the moment he selected something.

  **A schema's text set over its own box, and a line that gives way to it.** ⚠️ A schema's texts are set over **their own box** — a white rectangle at 0.85, its own box turned with its text — where each was drawn twice with a wide white stroke: a cloud round every letter, `statics_svg`'s own finding of v0.77, made here for a piece's label, a marker's id, a point's name and a dimension's value at once. Translucent because a schema's label sits *on* the piece it names, the middle of the longest stretch no other piece covers (v0.75), so an opaque ground would take a bite out of that piece's fill. ⚠️ **And a line gives way to a text**: a shown construction line is cut at the boxes of the labels it crosses, in the exported figure and in Woodcore's canvas alike, and a dimension line under its own value — the aids being drawn **over** everything (v0.77), no ground behind a text could mend a line drawn over it. What moved in an existing drawing: the two lines of each label's halo, and nothing else — held on the engineer's own detail, and `data/statics.json`, `data/plans.json` and their figures are identical to the byte. `tests/test_figure_style.py`, three planted defects, each caught.

  **The summaries: the loads, the line loads and the main results, by domain.** The engineer: «des tableaux récapitulatifs des charges (kN/m2), des charges linéaires (kN/m), puis finalement, des résultats principaux, le tout par domaine». Three commands in a file of their own, `lib/summary.typ`, imported by name into `lib/formula.typ` as `lib/distance.typ` is — and importing only `calc-engine.typ`, so with not one import inside a command's body and with `commands.typ`, a file of the *project*, unchanged. ⚠️ **`show-load-summary`** sets in one table every `results` name of every case of every load domain in kN/m², one row per case, grouped by domain by a **bold row of its own** carrying the domain's label — `render-table`'s `group` naming a run of columns and not of rows. The domains come in the export's order, the permanent actions before the variable ones; a domain with no case at all names none; the unit is in the header, every row of one table being in the same. ⚠️ **`show-line-load-summary`** does the same in kN/m, and **the export carries none**: a load reaches `data/` per square metre (§3.3) and a static model's line loads are a drawing (§3.3octies). The engineer: «pour l'instant rien. Mais le user doit manuellement pouvoir ajouter des lignes eq ou var dans typst» — hence **`summary: true`** on `#var` and `#eq`, and **the unit decides which table**, any other refused naming the two. Chosen over gathering by the unit alone, which would let every intermediate of a calculation in. ⚠️ **The mark is not a state of its own**: `summary-mark` is a pure function of the registry's dictionary, called inside the `var-registry.update` both commands already emit, so v0.85's rule — a state written from inside a `context` is not read back by the folded chain an update reads — is satisfied **by construction**, and the variable's var-domain, readable only there, is read. What is recorded is the **name**: an `#eq` computes its answer in that very update, and the value is read where the table is printed — which is also the unit the variable *ends up* holding, and the reason the refusal is raised there and not at the call. ⚠️ **`show-result-summary`** prints two tables: the *Hauptergebnisse* — every load domain, every connector's `R_d`/`R_d,Verb`, every polar moment's `I_p` and, beside it, the **F_max** `show-polar-moment` published under its name, computed in Typst and in no file (§3.3quinquies) — then the **Nachweisübersicht**, one row per member check with its η and the ✔ or ✘ the check itself printed, read from the var-domain `"<member>-verify-<check>"` (§3.3undecies): a walk and not a registration, so **`lib/timber.typ` did not change by one line** and a ninth check will appear there by itself. What a calculation calls a result is the data's to say (§3.3): a result whose value is a **word** is left out, which leaves SIA 261's category E out of the tables. ⚠️ **None of the three takes a `level:`**: a summary is complete or it is not one, and an argument that changes nothing is a lie in the signature (§13). ⚠️ The tables read the **whole document** (`var-registry.final()`), so they stand at the head of the report and a check printed fifty pages further down is in them. 16 more words in `calc/i18n/strings.json`, among them the eight headings of SIA 265 4.2 read off the German and French editions; the three commands translated in `calc/i18n/commands.json`. **No existing page moved**: the 276 frozen example pages, rendered with the engine of the day after, are identical by SHA-256. `tests/test_summary.py`, 18 checks, seven fired against a planted defect.

  **The example project and the frozen pages.** The example project's Woodcore-drawn export was brought up to date through each domain's own `export()` — never a full `woodcore-cli export`, the project carrying no load workbook and v0.89.1 exporting such a project without loads — so the style factors, the long dash, the `woodcore:` source prefix and the labels' new polygon ground reached `data/`, and the whole diff was read before it was committed. The frozen example pages were then re-rendered: **293 pages, of which 8 moved**, every one of them a detail schema whose labels gained their box. Seventeen pages are new, for the seven new commands.

  **0.99.0.** `project.VERSION` is 0.99.0, which is what starts the installer's own build (§12).

- **v0.98** (2026-09-27) — an RFEM 6 model is read into Woodcore: its own table export, the comments that say what a member realises, the structures it falls into, and a static model per structure (§2, §3.3duodecies, §10quater, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **Why.** The engineer models in RFEM 6. What he draws there — the members, where they are held, what acts on them — is the static system Woodcore's «Modèles statiques» tab draws by hand (§3.3octies), so drawing it a second time is the duplication §2 rule 1 forbids everywhere else. He asked that what is already in RFEM reach the report.

  **What is read, and why not the API.** RFEM's own table export: *Fichier → Exporter → Tableaux…* writes one `.xlsx`, or a folder of one `.csv` per table, and that file is what `calc/rfem_import/` reads. The gRPC API was tried first and reads the same model in about a second — and **it requires a Dlubal API key**, which not every office has and which a report's reproducibility must not depend on. So the file is the route and the API can only ever be an accelerator that writes that same file (§13, §14). Nothing in `calc/` imports `dlubal`. ⚠️ **The 120 s lock**, recorded for whoever writes that accelerator: a script holding the API past about two minutes locks the server, and recovering it costs an RFEM restart *and* the computed results — `app.export_to(ResultTableCsvExportAttributes)` took 167 s and did exactly that, where `app.get_results(...)` takes 18 s for everything.

  **What the engineer writes in RFEM.** One `@key value` per line in RFEM's own comment field: `@position T001` (the row of `Positionen.xlsx` a member or a surface realises), `@model` (the structure a component belongs to, and what merges two of them), `@label`, `@descr`, `@load snow-load/SL-Dach1`, `@be 3.6`, `@note`. ⚠️ **The sigil is `@` because `#` is impossible**: RFEM reads a `#word` in a comment as one of its own tags and the word is **gone** from the exported comment — a convention built on it would lose his work silently. A line written with another sigil, and a `#` found in a comment, are matched and **reported** rather than read as prose. One sigil serves every key rather than one per kind: with four sigils a line written with the wrong one is not a line at all.

  **What was learnt from RFEM's own tables, each having silently lost one first.** A **hyphen in a header is never a word boundary** — RFEM hyphenates inside a cell and across the two header rows (`Last-` / `kombin.`) *and* joins with it (`Cross-Section`) — which had lost the German combinations. RFEM **abbreviates the identifier header per language** («Quersch. Nr.», «Load Combs.», «Comb. charges»), so the first column is the identifier where no alias matches — without which the German sections and the German and English combinations came back empty. A number cell may be **a list or a range** (`2-4`, `1,2,5,8-10`). **A support and a hinge read `inf` oppositely** — held on a support, not released on a hinge — so both came out «pinned» until `Restraint.role` said which table it was. **A table of no rows is a table** (his model has no surfaces), refused as malformed until `_headers` stopped claiming two header rows in a one-row file. And **the load cases are not numbered rows**: a case's id is `CC1`, so `Table.data_rows()`, which drops a row whose first cell is not a number, gave back a model with **no loading at all, silently**.

  **The aliases are measured, never guessed.** The engineer exported the same model in French, German and English. Six aliases written from memory were wrong (`Stabgelenke` for `Stabendgelenke`, `Lasttyp` for `Lastart`, and four more). The three languages now read to the same model — the same nodes, members, sections, supports, hinges, metadata and 22 structures — and only what RFEM itself translates differs, which is carried opaquely.

  **CSV or XLSX, measured** (his question): opening the 490-sheet workbook is 1.48 s and reading the 19 sheets Woodcore needs 0.07 s, those 19 being **36 kB of 13.3 MB**; end to end 0.92 s against 0.24 s for the same tables as CSV. XLSX is kept — one file to move, to name and to hash — and the lever is which tables are read, not which format they are in. The workbook is read **lazily** for that reason: eagerly it took 43 s to use 4 % of itself.

  **The structures.** A model is not one static system. The members and surfaces are grouped into connected components by the nodes they share (union-find), every support, hinge and load on one of their elements is attached, and components carrying the same `@model` are merged. ⚠️ **One object may be in several structures**: a nodal support in RFEM holds a *list* of nodes, and his support 2 holds 23 across several components — so an item is placed in every component it reaches, and what is accounted for is counted by identity. **His own model came out as 22 structures**, most of one to six members, one named `VU-gunstig` (members 24–29).

  **What it means, and nothing refuses.** `validation.py` resolves what the metadata names: a `@position` only on a member or a surface, a position `Positionen.xlsx` does not have, the same position on two structures, a section that disagrees with the workbook's, a `@load` naming no instance, a width past 100 m, a structure with no name. Every finding is an `engine.Note` the window writes in its own language (§4bis) — the export is the engineer's file, read and not validated by him, so a comment he has not finished typing must not stop the report. ⚠️ **It found a real one on his own model**: member 24 realises T001, and RFEM carries it as 500/200 mm where `Positionen.xlsx` states 100/200. Both numbers printed, neither file touched.

  **The intermediate JSON.** `data/rfem/<export>.json`, schema `rfem-import` **1.0**: the source's path, size and SHA-256, the model, the metadata, the structures, the problems, and `results` — **absent** when none were read, rather than an empty object reading as «none found». JSON has no infinity, so an infinite spring constant is written as the string `"inf"`. It is the one file a checker opens to see what was read out of RFEM and what Woodcore took it to mean.

  **The static models.** A **named** structure becomes a `statics-source` record: its points, members, supports, releases and loads. **The frame needs no conversion** — Woodcore draws x right and z down, which is RFEM's own global frame here — so what is translated is the vocabulary (a support's kind, a hinge's degree of freedom, a rotational spring becoming a moment release with its stiffness in `kNm/rad`, a load's direction and distribution). ⚠️ **A load's number is per load case**, so an id made from the number alone collided the moment two cases were read: a load's id carries its case, `qCC1_4_26`. ⚠️ **A release's `stiffness` is `{value, unit}`** and not a bare number — which the *fixture* caught and the real export could not, his member 24 carrying a plain moment release and the model's one spring sitting on a member of an unnamed structure.

  ⚠️ **The record goes to `data/statics.json` and never to `Woodcore-Datei/statics/`**, which is the engineer's own decision and what the controller is arranged around: `Woodcore-Datei/` is what he draws, and a file written there by an importer would be a drawing he did not draw, open in a tab that would write it back and lose whatever RFEM said next. So `statics.prepare(source_dir, values, generated=…)` takes the imported records beside the drawn ones, checks each with the same `check_source`, and **refuses a duplicate id naming both** — keying `data/statics.json` by id had let the second quietly overwrite the first. They are regenerated at every export, so RFEM stays the source. A structure with no name has no id to be addressed by and is listed rather than imported.

  **The forces, behind a flag.** Reading a workbook's member-forces sheets is **105 s** against 1.1 s for the model, so an export reads the model every time and the forces when asked (`--results`). ⚠️ **The CSV writes `V_y` where the XLSX writes `Vy`**, so both are aliases of one force. 👉 **Four independent routes agree to the digit** — the GUI's CSV export, the API's own `get_results`, the workbook's result sheets and the stations counted by hand: CO1's largest moment **15.97 kNm on member 64 at x = 2.115 m**, member 1's normal force **−17.0937 kN**, CO1 385 stations.

  **In the window.** An **RFEM tab** (group *Structure*, before *Modèles statiques*): the exports the project names, the structures inside each with their member ranges, and cards for the identity, the contents and the problems; *Ajouter un modèle…*, *Un dossier de .csv…*, *Relire*, *Retirer*. ⚠️ `on_shown()` deliberately reads nothing — an export is tens of megabytes, and a tab switch must not cost a second. An export that cannot be read is listed with its error rather than dropped. ⚠️ A `QFormLayout` is emptied with `removeRow` backwards, not `takeAt`, which takes the item and keeps the row: the cards grew eleven blank lines per refresh until that was found. ⚠️ **«Importer Excel» is «Import données»** — the one action reads the load workbooks, `Positionen.xlsx`, everything Woodcore drew *and* every RFEM export the project names — and renaming it is not only three strings: `tests/test_pdf_button.py` holds every word the button has ever carried as forbidden in the messages that tell the engineer to click it, which is what caught the German `»Aus Excel importieren«` and the English `“Import from Excel”` where the first pass searched only for the French `«…»`.

  **On the command line.** `woodcore-cli import --source rfem [path]` adds an export to the project and reads every one of them, with `--list` and `--results`.

  **End to end on his own project.** `data/rfem/01_29509-Model.json` (160 kB, 62 members, 22 structures), `data/statics.json` carrying his two drawn models and `VU-gunstig`, its figure drawn in the three languages, and a Typst probe printing it — `T001`, `CC1 = 2.5 kN/m`, `CC2 = 1.1 kN/m`, `CC4 = 0.4 kN/m`.

  **Beside it.** The project's folder lost the accent of its name at the engineer's request, which also closed the long-standing red `test_project` check and changes the PDF's title page.

  **Tests.** 157 checks over eight new files — `test_metadata_parser.py` (34), `test_rfem_csv.py` (21), `test_rfem_results.py` (18), `test_structure_detection.py` (20), `test_model_tables.py` (20), `test_rfem_tab.py` (15), `test_rfem_import.py` (29), and the fixtures `rfem_csv.py` and `rfem_model.py`, which build an export in all three languages. ⚠️ Six of the tab's checks failed at once until each opened its project inside `project.using(copy)`: `conftest.project_copy` copies the *current* project, so each was carrying the previous check's exports.

  `project.VERSION` stays 0.96.0.

- **v0.97** (2026-09-27) — the head of a table is measured to its ink: the title and the formulas above a table stand as far from each other, and from the table, as the engine says they do (§3.3, §10bis, §13).

  **Why.** The engineer, reading a polar moment at level 3: «il n'y a pas assez d'espace dans le moment polaire entre F_i^* et le tableau» — the superscript on the closing bracket of `F_i^* = sqrt((F_(x,i)^*)^2 + (F_(z,i)^*)^2)` all but touching the thick rule of the table under it.

  **What was wrong, measured.** One fact about Typst, patched on one side since v0.32. A built-up formula — a fraction, a root — reaches well outside the line box its text edges give it, and that box is what its grid row and every gap around it are measured against. Measured at 11 pt on the superposition's own formulas: the row is 17.44 pt and the ink 27.45 pt, so it stands **5.01 pt above and 5.01 pt below** its row. The 1 em `row-spacing` between two formulas therefore left **1 pt** of white (2 px at 144 ppi, against the 22 px it promises), the 1 em between the second and the third left 1.5 pt, and the `v(0.4em)` under the last left **3 pt** before the table's own rule. v0.32 had met the same fact at the top and answered it with a push -- `v(extra + 0.25em)` on the *first* formula, `extra` being how much taller than `$x_i$` its **line box** was: the one side a push can reach, and the wrong quantity, over-correcting there (12.68 pt for 5.01 pt of ink) while saying nothing about the other three gaps.

  **What.** `render-table`'s head — its title and its `annotated-rows` of formulas — is set inside one block with `text(top-edge: "bounds", bottom-edge: "bounds")`, the instrument `row-cell` has used since v0.87 for a wrapped formula carrying display fractions (§10quinquies). Every row is then as tall as its ink, and every gap in the head is the white it says it is: `v(0.45em)` under the title, `row-spacing` (1 em) between two formulas, `v(0.5em)` above the table. The push is gone. ⚠️ The **title is inside the block** too: its ink hangs about 3 pt below its own line box, which is what had made the push necessary in the first place — with the title outside, 0.45 em under it came out as 2 pt of white on the page.

  **What moves.** The two kinds of table that carry a `formula`, which is all there are: the polar moment's «ungünstigste Überlagerung», whose head grows by about 20 pt (title → formula 8 → 5 pt, between the formulas 1 and 1.5 → 10.5 and 11.5 pt, formula → rule 3 → 5 pt), and the wind load's global and local forces, about 10 pt each — their product formula sat **1 pt** above its own rule and 3 pt under its title, and was never reported. A table without a formula is untouched to the pixel, `data/` does not move, and no number changes. The 276 frozen example pages were re-rendered.

  **Held.** `tests/test_display.py`, `test_report.py` (the report in three languages at three uniform levels), `test_connectors.py`, `test_commands.py` (every example compiles), `test_formula.py`, `test_distance.py`, `test_timber.py`, `test_command_review.py` — all green. `project.VERSION` stays 0.96.0.

- **v0.96** (2026-09-24) — «Katalog» becomes «Woodcore»: the window's name in every language, its code file and class, the project file's key, the archive's route, the tutorial's address, and every text the report and the site print (§3.3nonies, §10quater, §10sexies, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **Why.** The engineer: «je souhaite renommer le katalog en un mot qui est plus commun au fr, all et de (Katalog et plutôt allemand). Fais-moi des propositions (simplement "woodcore-gui" ?)»; of the words put to him, the program's own name, «texts and code files», the old keys still read. Wave 3 of the plan of v0.94, run once v0.91.1 (2ea14d3) and v0.95 (8554e5f) were committed, three agents on disjoint files.

  **What.**
  - **The window**: `calc/tools/catalog_gui.py` → `calc/tools/woodcore_gui.py`, its class `Catalog` → `MainWindow`; `Woodcore.bat` and `packaging/entry_gui.py` start it. `calc/tools/catalog_gui.py` is now a shim running `woodcore_gui.py` as the program (`runpy`), for the launchers installed with 0.83–0.90, which start the sources by that path; nothing imports it. The launcher starts `woodcore_gui.py`, else `catalog_gui.py` for an older checkout (`GUI_SCRIPTS`). The eleven message boxes titled «Typst-Katalog» and the stall log's header say Woodcore; every tab's comments and docstrings too.
  - **What files already written say**: a `.woodcore`'s empty folders under `gui` (`project.GUI_KEY`, `Project.gui()`), `katalog` read where `gui` is absent and replaced at the first change of folders (`schemas/woodcore-project.schema.json` documents both); the archive's route `gui`, `katalog` recorded as `gui` (`archive_export.ROUTES`, `LEGACY_ROUTES`); the `typst-katalog` settings folder still taken over once.
  - **The report and the export**: the `///` blocks and comments of `lib/calc-engine.typ`, `lib/distance.typ` and `lib/i18n.typ`, and the refusals of the `show-*` commands («… in Woodcore's connectors tab»); `calc/i18n/commands.json`'s translations brought along (18 units), `reference.json`, `strings.json`; the domain modules' reasons and warnings; the schemas' descriptions; the source of a value typed on a connector `woodcore:connectors/<id>.json` (`katalog:` until now; read by nothing).
  - **The site**: its words (`katalog_title/text` → `window_title/text`), the tutorial's fifth step `tutorial/window/` — «Importer et explorer dans la fenêtre de Woodcore», with a row for the Positions tab the table lacked —, the pages of 0.87.0, 0.89.0 and 0.90.0 and 0.90.0's notes; and **an address that moved keeps working**: `website/site.json`'s `moved` writes at `tutorial/katalog/`, in every language, a redirect to the new page (`Site.page_moved`), never over a page the site has, a target with no page a warning.
  - README, `prompt-audit.md` (its stall log path, stale since v0.83, corrected), the project template's Tinymist comment, and the pipeline's top-level modules.
  - **Tests**: `test_catalog.py` → `test_window.py`, `test_katalog_identity.py` → `test_identity.py`, six tests renamed; new — the shim starting the window (`test_home`), the launcher's fallback and the shim imported by nothing (`test_packaging`), a pre-v0.96 project migrated by its first change of folders (`test_new_project`), the route `gui`/`katalog` (`test_archive`), the window never saying «Katalog» (`test_i18n`), a moved address leading to its page and never covering one (`test_site`).

  **Verified.** The full suite with Typst 0.14.2: 970 passed, 4 skipped (three need data the example project does not carry, one a file system that keeps two names differing only in case), 1 failed — `test_toolbar.test_the_tooltip_comes_after_half_a_second_and_goes_with_the_pointer`, on the working tree and not at HEAD: the window shown under the pointer entered a tool, and that tool's timer showed its tip during the test's own wait, before the Enter the test sends. The test now moves the pointer off every tool and stops the timer first; `test_toolbar` passes, 16 of 16, twice. `test_the_export_is_reproducible` failed once, `data/project.json` naming 0.96.0, then passed. A first run, without Typst on the PATH, stopped once inside `test_updates` with an access violation (exit 139), which the run with Typst did not repeat.

  **Not changed.** The history — this file's earlier entries, the audits, CLAUDE.md's status lines — keeps the word of its time; the engineer's own words are quoted as he wrote them; a manufacturer's «Katalog» in two worked examples is not the window's. The frozen data (`examples/data/`, `tests/projet-exemple/`) keep `katalog:` in their connectors' sources until frozen again.

  **Released as 0.96.0** (at the engineer's request, the same day: «la prochaine version doit être la version la plus à jour»): `project.VERSION` is 0.96.0, carrying v0.91 to v0.96 — the frame, the licence, the calls, the cards, the positions and the templates, the rename. Its notes are in `website/releases.json` and its page in `website/content/<lang>/changes/0.96.0.md`, in three languages: what is new, `#show-position` replaced by `#member(id, level: 1, description: true)` with the numbers unchanged, and what to do. Published by `packaging/publish.py` once the installer the commit starts is built.

- **v0.95** (2026-09-24) — forms in cards on the position plan, the connectors, the static models and the schemas (§10quater, §11, §11ter, §13, §14). The plan's properties — Plan, Arrière-plan, Échelle, Taille des symboles, Cadrage, Sélection, Commandes Typst —, the connectors' boxes, figures, results and calls, and the static models' and the schemas' properties are cards (`calc/tools/cards.py`), on the four tabs the other session released, the last two once their tool bars were icon-only (22f38c5); its own release is v0.94. **Modèles statiques**: Modèle, Largeur d'action du modèle, Sélection and Commandes Typst are cards, and what the selection shows of an entry — a support, a release, a spring's stiffness, a dimension, a load's look, intensity and influence width — cards inside the selection's card. **Schémas**: Identité, Sélection, Cadrage and Commandes Typst, the block of calls taking the height the cards above leave it, as it did. **What cards.py gained**: `form()`, a `QFormLayout` whose label goes above its field where the two do not fit side by side (`WrapLongRows`); `Reflow`, cards side by side from their width up and one under the other below it, its minimum one card's width; and `Column` telling its splitter when its cards' width changes (a card grown or shrunk: a member added, a selection shown). **Plan de position**: the scale's choice no longer as wide as its longest item. **Moyens d'assemblage**: a titled «Pièce» a card inside its card, the untitled member of a Stahl-Holz joint a flat box; «Pièce 1» and «Pièce 2» of *Géométrie et matériau* and *Broches* in a `Reflow` — the form measured 888 px for a pane of 676 in a window of 1440 px, and the second piece was cut at the column's edge. **Tests**: `tests/test_cards.py` holds the four tabs (five checks); `tests/test_panes.py` measures the room between the plan's panes in the window's own fonts, in a process of its own, the off-screen default font setting words about twice as wide as Segoe UI (403 px for the properties pane in the test, 269 in the engineer's window). Verified: test_cards 10, test_connectors 66 (4 skipped, as before), test_panes 6, test_statics 13, test_schema_tab 36, test_toolbar 16, test_fit 7, test_schema_edit 33, test_schema_section 25, test_katalog_identity 13, test_editor_buttons 5, test_richtext 9, test_i18n 19, test_polar_tab 13, test_theme 10, test_chrome 8, test_catalog 8, test_groups 15, test_wheel 5. `project.VERSION` stays 0.90.0.

- **v0.94** (2026-09-24) — a position's section read once and `#member` for every position, Positionen.xlsx written from the Katalog, *Fichier → Paramètres…* and project templates, Typst checked at every start, the commands tab and the website on one set of data, and the static models' and the schemas' tool bars as the plan's (§3.1, §3.3decies, §3.3undecies, §7, §10bis, §10quater, §10sexies, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14, §15).

  **Why.** One message of the engineer's, eleven requests — Positionen.xlsx written by the Katalog, a name common to French, German and English for the Katalog, `show-position` replaced by `#member`, the commands' descriptions and examples shared between the Katalog and the website, the website's sidebars, a column for the section type after SZS C5, η_w on the stiffness, a rule for every commit, settings with project templates — then a second one: Typst checked at every start, the static models' and the schemas' bars after the plan's, «Schémas» under «Structure». The questions went first, and his answers are the design (§13): «Woodcore» for the name (the renaming is the next step), the section types `rec`, `cir`, `pla`, `clt` and C5's 24 families by their codes, round timber computed, η_w left as it is, templates in three places, winget and a pinned version for Typst, thin separators with no titles. Carried out by nine agents in two waves, beside two other sessions (v0.91.1, v0.92, v0.93, v0.95), each committing only its own hunks.

- **v0.90** (2026-09-23) — the Katalog dressed as the website: one file of colours for both, a light and a dark scheme chosen in *Fichier → Apparence*, and the site's typography (§10quater, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **Why.** The engineer: the website «a une interface très lisible et moderne» — could the Katalog have a better one too? A throwaway prototype (the site's colours on the real window, the example project, nothing in the repository) and proposals in three stages were put to him — **A** a theme, **B** the window's structure, **C** each tab — with four questions; he chose all three stages, a grouped side navigation, the scheme following Windows with a choice in the menu, and a compact density, and asked for «la typographie exacte du site internet (s'il n'y a aucun problème avec l'affichage d'indice, d'exposant et de langage mathématique)». This is stage A.

  **What.**
  - **`calc/tools/theme.json`** holds every colour of Woodcore, light and dark, the radius and the two font stacks: the site's CSS custom properties, and beside them the Katalog's roles (`primary`, `primary-hover`, `on-primary`, `override`, `warning`, `danger`, `lit`, `lit-ink`, `lit-line`, `desk`). `website/assets/site.css` carries a `/* @tokens */` line where its two `:root` blocks stood, and `website/build.py` (`site_css`) writes them in; the values are the site's to the digit, the mono stack gaining «Cascadia Mono» (Cascadia Code without its ligatures, which the site turns off anyway). `ASSET_STAMP` hashes theme.json too.
  - **`calc/tools/theme.py`**: the palette, one compact style sheet and the fonts, on Fusion where the window wore Windows 11's own style; `apply()` in `main()` before any window, `ensure()` at the top of `Catalog` and `HomeWindow`; the arrows and the check and radio boxes as small SVGs written into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Woodcore\theme\`, each named after its content. It imports no Qt at its top: the website's CI image has none.
  - ***Fichier → Apparence***: *Comme Windows* · *Clair* · *Sombre*, one exclusive group, per user (`appearance` in settings.json). *Comme Windows* follows Windows live; a chosen scheme is told to Qt (`QStyleHints.setColorScheme`), which turns the title bar and the native dialogs. Every top-level window with a `retheme()` is built again — the Katalog's own colours, then `rebuild()`; the start page.
  - **Fifteen colours written into six modules are roles.** Two oranges for one meaning (`#b35c00`, `#b06000`: a note the engineer should act on) → `warning`; `connectors_tab.OVERRIDE_COLOR` and `schema_tab.OVERRIDE_COLOR` → `override`, the report's `#b35c00` in the light scheme and `#f0a050` in the dark one; the three Bootstrap-coloured banners (read-only, stale export, newer packages) and the connectors' yellow warning box → the site's callouts, a `callout` property (`danger`, `warning`, `info`); `BAD_CELL` and the canvases' unreadable distance → `danger-soft`; a connector's lit result → `lit` / `lit-ink`, and the lit box's frame from the tab's own `BOX_STYLE` into the window's sheet (`QGroupBox[hot="true"]`); the plan's desk → `desk`. *Exporter PDF* and *Ouvrir le projet* are the window's primary buttons, in the site's teal (the stronger one in the light scheme, white on the lighter being 3.2:1).
  - **The typography.** The site names Inter and JetBrains Mono first and loads neither — measured in Edge on the published site: no `@font-face`, and «Inter» the width of the fallback — so on Windows it shows Segoe UI and Cascadia Code. Put to him, the engineer chose that over bundling the two (about 3 MB): `theme.installed` resolves each stack as a browser does, Segoe UI for the text and Cascadia Mono for the code (Consolas until v0.89), which Windows Terminal shares with every program — Consolas where it is absent. His condition holds: Segoe UI carries every Greek letter, subscript and superscript digit and letter, − √ · × ≤ ≥ ≠ ≈ ∑ ∞ that Segoe UI Variable, the window's font until v0.89, carried, and neither carries ⩽ ⩾ ✔ ✘ ⊕ ⧉, which Windows draws from Segoe UI Symbol or Cambria Math as it did.
  - **Two defects of the dark scheme, fixed on the way**: the schemas tab drew its fastener and dimension tools in the figure's black on dark buttons (`schema_tab.on_ground`, the recolouring statics_tab and connectors_tab already did), and Fusion's check boxes were outlines too faint to find. And the connectors' 30-px «⧉» copy buttons, which the sheet's side padding left no room, are `compact`.

  **Verified.** The suite, whole: 836 passed, 5 skipped, none failed — 763 in one run, which then hung on the new menu test at the question a tab left dirty by an earlier file asks off screen (the test now clears the marks before it closes the window), and the 73 after it run file by file. Then the window's and the site's files again after the last edits (the dark scheme's icons and check boxes, a scheme not applied twice): 12 files, 237 passed. The 5 skipped are the suite's own: four connector cases the example project cannot make, and the reproducibility of the engineer's own export, whose engine 0.88.0 asks to be replaced. `tests/test_theme.py` (10, new) holds one file for two readers, no hex in a `setStyleSheet`, 4.5:1 for every text token in both schemes, the style sheet parsing, the appearance remembered and every window rebuilt, and — asked of Windows' own fonts in a process of its own — Segoe UI's coverage; `test_site.py` builds the site with the tokens written in; test_constants, test_connectors and test_katalog_identity read the tokens where they read the tabs' constants. Every tab captured light and dark on the example project, on Windows' own platform and off screen.

  **Not changed.** The report and its figures; the drawing areas, paper in both schemes as the site's `--paper` is; `project.VERSION`, 0.89.0.

  **Released as 0.90.0** (at the engineer's request, the same day): `project.VERSION` is 0.90.0, carrying stage A, v0.89.1's export without a load workbook and v0.89's link from the update window to the «Nouveautés» pages — not stage B, which is v0.91. Its notes are in `website/releases.json` and its page in `website/content/<lang>/changes/0.90.0.md`, in three languages: what is new, nothing changed in a report, and the one case that still stops — a workbook an export has read and that is gone. Published by `packaging/publish.py` once the installer the commit starts is built.

- **v0.89.1** (2026-09-23) — no load workbook is a warning while no export has read one (§3.3, §11ter, §12, §13).

  **Why.** The engineer, having found that a new project's first export stops on the missing `Belastungsannahmen` workbook: could it only warn, «l'excel n'a pas été trouvé», and go on? Three rules were put to him — always a warning, a setting per project, a warning only where no export has read one — and he chose the last.

  **What.** `registry.open_workbooks(folder, read_before, record)` takes the workbooks the last export read, which `data/project.json` has named since v0.82: where the folder holds no load workbook and that record names one, the export stops naming it and the record, and says that a project which gave up its loads is exported without them once the record is deleted; where it names none, it returns no workbook and `export_all.run` warns with `registry.no_workbook_note` — the three sheet domains without a case, the live loads of SIA 261 Tabelle 8 all there. `registry.is_load_workbook` applies `find_workbooks`' two tests to a name. The Katalog needed nothing: *Importer Excel* and *Exporter PDF* show an export's warnings in their «Export terminé avec des avertissements» box, and a seeded project's calculations were already empty.

  **Verified.** On a new project in a scratch folder: the export writes `data/` and the PDF with the warning; with a workbook read and then removed, it stops naming `Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` and the record; with the record deleted, it goes on again; with the workbook back, it reads it. `tests/test_new_project.py` holds the three, and fails against the old stop and against «always a warning», each planted. `project.VERSION` stays 0.89.0, so the installed program keeps the stop until the next version.

- **v0.89** (2026-09-23) — Woodcore announces its own updates, and has a website: download, requirements, a tutorial and every command with its examples, in three languages (§10quater, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **Why.** The engineer, in one message: that whoever installed Woodcore.exe sees a message when a new version is published, to install it; and a page of the project's GitLab (private, gitlab.ti.bfh.ch/lampf1/woodcore) informing about Woodcore, offering the download, the prerequisites, examples of use, every command's examples as in the Katalog, and a step-by-step tutorial, in French, German and English — laid out after typst.app, the commands after typst.app/docs with the search on the left. Four questions went first; he took the recommended answer each time: the installer on GitLab Pages through the Package Registry, a window at the start, «Installer» downloading and running the installer, and the example pages that set a norm table in full not published.

  **The update check** (`calc/tools/updates.py`, Qt-free; `calc/tools/update_gui.py`). `main()` calls `update_gui.schedule(app)` once the first window is up, never under `--self-test`. In a thread, `updates.fetch()` reads `https://lampf1.pages.ti.bfh.ch/woodcore/version.json` (5 s timeout, never raises) and `updates.validate()` refuses a manifest without its shape, an installer not on HTTPS or not on the manifest's host. `updates.offer()` announces only a version newer than `project.VERSION`, not installed already (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Woodcore\<version>\Woodcore.exe`, the launcher's `INSTALL_ROOT`), not set aside (`updates.skipped`) and not put off (`updates.snoozed`, 24 h — a day, each project opening in its own process). The window: «Woodcore X est disponible — vous utilisez la version Y», the notes, «Installer» / «Plus tard» / «Ignorer cette version», in the window's three languages, waiting for a question already up. «Installer»: a cancellable progress dialog, the download into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Woodcore\updates\`, written as `.part` and renamed only once its size and SHA-256 are the manifest's, then `os.startfile` on it; a refusal says why and names the download page. Asked only by an installed Woodcore (`sys.frozen`); `WOODCORE_UPDATE_CHECK=1|0`, `WOODCORE_UPDATE_URL` and the settings' `updates.enabled` override.

  **Publishing** (`packaging/publish.py`, standard library only). `dist/Woodcore-<version>-setup.exe` uploaded to the Package Registry as generic package `woodcore/<version>` with a personal token (`WOODCORE_GITLAB_TOKEN`, `GITLAB_TOKEN`, else asked), refused where `website/releases.json` has no notes for the version in all three languages, and recorded there — file, size, SHA-256, date. `--dry-run`. Committing and pushing `website/releases.json` publishes.

  **The website** (`website/build.py`, `website/strings.json`, `website/content/<lang>/`, `website/assets/`, `website/site.json`, `website/releases.json`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`). Home page, download page with requirements and history, an eight-step tutorial, a gallery of twelve examples, and the reference: a page per public command (74) in each language, with signature, paragraphs, arguments, the minimal and complete calls, and every example with its 1 · 2 · 3 levels; a sidebar with a search (`/`, Ctrl+K) and a table of contents on the right; light and dark themes; the root choosing the language. Everything the Katalog also shows is read from the same place: `build_reference`, `command_docs.localise`, the fences, the frozen snapshot. The examples are compiled to SVG (browsers read `<symbol>`, QtSvg does not) in parallel — 250 pages in 50 s — and minified from 29 MB to 12 (numbers to 0.01 pt, whitespace between tags and classes removed), the committed PNGs the fallback (`--images png`, level 2 only). Masked, by glob on the stem: `distance-*`, `show-live-load` at level 3, `show-connector-resistance-*`. `version.json` is written only when the newest recorded installer is in the site and matches its SHA-256 — fetched by the CI with its job token (`--fetch-installer`) or given (`--installer`). The CI job `pages`: `python:3.13-slim`, Typst 0.14.2 from its GitHub release, the pinned openpyxl alone. `--serve` previews on localhost:8000; `.claude/launch.json` serves `public/` for the desktop app's browser pane. `public/` and `examples/.site/` are ignored.

  **Beside it.** The reading of a `///` paragraph — code spans, bold, italic — moved from `commands_tab._prose` to `command_docs.prose` (with `PROSE_CODE_SPAN`, `BOLD`, `ITALIC`), the tab keeping `prose_html`/`prose_plain` over it: the CI has no Qt. `project.VERSION` is **0.89.0**: the first installer carrying the check must be installed by hand, and is the one to publish first. Found on the way and fixed before it shipped: the sidebar's active entry was scrolled into view with `scrollIntoView`, which scrolled the page as well.

  **Held** by `tests/test_updates.py` (13) and `tests/test_site.py` (17): see §11ter. A planted defect — the SHA-256 check removed — fails `test_the_download_checks_the_file_and_leaves_nothing_that_is_not_it`.

  **Published, the same day.** The first push ran no pipeline: CI/CD was off in the project's features. Once it was on, the instance runners allowed and a pipeline run, the site went up at `woodcore-c17851.pages.ti.bfh.ch` — GitLab's «unique domain», on by default — and moved to `https://lampf1.pages.ti.bfh.ch/woodcore/` when the engineer turned it off, public, Pages being «Everyone». `packaging/publish.py` uploaded `dist/Woodcore-0.89.0-setup.exe` (69 289 266 bytes, SHA-256 b3ceac19…658a) and a push of `website/releases.json` put it on the site with `version.json`: read by `updates.fetch`, accepted by `updates.validate`, offered to 0.88.0 and not to 0.89.0, and the installer downloaded in 7 s and checked (commit `ddff523`).

  **What changes between versions, for the user** (the engineer: «ajoute au site internet des informations sur les changements entre mises à jour et ce que cela signifie pour le user — quelles commandes utiliser, comment»). A **«Nouveautés» section** in the top bar and the sidebar: a page on how to update — the engine's question at the opening («Voir les différences…», «Mettre à jour», «Plus tard»), re-exporting and reading the PDF, pinning a finished project, the archive — and one page per version a user can have installed, the newest first: **0.87.0** (everything since 0.83.0 — `project.VERSION` stayed 0.83.0 through v0.86, so no one installed 0.84 to 0.86), **0.88.0** and **0.89.0**, each in three parts, *Nouveau*, *Ce qui change dans vos rapports*, *À faire*, with the calls and the examples inline, in three languages (`website/content/<lang>/changes.md`, `changes/<version>.md`). The download page links each version published to its page; the search finds them. `version.json` carries `changes`, the page per language, and the update window links to it under the notes (`updates.changes`: HTTPS on the site's own host only; the notes escaped once the text is rich) — from the version after 0.89.0, which was published without it. The site's Markdown learned one level of nested lists. Tests: `test_site.py` 20, `test_updates.py` 15.

  **Every example in the page's language** (the engineer: «vérifie que tous les exemples fonctionnent et soient disponibles en 3 langues»; commit `584bf49`). The site compiled every example in German, as the Katalog shows them; each is now compiled in French, German and English, `set-report-language` and Typst's `set text(lang:)` set to the page's — 738 pages, 0 failing. A page the three languages print alike is stored once, named after its content (480 distinct), and `minify_svg` gained a lossless compaction (path data, short glyph ids, default attributes left out), checked pixel for pixel in Edge (0 pixels differ on the five heaviest pages, 35 to 40 % fewer bytes): a site of 23.5 MB, 89.6 MB with the installer. The words a French page keeps German are the workbook's own dropdown values (`Nein`, `behindert oder erschwert`, `Flachdach`, §4bis). `tests/test_site.py` 21.

  **Not done, and said** (§14): the installer is unsigned; the size budget is tight; the Belastungsannahmen template is not offered; no manual «check for updates» entry; the tutorial and the «Nouveautés» pages are not held to the window's labels or the commands' arguments.

- **v0.88** (2026-09-23) — the member checks of SIA 265:2021 chapter 4.2, written in the report: `#member` and eight `verify-*` commands (§2, §3.3undecies, §10bis, §10quinquies, §10sexies, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **Why.** The engineer asked for the checks in compression, tension, bending, bending with a normal force, buckling and lateral torsional buckling after SIA 265, «uniquement au-travers de commandes typst (sans passer par le katalog)». A proposal went first, with nineteen questions; his answers are the design: a member written down once and checked by name (option B), the prefix `verify-`, (22) chained with (37) and **«Tragsicherheit» and «Stabilität» set apart** on the page, **every case of 4.2** in the first version, Anhang C and SIA 265/1 left out, rectangles only, b and h read from the position and written over by the call, the strength and moisture class likewise, an action as a number, a value with its unit, a name or a var object, **N signed**, buckling lengths direct or as β · l, **the exact λ_rel only**, **eta_w on E_0,05 too**, σ_m,crit given as an option, (37) on both axes from level 2, the utilisation a ratio with `"%"` as an option, a heading per check with its clause, and the results published in `"<member>-verify-<check>"`.

  **What.** `lib/timber.typ`: `member` and `verify-tension`, `verify-compression`, `verify-compression-perp`, `verify-bending`, `verify-bending-normal`, `verify-shear`, `verify-shear-off`, `verify-shear-perp` — 4.2.1 to 4.2.9, equations (6) to (44). A check is a run of `#eq` and `#verify` in a `var-domain`, so levels, units, stacking and verdicts are formula.typ's; the branch of k_c (λ_rel ⩽ 0.3) and of k_m ((39)'s three) that holds is the one printed, decided at the call from the member's record — read back from the registry, it cost a layout pass per check and eight frozen examples did not converge; a sum leaves out the terms whose action is not given; lateral torsional buckling without a or σ_m,crit is said not checked. formula.typ imports the nine commands by name and lib/timber.typ imports `var`, `eq`, `verify` back **inside each command's body**, which Typst does not count as a cycle. `project.ENGINE_FILES`, the commands tab (a category «Vérifier un élément (SIA 265)»), build_reference's copied calls (a `#member` line before a check, and the actions it cannot do without) and the frozen examples carry them.

  **The norm.** Seven files transcribed off page renders — pypdf lost the square roots of (32), (34), (42) to (44) — Anhang B as **Tabellen 47 and 48** (f_c,0,k and E_0,05 of the 11 classes; E_0,05 = 7400 for C24 where 2/3 · E_0,mean gives 7333), and the constants of 3.4.2.3 (k_h), 4.2.6, 4.2.7, 4.2.8 and 4.2.9, each checked by calc/norms/sia265.py and held by 17 tests of tests/test_norms.py with a planted defect each. Two misprints noted: (39)'s middle bound reads «0,75 · λ_rel,m ≤ 1,4», and 4.2.4.2 «Kippen bzw. Kippen».

  **The oracles.** 46 worked cases gathered in tests/fixtures/timber-oracles.json from the Bemessungsbeispiele zur SIA 265, the Holzbautabellen and the reference templates; 16 reproduced by tests/test_timber.py (pp. 72, 73, 87, 114, 122, 125, 143, 166, 167, 263, 265 and the beam template to full precision) and the HBT's two k_c · f_c,0,d tables row by row. The other 30 follow another method and say which. One thing they changed: **torsion** took Roark's approximation of W_t first, 4.5 % above the book's Saint-Venant value (p. 114) — on the unsafe side; it is Saint-Venant's series now, `timber-torsion-k`.

  **Beside it.** `pi` is a number in a formula (`#eq`, `#verify`), and refused as a key; `norm-text` cites a table «SIA 265 Tabelle 8» without §; a bare number of `#verify` keeps its written unit, `eta <= 100 "%"` printing «100 %». tests/test_distance.py's list of engine norms now allows lib/timber.typ's files. The glossary gains the checks' words in French and English, calc/i18n/commands.json their documentation in French and German. `#verify`'s double rule is measured to the ink where a relation carries a display fraction (`show math.frac: it => $display(it)$`, the engineer's config.typ): it crossed the denominator of `F_Ed / R_d`, and three frozen `verify` pages moved -- the only earlier pages that did. **Every separating dash is a long one**, the engineer's answer when the version was shown him: a member check's heading, «Stabilität — Kippen», a connector figure's caption, «Bauteil 1 — Seitenholz», an empty table cell, a static model's figure notes and every spaced dash of the glossary; ranges («1–4») and a cited document's title keep their short dash. 258 frozen examples. Suite: 785 passed, 5 skipped -- among them `test_the_export_is_reproducible`, the engineer's project still carrying the 0.87.0 engine, which its first export or opening replaces once asked. `project.VERSION` **0.88.0**.

- **v0.87** (2026-09-22) — seventeen requests from the engineer's first exercise written with Woodcore: `#var` inline with a `note:` argument, a `#distance` command, words and stars in formulas, a minus sign, the PDF beside its `.typ`, a position without a dimension, load colours and what a dimension shows (§2, §3.3, §3.3octies, §3.3decies, §4bis, §7, §10bis, §10quater, §10quinquies, §10sexies, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **Why these, and why now.** The engineer wrote a reinforced-concrete exercise with the program installed in v0.83 and sent back what he met, one line each. Three of them were not defects of these sources at all: `#eq(label:, descr:)` exists since v0.85.2, and the exercise's engine copy was the installed v0.83's. It went unseen because **`project.VERSION` had stayed at 0.83.0 since v0.83**, so the sources took a v0.83 copy for one of their own edited by hand (`stale`) rather than for an older one. It is **0.87.0** now, and both of the engineer's projects had their engine replaced (`woodcore-cli sync-engine --update`) and their Tinymist setting brought to the new PDF place.
