  **`#var` is inline, and where its extras go is an argument.** «de base, #var doit s'afficher inline (afin de pouvoir afficher plusieurs var les uns après les autres). S'il y a deux retours à la ligne, alors ça fait un nouveau paragraphe.» A `#var` outside a `var-line` prints `symbol = value unit` as text: calls on consecutive lines flow into one paragraph, a blank line starts the next. Asked where the label, the description and the citation then go, the engineer chose neither of the two placements put to him but an argument, on `#var`, `#var-unit`, `#eq` and `#val` alike: **`note: "footnote"`** gathers the three into one footnote and **`note: "line"`** sets them on the line — for `#var` and `#eq` exactly as they were printed until now (the label after an em dash, the description a footnote, the citation in the right-hand column), for `#val` as `value — label (description; citation)`. Defaults: `"footnote"` for `#var`, `#var-unit` and `#val`, `"line"` for `#eq`, which stays a block. No effect inside a `var-line`.

  **Words and stars in a formula.** «pourquoi dois-je écrire `G_(1,\"Ed\")` et pourquoi `G_(1,Ed)` ne fonctionne pas ?» A run of letters Typst's math does not know (`dictionary(math)`) is quoted before a symbol is evaluated (`quoted-words`), on `#var`, `#eq` and `#val`: `G_(1,Ed)` and `W_(y,erf)` work as written. ⚠️ A name math knows keeps its meaning — `c_(pi)` is c with π — and a letter followed by digits is a letter and a number (`gamma_(M1)` → M and 1). And `*` is multiplication in the formula strings of `#eq` and `#verify`, printed `·`: the `show math.star` rule the engineer tried cannot rewrite a formula string. `#verify` at level 3 prints an expression side as its letters, its numbers and its value — `F_Ed/R_d = 12 kN/24 kN = 0.5 ⩽ 1` — the right-hand side mirrored so the two values meet at the sign.

  **A minus sign.** Every number printed as text takes U+2212: `signed-text` in calc-engine.typ, used by `fmt-number`, the answer of a numbers line, the connector's line, the legend note of a connection figure and every value formula.typ prints; `prose-minus` turns a hyphen standing for a sign into a minus in a sentence of the data («Δh = -200 m» out of the Dropdowns sheet). `fmt-decimals` keeps the hyphen, being read again as math source and as SVG. The static models' and the schemas' figures do the same (`figure_number`, Arial's minus advance in the width tables).

  **`#distance`** (lib/distance.typ, the engine's fourth file, re-exported through formula.typ by name). «Cette commande doit afficher un tableau contenant les distances minimales de l'assemblage dans la pièce (calculées avec le diamètre (d) donné en mm). Voici les tableaux concernés dans la SIA265: Tableau 24, 27, 28, 32, 33, 36, 37, 38, 39, 40, 41.» Transcribed off page renders of SIA 265:2021 DE (French words from the FR edition), one file each in `calc/norms/`: Tabelle 24 p. 66 extended (dowel.py reads it unchanged), 27 and 28 p. 68, 32 and 33 p. 71, 36 p. 74, 37, 38 and 39 p. 75, 40 p. 76, 41 p. 80, and the diameter ranges of nails (6.4.1.1) and screws (6.5.2.3.1). `#distance(d, connector, tab: auto, title: auto, label: none, descr: none, norm: auto, norm-ref: auto, alpha: none)`: every multiple of d computed in mm, the tables that hold for that d unless `tab:` names one, `connector` taking English keys and the German names of `connector.TYPES`, `alpha:` for Tabelle 41's one angle-dependent spacing. ⚠️ **`calc/norms/` is now read by Typst too**, the same files Python reads: `ENGINE_FILES` copies `lib/distance.typ` and the tables into every project's `_woodcore/`, and `engine_state` checks only the files a copy's VERSION names, so an older copy without them is older and not edited. ⚠️ Tabelle 38's first column is headed «d = 4 mm» in both editions; read as d ≤ 4 mm, the printed text kept in the file.

  **The report's page.** A point highlighted by `show-schema` is ringed over the pieces, at a second marker `schema_svg` writes, `<!-- highlight-points -->` («Un point doit être affiché au-dessus de tout»); an older figure takes every halo at the first. `find` sets a text value as words that wrap, and `render-table` sets every cell ragged. With `show math.frac: it => $display(it)$` in his config, two lines of one wrapped calculation crowded each other: Typst does not count a display fraction's height in a line, so raising the leading did nothing, and `annotated-rows` measures a wrapped row carrying a fraction to its ink and sets 0.5 em between its lines (`row-cell`); every other row is as it was.

  **Positions and the PDF.** A position's `dimension` and `units` are facultative, like `material` («les dimensions d'une position ne sont pas obligatoires»): the export, the validation, `show-position`, the Katalog's position summaries (`connector.position_dimension`, never a unit alone). And **`Statischer Rapport.pdf` is written beside its `.typ`**, in `Statischer Bericht (typst)/` — the engineer's choice of the two places put to him: `DEFAULT_LAYOUT["pdf"]` moved, the template sets no `tinymist.outputPath` (its default writes there), the archive keeps the PDF out of the zip and its flat copy beside it, and a project whose `.woodcore` states `layout.pdf` keeps its own. The PDF at the top of Musterprojekt stays where it is, from its last export.

  **The Katalog.** In «Modèles statiques» a load takes a `color` — red (the default, and the look until now), green, orange, blue, violet — its arrows, band and cap drawn in it and its label left black; in both «Modèles statiques» and «Schémas» a dimension takes a `show` mode — value, name, both, nothing — as a load does, an older dimension with a label reading as «name». Both optional fields, no schema bump. The lists under the drawing take column widths dragged by hand, kept through a refill and in `view_state`; the tool bar is compact (fixed button heights, tight group boxes, the loads' and dimensions' tools icon-only), at 1400 × 780: static models 264 px in 3 rows → 132 px in 2 (the drawing 223 → 355 px), schemas 312 px in 3 rows with a sideways scroll → 200 px in 3 without (188 → 300 px).

  **Examples and translations.** Examples with `level: 0` for `var`, `var-unit`, `eq`, `val`, `verify` and `var-line` — the engineer chose examples over a 0 in the tab's selector, a report level being 1 to 3 — and with `domain: "…"` and `domain: (…, …)` for `var` and `val`. The example project's Katalog-drawn export was brought up to date with each domain's own `export()` and frozen again: 221 examples rendered, where there were 199. `calc/i18n/commands.json` carries the French and German of every paragraph and argument new or changed.

  **The second half, the same day** («Fais la suite», and two questions). **Every key is accepted**: `key-symbol` sends one that does not start with a letter or a Greek name through `quoted-words`, so `#eq("M2", …)` prints M2 as written — reversing v0.79's refusal (AUD-7-425), `M_2` still being the subscript — and only a `-`, a leading `_` and an operation's name (`dot`, `max`, `sqrt`) are refused; `atom-symbol` keeps `M2 / a` a fraction of M2. **`#var-unit` takes `label:`, `descr:`, `norm:` and `norm-ref:`**, stored with the variable. **A formula too wide for its row is stacked at its `=`** — the letters on the first line, the numbers and the answer after, the signs aligned — in `#eq`, `show-calc`'s steps and `#verify` (`chain-rows` in `annotated-rows`); a row that fits is set exactly as before, and the exercise's B_v stands in three lines with its citation level with the first. `#var`, `#var-unit` and `#val` no longer break between a symbol and its value. **A typed `*` is drawn `·`** in a static model's and a schema's labels, the source keeping the star. **The engine is checked when a project opens** (`check_engine`): an absent or stale copy is replaced silently, an older, unknown or edited one asked about — «Ce projet utilise le moteur de Woodcore 0.83.0. Le mettre à jour vers 0.87.0 ?», with «Voir les différences…» and «Plus tard» — a newer one named, a read-only project left alone; the question is `engine_consent`'s own, shared with the export. The export refuses an `unknown` copy up front too, where it wrote `data/` and then stopped at the engine. **The installer is rebuilt at each new version**: a `post-commit` hook (`packaging/hooks/`, activated by `python packaging/auto_build.py install`, done on this clone) starts, when a commit changes `VERSION` and `dist/` has no installer for it, a detached build of **that commit** in a clean `git worktree` — never the working tree several sessions edit — queued behind a running one, logged in `dist/build-<version>.log`; `WOODCORE_NO_AUTOBUILD=1` skips it and `auto_build.py start` runs it by hand. **A `*` typed in hand-written `$…$` math prints `·` too**, the engineer having asked again: two show rules in `config.typ` (the template's, the examples', the example project's and both of his projects') — `show math.equation: it => { show "∗": sym.dot.op; it }` and `show math.attach: it => { show "⋅": sym.ast; it }` — the second keeping a star in a sub- or superscript a star, so the engine's own `F^*` of the most unfavourable superposition and an `x^*` are untouched (a star inside an index, `x_(i*j)`, stays a star too). **A project a newer Woodcore wrote opens read-only** (the engineer's yes): its lock let go, its banner naming the version, where until then only its export was refused and a drawing saved from a tab re-exported its part of `data/` with older code; and the launcher's refusal names the version to install («Installez Woodcore 0.87.0 ou une version plus récente pour ouvrir ce projet»), where it said «Installez-le». The 0.87.0 installer was built again with both.

  **Checked**: the full suite after the first half, 713 passed, 4 skipped, 1 failed — `test_export.test_the_export_is_reproducible` on `project.json`, the first export of Musterprojekt after the version moved to 0.87.0 — and after the second, **738 passed, 4 skipped, 1 failed**, the same test on the reference workbook, which a changed `///` block makes stale once; both times it passes run again on its own. New: `tests/test_distance.py` (12), `tests/test_display.py`, `tests/test_engine_check.py` (10). **The 0.87.0 installer is built**: `python packaging/build.py` from the working tree, every check of the built programs passed, `dist/Woodcore-0.87.0-setup.exe` (63 MB, the program 194 MB); the 222 example pages rendered again. `command_docs.CODE_SPAN` and the commands tab read a double-backtick span, which eq's `formula` argument carries and which made a translation impossible to check. Probes compiled and read: a point ringed over the pieces, a long word wrapping in `find`, `G_(1,Ed)` and `*` in an `#eq`, `−2.5 kNm`; both of the engineer's reports compile against the new engine, his exercise read page by page.

- **v0.86.2** (2026-09-21) — a unit typed with a caret is printed with a real exponent (§10quinquies).

  The engineer wrote `#var("f_y", 235, "N/mm^2", level: 3)` and the page read `f_y = 235 N/mm^2`. `parse-unit` has read `^2` as an exponent since v0.60, so the value was right and every formula naming it computed right; only the page showed the caret, a unit being printed as a quoted word inside math. `written-unit` in `lib/formula.typ` turns `^` and its digits into superscript digits where a unit written by hand comes in — `var` (a number, or a var object given a unit beside it, which `kN/m^2` beside a `kN/m²` no longer mistakes for a conversion), `var-unit`, `eq`'s `unit:`, `unit`, `val`'s `unit:` — so the registry holds one spelling and `find` and `val(type: "unit")` show it too. `^-1` is left as written, `parse-unit` reading no sign after a caret. `tests/test_formula.py` holds all five doors and the printed page, and fails with the conversion taken out; the 199 example pages were regenerated, none moved; the engine copy of Musterprojekt was synced.

- **v0.86.1** (2026-09-21) — a new project's report compiles before its first export (§11ter, §12).

  **The defect.** The engineer's first project made by *Nouveau projet*, a reinforced-concrete exercise, stopped Tinymist on «file not found … `_woodcore/data/project.json`», called from `project-info` on the title page. `project_template.create` copied the engine into the project and wrote nothing of `data/`, where the report reads six files at every compile — `project.json` for the title page, and `positions-index.json`, `calculations.json`, `connectors.json`, `plans.json` and `statics.json` for the loops of its sections — and the one route that writes them all, a full export, refuses a project with no load workbook (§3.3), which a new project has not got. The Katalog's own saves each write one file: this project had `plans.json` from a plan saved, and nothing else. §12 said so in one sentence, «the report compiles from the first export on», and nobody had opened a new project's report in Tinymist before exporting it.

  **The fix.** `export_all.seed(here)` writes the export of a project holding nothing yet — every collection empty, `calculations.json` carrying SIA 261 Tabelle 8's live loads alone, which need no workbook, and `project.json` the record an export writes — each file checked against its contract and written **only where it is missing**, so an export or a save in the Katalog is never touched and seeding twice writes nothing. `create()` calls it after the engine, and the window calls it when it opens a project it may write to (not read-only), which is what repairs a project made before; a failure there keeps no project from opening. `registry._discover` says nothing about a domain having no sheet when it was given no workbook at all, where it printed three warnings naming none. The engineer's project was seeded by hand: `project.json` and five others written, the `plans.json` and `statics.json` his saves wrote left as they were.

  **What it does not settle.** The report then stops on `show-position("T001")`: T001 is a row of the project's `Positionen.xlsx`, and positions reach `data/` only through a full export, which still needs a load workbook. That rule is §3.3's and §13's, and is the engineer's to change.

  **Tests.** `test_new_project.py`: the report compiles straight after `create`, its title page from the project file and a second seed writing nothing; seeding a project with some of its export writes what is missing and leaves the Katalog's own file byte for byte; and the compile-after-export test copies the example export over the seeded one (`dirs_exist_ok`). 10 passed there; `test_home`, `test_project`, `test_lock` and `test_export` pass, the last after the one priming run §11ter describes.

- **v0.86** (2026-09-21) — the commands tab brought up to date: its documentation in French and German, its examples frozen again from the anonymised example project, and what a probe of every command found put right (§2, §10bis, §10sexies, §11, §11ter, §13, §14).

  **The request.** «Vérifie dans le Woodcore, l'onglet "Commands". Mets-le à jour, mets les exemples à jour, traduit en français et en allemand. Vérifie le fonctionnement des commandes et analyse les potentiels problèmes.» Two questions were put to the engineer first and answered: where the translation lives (a file of its own, over `/// fr:` lines in the blocks or the summary alone), and whether the example pages exist in French too (no — German only).

  **The translation** (`calc/i18n/commands.json`, `calc/tools/command_docs.py`). Every paragraph and every argument of the 64 public commands, some 7 500 words, into Swiss German and French, translated in five parallel lots against a brief carrying the project's terminology (the glossary's and SIA's words: «moyen d'assemblage» / «Verbindungsmittel», «référence (normative)» / «Normverweis», «Stufe», «Fussnote» with no ß) and merged against the current `///` blocks. A unit carries the English it translates and is matched on it, so a block edited without its translation shows in English with a line saying how many passages, and `tests/test_commands.py` names the unit. The French typography is the window's own (a straight apostrophe, a space inside « » and before a colon). Tinymist's hover and `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` stay English.

  **The tab.** The documentation follows the window's language, and a search finds a command by the words of either. The prose is rich text: code in the fixed font, **bold** and *italic* as such — across a code span too, which the first version missed on `val`'s «**`unit:` is display only**» — and a symbol's `*` (`F*_x,i`) left alone. The count says «64 sur 64 commandes · 173 internes» where it said «236 sur 236 commandes», and «Import, en tête de la section :» lost its second colon (`app.label` added one to a text that carried its own).

  **A defect of the doc parser, found by a translator.** `- norm (str, none), norm-ref (str): …` documents two arguments, and `BULLET_RE` matched no such line: it was read as the continuation of the bullet above, so `norm` and `norm-ref` had no row in the tab or the workbook for `var`, `eq`, `val` and `var-line`, and `level`'s (or `label`'s) meaning ended with their text. `MULTI_BULLET_RE` gives each name its row, the meaning shared. `docs/typst-reference.xlsx`'s Parameters sheet gains the rows, which is why `test_the_export_is_reproducible` failed once.

  **The examples.** Frozen from `tests/projet-exemple/` (`build_examples.EXAMPLE_PROJECT`), never from the open project, whose architect's plans and client name must not enter the shared repository; `--project` still names another. The example project's connector export was first brought to v0.84's layout with the Katalog's own `connector.export()` — nothing else of it had moved — and the 23 fences and prose mentions citing `SD_02`, `SD_01`, `SD_01-8mm`, `SD_02-10mm`, `Beispiel`, `VU-SD-10` and `R01` name `SD-10`, `VU-MP-1`, `VU-Skizze` and `Bankli-Fall1`. **B25's two deliberate failures are gone**: the dangling check compares with the example project, and the planted test no longer expects `fmt-2`, which went with `fmt` in v0.80. Two fences corrected: `uniform-level`'s second example now sets `uniform-level`, and `render-prose`'s passes the description through `tl-str`, as the engine does. 199 pages re-rendered; the snapshot carries no client data (`project.json` names «Exemple», «Musterbüro») and its plan backgrounds are still the 439-byte blank pages.

  **Three probes of every command.** Subagents went through the formula commands, the display commands and the data, format and language ones against the frozen data, with a harness compiling each snippet the way the tab compiles an example. Repaired, each held by `tests/test_command_review.py`:
  - `verify` crashed on `≤`, `≥`, `≠` — the operator's length counted in clusters where `slice` counts bytes — and its unknown-name message offered `value:`, which is eq's;
  - `eq` at level 3 substituted keys one after the other, so `N` rewrote the «N» of the «21 N/mm²» `f_c` had become (placeholders first, as `evaluate-formula` does); a `value:` answer was stored without a unit; `d^2.6` crashed on a superscript of «2.6» and is refused naming what to write; `atan`/`asin`/`acos` evaluated and could not print, `arctan` printed and could not evaluate — both spellings do both;
  - `norm-ref:` alone was ignored by `var` and `val`; `var` dropped a var object's `choices`; `val` refused `%` on a ratio (and says so in orange as «a unit set for display», a new template, where «statt in .» named no unit); `val` with a pair did not fall back to the var-domain a polar moment publishes, as `var` and `find` do;
  - `render-symbol` of a word failed outside `context`; `resolve-level` accepts a report level of 1-3 only, where 0 hid every `#var` and 4 printed 3; a pure shear printed `arctan(2.5/0)` (a cos 90° residue compared with zero); `show-plan(zoom:)` ran past both page edges; F_max's label was an engine key no `tl-str` translates (a term now); a footnote marker after `kN/m²` read as its exponent (`h(0.2em)` in `line-note`, `var-row`, `show-live-load`); a number could part from its unit (a no-break space in `fmt-number`); `show-connector`'s colon ignored French; `set-report-language(1)` and `tl-of("it", …)` failed on Typst's own messages;
  - the documentation of `var-domain`, `val`, `var-unit`, `var-line`, `find`, `verify`, `calc-block`, `show-polar-moment` (N, V, M take a name too), `show-static-model`, `show-plan`, `get-description`, `get-choices`, `var-of` (a stray `->` inside three bullets), `fmt-decimals`, `t`, `t-label`, `t-pattern`, `set-report-language` (it sets the engine's language, not the whole report's) and `render-prose` («a raised red» for a subscript, found by a translator); and the copied calls of `get-description` and `get-choices`, which named a var carrying neither.
  Not repaired, and listed in §14 for the engineer: `tl-of` translating the first word of an engineer's text through a template with a trailing hole, negative numbers printed with a hyphen, a schema highlight hidden under its pieces, `find`'s text values in a math cell, halos on hidden parts, two currency dollars read as math, and a list of smaller things. ⚠️ The regression tests were written after the repairs; each defect had been reproduced by the probes first.

  **Suite**: 657 passed, 4 skipped, **none failed** — the first full run with no deliberate failure since v0.78 (B25).

- **v0.85.2** (2026-09-21) — level 3 says everything a value carries, and `label`, `descr`, `norm`, `norm-ref` are on all three commands (§10bis, §10quinquies, §13).

  **The request.** The engineer read v0.85.1 back and closed the two gaps it left: «le level 3 doit donc afficher le label, la description, la norm et la norm-ref (il faut rajouter la description pour être complet)», and «les fonctions var, val et eq doivent avoir comme arguments label, descr, norm et norm-ref afin de pouvoir donner ces arguments ou les écrasés s'ils existent déjà».

  **Completing level 3 meant deciding where the description goes**, the rest being settled already: the label after an em dash on the line, because what a variable *is* is read with it (v0.61.1); the citation in the right-hand column, which is what keeps it level with the line it cites (§10). So the description is §9's footnote and stays one, with the `choices` it explains. A level-3 line now reads «𝐶ₑ = 1 — Expositionsbeiwert¹» with «SIA 261 §5.2.2» in the column and «¹Gewählte Windexposition: normale Windexposition.» at the foot of the page — the first time all three have been on one line, and the first time `#var("ce", var-of("snow-load", "SL", "ce"))` has shown both of the var's own sentences.

  **The description is level 3's, not level 2's.** A label names a number and is wanted the moment the number is shown; an explanation of a choice is what a checker reads. That moved `val`'s `choices` from level 2 to 3 with the description they belong to, which is the one line of v0.80's own row that had to be rewritten.

  **The four arguments, and overriding is their point.** `var`, `val` and `eq` each take `label:`, `descr:`, `norm:` and `norm-ref:`, given where the value carries none and overriding where it carries its own, field by field: `#var("ce", var-of(…), norm: "SIA 265")` keeps «Expositionsbeiwert» and takes the new citation. A var of the export arrives with all three, and a report quoting it for something narrower than its own label says has to be able to say so at the call.

  **`descr:` is an argument again**, with its own meaning rather than the label's. That is v0.80's rename corrected and not reversed: it renamed `#var`'s `descr:` to `label:` on the ground of «one word for one notion» (§4bis), which was right about the word and wrong about there being one notion — the thing v0.85.1 discovered from the other end, in the data.

  **`line-note` learned to be asked for part of itself**, `with-label` and `with-norm`: a block line has an em dash for its label and a column for its citation, so its footnote carries the description alone, while an inline one carries all three. One builder, two shapes, rather than a second copy of the choices-rendering.

  **Tests.** Two added: level 3 carrying all four on `var`, `eq` and `val` at once and level 2 carrying only the label, and every one of the four given and overriding on the three commands. `test_formula` 69. The 199 frozen examples were re-rendered.

  **A trap paid for twice in one session.** The script that applied this was written through a `<<'PYEOF'` heredoc for one line, and the heredoc turned a `\n` inside a single-quoted Python string into a real newline — a syntax error, caught immediately, but it is trap 1 of the session notes met again an hour after trap 11. The rule holds without exception: anything carrying a backslash, a backtick or a `$` goes through a file written with the Write tool.

- **v0.85.1** (2026-09-21) — `label` and `description` are two fields, and `var`, `val` and `eq` share one scale (§10bis, §10quinquies, §13).

  **The question.** The engineer ran `#find(object: "variable", domain: ("connector", "VU-SD10"))`, saw a column headed *Bezeichnung*, and asked where that value is kept in the data — adding that it ought to be the label. It is, for a var of the export; it is not, for a var the report wrote down, and that is the defect.

  **What was wrong.** `label` and `description` are two fields in the data and have been since §3.1: `label` is what the variable *is* («Expositionsbeiwert», «Durchmesser»), `description` is why *this* value was chosen and carries the `choices` with it, which is what §9's footnote exists for. In the registry they were one field: v0.80 renamed `#var`'s `descr:` to `label:` and left the field called `description`. Two consequences, both silent. A var object read out of the export had its **label dropped** — `#var("ce", var-of("snow-load", "SL", "ce"))` at level 3 printed «𝐶ₑ = 1 — Gewählte Windexposition: normale Windexposition.» and never the word «Expositionsbeiwert» — and `find`'s column had to read `label` with a fallback to `description` to show anything at all, under a comment claiming the two were the same thing.

  **Why it took five versions.** Nothing printed both fields side by side. `find`, a day old, is the first thing to put a var of the export and a var of the report in one column; the engineer read it and asked one question. That is the argument for the command existing at all, more than the listing it was written for.

  **The fix.** `var-record` carries `label` **and** `description`; `#var`'s `label:` writes `label`; `val`'s footnote takes the label from `label` and the choices from `description`; `find`'s `designation(v)` reads `v.label` alone and its fallback is gone. `show-polar-moment`'s seven published vars (F_max, each fastener's resultant, each member's force-grain angle) moved to `label` too — what they say is what the variable is, none says why a value was chosen — which is what keeps `find`'s column filled for the very command the engineer was reading, and stops `#var("F_max", domain: ("connection", id))` panicking on a missing key. `#var`'s own read is defensive besides, a publisher being by definition code that writes records from outside `var`.

  **One scale for the three commands that print a single value**, the engineer's own: **1 the value, 2 the label with it, 3 the citation as well.** `val` already read that way (v0.80), so only `var` and `eq` moved. `#var` printed its label at level 3, one level later than the word naming a number is wanted, and printed its **citation in the right-hand column at every level** — so a client report carried «SIA 265 §6.2.1.1» beside a bare number and nothing saying what the number was. `#eq` had no label at all and takes one now, printed after its answer as `#var` prints its own; its two formula lines did not move, being the working rather than an extra. A `var-line`'s own citation follows its items' and waits for level 3.

  **What moved on a page.** Every level-1 and level-2 line that carried a citation loses it, the engineer's `Sections/005` included. `test_report`'s annex-citation probe moved to level 3 — its subject is the wording of a reference, «SIA 261 Anhang D» without a §, not the level it appears at. The 199 frozen examples were re-rendered. Suite: `test_formula` 67, `test_report` 24, `test_commands` 20 with its two deliberate B25 failures, `test_connection` 12, `test_arrows` 4.

  **A trap recorded rather than fixed.** Explaining this in a source comment meant writing `label` and `description` in backticks; passed through a `python -c "…"` string from the Bash tool, **bash ran them as command substitutions** and Windows' own `label` command spliced «Volume in drive C: has no label» and a serial number into the middle of the comment, breaking it across two lines of which the second was no longer a comment. Caught by reading the file back, not by the compiler. Anything carrying a backtick now goes through a script file.

  **`Verbindung.xlsm`, edited twice while this was written, and frozen again each time** (§11ter, §13). First the force-grain angles C19/D19, 0° becoming 60.4°/29.6°: `test_dowel` went red while the engine was right — the case restated α = 0 and compared the governing k_red with member 1's — and the frozen set, which watched every result and only some of the inputs, did not say the workbook had moved. It now freezes every input the case restates, 25 beside 9 results, and the case reads them from the cells. Then D32, a_1 of member 2, 120 mm becoming the 80 mm the schema gives that member (21.09.2026, 12h42): the classeur and the project's `VU-SD10` now agree on every cell the test reads — k_red,2 = 0.921, R_d = 4.552 kN, R_d,Verb = 18.207 kN — to the last digit, where they had differed by 7 % on R_d. `test_dowel` and `test_norms`: 95 passed.

- **v0.85** (2026-09-20) — a level that prints nothing, one command that says what there is to name, a value read wherever it was written down, and a polar moment that publishes what it worked out (§3.3quinquies, §3.7, §4, §10bis, §10quinquies, §10sexies, §13).

  **The request.** Two sentences of the engineer's, the second correcting the first as he sent it: «pour var et eq, rajoute le level:0. Au level 0, il ne doit pas y avoir d'affichage», then «lorsqu'un level:1 fait en sorte que rien ne s'affiche, alors il faut que ce soit plutôt level:0. Tout autre chose que level:0 doit toujours afficher quelque chose.» The second is the general rule and it reaches further than `var` and `eq`: it is a statement about every command of `lib/formula.typ`.

  **What was wrong.** A declaration was hidden with `level: 1`, and level 1 is the *client* report (§3.7). So one scale carried two notions — how much of a calculation a report shows, and whether a line belongs on a page at all — and the confusion fell in exactly one direction: `#var("k_dens", 0.806, level: 1)` meant «hide this», and what it did was hide it **from the client alone**. A variable written by hand was in the engineer's report and the checker's and missing from the one report where the bare answer above it is most nearly the whole point.

  **What changed.** `var` and `var-unit` print from level 1 rather than from level 2, so level 1 prints what level 2 prints; `var-line` lays out its row from level 1; and `eq`, `val` and `verify`, which printed at every level, gained a level 0 that prints nothing. Six commands, one rule: **0 is not on the page, 1, 2 and 3 are how much of the page.**

  **Level 0 prints nothing and does everything else.** The variable is stored, the formula is evaluated and checked, the conversion is performed, the relation is compared — only the emission is held back, and every refusal fires as it would at level 3: a name the registry does not hold, a sum of different dimensions, two sides that cannot be compared. A line hidden today is a line printed in the next report, and it must not be that report which finds the mistake. `tests/test_formula.py` asserts all three of them at level 0.

  **A level nobody had declared had three meanings.** `check-level` refuses anything outside `auto`, 0, 1, 2 and 3, on all six. Only `var-line` checked until now: `level: 4` was read as 3, and `level: 0` printed the whole line in `eq`, `val` and `verify`, printed nothing in `var` and `var-unit`, and was refused by `var-line`.

  **What moved on a page.** The 199 frozen examples were regenerated and **12 changed**, every one of them an `-L1` variant (`var`, `var-unit`, `var-line`, `val`, `verify`, `eq`): the declarations that used to vanish at level 1 are printed there now. No level-2 or level-3 page moved, and `data/` was not touched. The engineer's own `Sections/005-tragwerksanalyse.typ` was migrated with it — five `#var(…, level:1)` became `level:0`, and the `#show-connector-resistance("VU-SD10", level: 1)` beside them was deliberately left alone, a `show-*` level 1 being the client version and not a hidden line. That migration is readable by the **old** engine too, which treated `level: 0` on a `#var` as «below 2, print nothing», so the section could be written before the engine moved.

  **Four tests.** Level 0 printing nothing asserted on the *whole* page rather than value by value, which is what catches a command leaving a mark of itself behind — a separator, an empty grid row, a footnote marker, the ✔ of a relation; level 0 still declaring, computing, converting and refusing; every level but 0 printing something, on all six commands at once; and a level outside 0-3 refused by each of them. 64 pass in `test_formula.py`.

- **v0.84** (2026-09-20) — a table is three rules and nothing else, a polar moment is read in fewer lines, and a dowel connection prints the norm's formulas instead of tables of its results (§3.3, §3.3quinquies, §3.3nonies, §10bis, §10quater, §11ter, §13).

  **The request.** The engineer asked for every table the engine sets to be drawn the way a printed norm and a paper draw one, and wrote the Typst he wanted to read: `stroke: none`, an `align` per column, `table.hline(stroke: 1pt)` above the headings and under the last row, `table.hline(stroke: 0.5pt)` under them — with the important columns bold, which `render-table` already did for a `results` var, a `totals` row and a `bold-columns` column.

  **One edit.** `render-table` in `lib/calc-engine.typ` is the only `table()` in the project — every other tabular thing here is a `grid` — so the layer build-ups, SIA 261's Tabelle 8, the wind's six global forces and its local envelope, a connector's four tables and a polar moment's three all changed together, and no `Sections/*.typ` was touched. `stroke: 0.4pt + luma(70%)` became `stroke: none`, `inset: 4pt` became `inset: (x: 5pt, y: 4pt)` — with no vertical rule, the space between two columns is what parts them — and the rules are `rule-thick` (1 pt) above the headings and under the last row and `rule-thin` (0.5 pt) under the headings and above the `totals`, which was a heavier 0.8 pt while it had a grid to be seen among.

  **A column is aligned by what it holds** (`column-align`): `number` and `value` read from the right, so the digits line up down the column; `math` centred, being a column of symbols (`g_k`/`a_k` over a layer build-up); `text` and `wide` from the left, like the prose around them. A header follows its own column, so a numeric heading stands over its last digit. This is part of the change rather than a decoration on top of it: with the grid gone, alignment is what the eye reads a column by. ⚠️ A `math` column holding numbers — SIA 261's Tabelle 8 — stays centred: right-aligning it would align its footnote markers rather than its digits, so the one rule serves both uses of the kind.

  **A group's name carries a rule spanning exactly its own columns** (`group-spans`, `group-rules`) — «in Faserrichtung» over Tabelle 24's two columns, «Bezugsfläche» over the wind's three — a vertical rule no longer being there to say how far the name reaches. `group-header-cells` was refactored onto that same `group-spans`, so a run of columns sharing a group is worked out in one place and read twice. ⚠️ Both header rules sit **inside** `table.header`: an hline written outside it is not repeated, and a table that breaks would carry its headings onto the next page with nothing above or below them.

  **The Katalog's own copy of that table follows** (`calc/tools/connectors_tab.py`, `record_table`), whose docstring has promised since v0.77 that «Minimale Abstände» and «Verschiebungsmodul» are laid out as the report lays them out: an `align` per cell from the column's kind, `border-bottom` on a group's `<th>`, and the rules under the headings and above the totals. ⚠️ Doing it turned up a rule that had **never shown**. The totals' rule was written `<tr style="border-top:1px solid">`, and Qt's rich text honours a border on a **cell** and ignores one on a row — measured by rendering both forms through a `QTextDocument` into a `QImage`, rather than read off the documentation. It is set on the cells now, and the window shows the rule the docstring always claimed for it. The window keeps no outer rule: a panel is already a box.

  **What did not change.** `data/` and every number in it, so the export is unchanged to the byte and the report says exactly what it said; the report's text; and the rest of `render-table` — a narrow table still centred in the calculation's own column, set at the text's own size, kept whole where it fits on a page, its `highlight` row still bold on its tinted ground. The 187 frozen example pages were regenerated and 13 moved: `show-calc`, `show-connector-resistance`, `show-live-load` and `show-polar-moment`, which are exactly the examples that print a table (§10sexies).

  **A polar moment, read in fewer lines** (§3.3quinquies, §10bis, §13). One message of the engineer's, about the «Moments polaires» module, in two points.

  **The positions table opens level 3.** `show-polar-moment` printed the rotation centre — `x_c = Σx_i/n = …`, `z_c = …` — and then *Lage der Verbindungsmittel*, whose x and z columns are the very numbers those two means are taken over. The order is reversed: the table first, x_c and z_c under it, and the Bezugselement and the angles θ_(r,j) after them as before. The table, now the first thing under the heading, takes the heading's own 0.7 em rather than a table's 1.6 em, which is `show-calc`'s own rule for a table opening a calculation. ⚠️ One consequence, and it is stated rather than hidden: with `title: none` the citation used to stand beside the rotation centre at the top and now stands beside it under the table — a table read from the data carries no `norm` of its own, so there is nowhere on it to put one.

  **A declaration and its working share a row.** `n` and `I_p` are joined by the semicolon `show-calc` already joins the vars of a `group` with — `n = 4; I_p = 28800 mm²` at level 2, and at level 3 I_p's own two lines with them, `n = 4; I_p = I_p,x + I_p,z; I_p = 14400 + 14400 = 28800 mm²` — and each of the three force formulas is joined to its own numbers line: `F_(x,i) = (N_x + V_x)/n + (M·Δz_i)/I_p; F_(x,3) = (40+0)/4 + (3.004·10³·60)/28800 = 16.259 kN`, and likewise F_z and F. Four lines fewer at level 2, five at level 3. The answer stays bold and the letters line does not, which is what still marks the answer; the moment's footnote marker keeps its place before the semicolon; and the joint is `semicolon-str()`, so a French report keeps its narrow no-break space before it.

  **Checked** on the example project's VU-MP-1 at levels 1, 2 and 3, with a heading and with `title: none`, with M = 0, and in German, French and English — the last with five-digit forces, where the joined lines are longest and still fit beside the citation column. ⚠️ Nothing computed changed: the same numbers, in the same order, on fewer rows.

  **A dowel connection prints the norm's own formulas** (§3.3, §3.3nonies, §10bis, §10quater, §11ter, §13). One message of the engineer's about the *Moyens d'assemblage* tab and the report it produces, ten points, taken in one pass.

  **Three tables became formulas.** *Lochleibungsfestigkeit* (Tabelle 22), *Hilfswert k_beta* (Tabellen 45/46) and *Verschiebungsmodul* (Tabelle 25) held nothing but the numbers a formula of SIA 265 had produced. Each is a chain of steps now, composed from the very JSON factors the arithmetic used (§11): `f_(h,0,1) = 0.082 · (1 − 0.01 · d) · ρ_(k,1)`, `f_(h,90,1) = f_(h,0,1)/(1.35 + 0.015 · d)`, the interpolation across α; `k_(β,max) = sqrt(4 β_f/(1+β_f))`, the required thicknesses `t_(i,1)`, `t_(i,2)` with the bracket of the row they were read off, the corner values, and **the branch of Figur 45 the thickness actually falls in** — interpolated, proportional from the origin, or flat past the upper corner, which is the rule `show-calc` already follows for the snow load's own conditional formula; and Tabelle 25's `K_("ser",i)` with its two tabulated coefficients and η_w. A member that is **not predrilled** gets one line and not three: that row of Tabelle 22 gives one value for both directions. `analyse` records on each corner the row of the table it came from (`shape`), which is what lets the formula be composed rather than described.

  ⚠️ **Tabellen 45 and 46, not Tabelle 23.** The engineer named Tabelle 23, which is the *simplified* k_beta 6.2.1.1 offers as an alternative; this module has computed the Anhang A form for every configuration since v0.68, at his own choice. Printing Tabelle 23 would put formulas on the page that do not produce the numbers beside them. Flagged here, in `dowel.record`'s docstring and in §13 rather than done silently.

  **Less spread down the page.** A step may declare itself **`inline`** (§3.3): its letters, its numbers and its answer on one row — `M_(u,k) = 0.3 · f_(u,k) · d^2.6 = 0.3 · 510 · 10^2.6 = 60910.397 Nmm`. Equation (88) itself deliberately is not, being the long one. A **bounded** formula never is, having no numbers line to join, and where the substitution would say nothing new — `k_(β,1,2) = k_(β,max)` — the numbers are left out rather than printed twice. And a run of single values is one row: `d = 10 mm; n_tot = 4; f_(u,k) = 510 N/mm²`; `Bauteil 1: ρ_(k,1) = 350 kg/m³; t_1 = 50 mm; Bauteil 2: …`, for which a var that *joins* a group already open may now name itself inside the row; and `k_β = 1.266; p = 2; (p · k_β) = 2.531; k_red = 0.977; n_(quer,1) = 2; n_(quer,2) = 2; k_α = 0.73; η_w = 0.8; η_t = 1`. Level 3 of the project's own connector went from 34 rows to 28 while gaining nine formulas.

  **A var may say `keep`** (§3.3). `show-calc` has left an intermediate step's operands off rows of their own since v0.20; writing the tables out as formulas turned d, ρ_k and t into operands, and all of them would have vanished from the page — which is the opposite of what was asked. α is deliberately not kept: it shows where it acts, inside f_h,α,k and inside k_red.

  **R_d first, then R_d,Verb.** Equation (88) without n_tot *is* the resistance of one fastener, so `analyse` computes it and `R_(d,"Verb") = n_"tot" · R_d` follows; `results` is `["R_d", "R_d_Verb"]`. The numbers are unchanged to the last digit, which `tests/test_dowel.py` holds against `Verbindung.xlsm` cell by cell. A **truss node reduces both** — `R_d_Fachwerk` beside `R_d_Verb_Fachwerk` — because the report gives both.

  **η_w inside Tabelle 25's formula**, on each member's own K_ser rather than on the connection's: the product is the same and this is where the report can say it. `K_("ser","Verb") = n_"tot" · p · K_"ser"` is a formula and no longer a totals row, and it is **level 1** — `show-connector-resistance` prints it under R_d,Verb at levels 1 and 2, pushed after the `results` rather than added to them, since `results` are the resistances a verification reads.

  **Tabelle 24's empty top-left cell names the diameter**, `$d$ = 10 mm`: every distance in that table is a multiple of d and it said nowhere which. It is a `group` over one column, which `render-table` now sets at that column's own alignment, through `render-prose` so the label may carry math, and with no rule under it.

  **What is left of the k_beta table is its figures**: a `tables` entry with a title, a note and its `curves`, and `"columns": [], "rows": []`. `render-table` skips the grid for such a plate, so SIA 265's Figur 45 keeps its own `level` and `place` without a second mechanism; `connector.figures` still finds its curves there and `validation._check_tables` needed no change.

  **Where each member's strength class came from** — the note under the Lochleibungsfestigkeit table, a list of sentences since v0.79 (AUD-3-7) — is the **description of that member's own ρ_k** now: one sentence about one var, which is a stricter form of the rule it was fixed to.

  **In the Katalog.** *Disposition des broches* stands before *Broches* (plural in French and English), because the pattern is drawn first and n_tot is read off it — and that field now **shows the count of ticked markers** and is read-only while it follows it, `draft()` having taken n_tot off the drawing since v0.74 while the spin box showed whatever was loaded. Hovering *Pièce 1* or *Pièce 2* rings that member's piece **on the section** as well as its element on the pattern: the outer two of a *zweischnittig* joint for member 1, the middle one for member 2, every timber piece for the one member of a Stahl-Holz joint (`connector_svg.highlighted_pieces`). And a lit result sets its own ink with its ground — a pale ground under the palette's text is unreadable in a dark theme and barely there in a light one, so lighting a row was making it harder to read. The *Verschiebungsmodul* table left the pane with the table itself; K_ser and K_ser,Verb are two rows of the results now.

  **The glossary** gained 16 terms and 2 templates (§4bis) — the labels and notes the new steps print, and *Feuchteklasse {n}.* and *Der Schlupf beträgt rund {slip} mm*, both of which had been untranslated before this version. A step's **name** carries none, and §13 says why: no surface prints one.

  **Checked** end to end on the project's own connector at levels 1, 2 and 3, compiled and read back out of the PDF; `data/` re-exported and every number held against `Verbindung.xlsm`. `tests/test_connectors.py` gained seven checks — the box order, n_tot following the ticks, the section highlight in all four assemblies, `inline` and `keep`, the formulas written out with the branch that applied (planted: a timber thinner than its own lower corner), the plate with no grid, and a truss node reducing both answers — and `test_i18n` and `test_dowel` were brought to the new shape.

  ⚠️ **Not ours, and still red**: `test_dowel.test_the_workbook_still_holds_the_frozen_cells`. The engineer changed `Verbindung.xlsm`'s Holz-Holz sheet after 19.09 — C33, C40, C43, C44 and C62 all moved — so `tests/fixtures/verbindung.json` is stale. That test exists to say so, and re-freezing is the engineer's decision, not this version's.

  **A run of calculations is announced by a line of its own** (§3.3, §3.3nonies, §10bis, §13). The engineer read the level 3 the paragraphs above had just produced — some twenty-five formulas one under the other — and asked for the presentation of Bemessungsbeispiele zur SIA 265 p. 291: «le level 3 est trop brouillon … il y a des indications de ce qu'on calcule (e.g. Charakteristische Lochleibungsfestigkeiten für C24:) avant le calcul. Copie l'affichage».

  **The mechanism.** A var may carry a **`heading`**, which `show-calc` prints as a row of its own above it — plain text in the report's own language, its colon written by the engine so French keeps its narrow space, and its symbols set as math like every other sentence the data carries. Twelve lines: `heading` on `engine.var`, one field in `schemas/calculations.schema.json`, `calc-heading` and four lines in `show-calc`'s loop. ⚠️ It is printed **where that var's own first row is printed**, so it follows the level and the rule that absorbs an operand into the formula that shows it: a heading on a var that prints nothing is dropped in silence. That happened three times while this was written — `n_1`, `eta_(w,"Steif")` and the first place tried for the reduction factor — and `tests/test_connectors.py` now walks every heading of all four assemblies and refuses a dropped one.

  **The layout.** The blocks are the book's: «Charakteristische Lochleibungsfestigkeiten für C24», «Verhältnis der Lochleibungsfestigkeiten», «Holzdicken», «Erforderliche Holzdicken und Hilfswert $k_beta$», then Reduktionsbeiwert, Fliessmoment, Bemessungswert des Tragwiderstands and Verschiebungsmodul. The thicknesses left the material row for a block of their own, M_u,k moved down to the resistance — which is the only formula that reads it, and where the book puts it — and each piece is named on a line of its own, «Bauteil 1 – Seitenholz», the form the two Figur 45 plates are already captioned with. A einschnittig joint's pieces are both Seitenhölzer, so there the number stands alone; a shear plane is never named that way, its letter being in every symbol of its run. The two plates and Tabelle 24 are kept, as the engineer asked.

  **The formulas are Anhang A's, not Tabelle 23's.** p. 291 is the simplified calculation and this module computes the accurate one (v0.68). The layout is copied, the formulas are not: a page whose formula does not produce the number beside it is the one failure this display layer exists to prevent.

  **Checked**: the four assemblies through `dowel.record`, every heading surviving the row rule; the report compiled at level 3 and the headings read back out of the PDF; every heading a term or a template of the glossary, in French and English. Nothing computed changed — the same numbers, in the same order, under names.

  ⚠️ **A loss to record.** A `git checkout -- tests/test_connectors.py` of mine, meant to undo a mangled insert of my own, took the uncommitted v0.84 work of the session that wrote the connector half with it, and that session had ended before it could be recovered. The tests it had added or updated in that file are **reconstructions**, written from the code they guard and from §11ter: `test_the_inputs_and_the_factors_are_each_one_row`, `test_the_norms_own_formulas_are_written_out_rather_than_tabulated`, `test_the_short_formulas_are_set_on_one_line`, `test_the_slip_modulus_is_a_formula_and_not_a_table`, `test_a_truss_node_reduces_both_answers`, `test_the_dowel_layout_comes_before_the_dowels`, `test_the_dowels_box_takes_its_count_from_the_layout`, `test_hovering_a_member_rings_its_piece_on_the_section`, and the updates to the Figur 45, spacings, lit-result and results-order assertions. They pass against the code, and they are not the tests that were written for it.


- **v0.83** (2026-09-19) — Woodcore, steps 5 and 6 of 6: the start page, the *Fichier* menu, one project per window and a lock between machines, `woodcore-cli new`, and the installed program (§10quater, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **The go.** The engineer asked for steps 5 and 6 to be carried out without stopping, after step 4 was committed.

  **The start page** (`calc/tools/home_page.py`). Started without a project, Woodcore opens on the projects opened lately, the last first — number, folder, last export, engine version —, a project gone greyed and offered to be forgotten, *Ouvrir…*, *Nouveau projet…*, *Convertir un ancien dossier…*, and a `.woodcore` dropped on it. `Woodcore.bat` with no argument opens it (`--home`); with one, that project.

  **One project per process.** The *Fichier* menu — *Nouveau projet*, *Ouvrir* (Ctrl+O), *Projets récents*, *Ouvrir dans une nouvelle fenêtre*, *Fermer le projet*, *Propriétés du projet*, *Ouvrir le dossier du projet*, *Ouvrir le PDF*, *Ouvrir dans VS Code*, *Quitter*, and from the sources the choice of these sources for a double-click — opens another project in a new process, closing this window first through `closeEvent`, and never under a running export. The title reads «• Musterprojekt — Woodcore» while a tab holds unsaved changes. `main()` takes `--project`, `--home` and `--self-test`.

  **The lock** (`calc/project_lock.py`): `<name>.woodcore.lock` beside the project, not `~$<name>.woodcore` as planned — NextCloud's default exclusion list carries `~$*`. A project another window holds opens read-only on the engineer's answer: a red banner, «(lecture seule)» in the title, and every write refused — `WRITERS` wrapped once on the five tab classes and on `source_archive.Actions`, the export, the PDF and the properties refused.

  **Woodcore's own settings** (`calc/tools/usersettings.py`): `%APPDATA%\Woodcore\settings.json` with the recent projects, the caches and the stall log in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Woodcore\`, the Typst-Katalog's taken over once; `WOODCORE_SETTINGS` for the tests, `TYPST_KATALOG_SETTINGS` until now. The empty folders of the editor tabs moved into the project file, `katalog.empty_folders` (`project.update`, `project.set_empty_folders`). The icon is a «W» on a beam, `woodcore.svg`; the window's words, title and message boxes say Woodcore.

  **A new project, an old folder** (`calc/project_template.py`): `woodcore-cli new <folder>` and *Nouveau projet* copy `templates/projet/` — the report, its sections looping over the export, `.vscode` — make the Katalog's folders and a `Positionen.xlsx` with the export's headers, write the `.woodcore` and copy the engine; no load workbook, the office's template being one openpyxl cannot write. *Convertir un ancien dossier* writes a `.woodcore` naming a pre-Woodcore folder's parts where they are; `engine_state` gains `unknown`, engine files no VERSION names, replaced only once asked. `woodcore-cli export --data-only` is the export alone, and *Importer Excel* runs it. The project file may state `woodcore_version` and `katalog` (`schemas/woodcore-project.schema.json`, still 1.0: both optional).

  **The installed program** (`packaging/`). `python packaging/build.py [--compare <project>]` builds in an environment of its own (requirements.txt and PyInstaller 6.22.3, `packaging/requirements-build.txt`) `Woodcore.exe` and `woodcore-cli.exe` in one folder with Typst 0.14.2 (`woodcore.spec`, 191 MB) and `Woodcore-Launcher.exe` (`launcher.spec`), the program's sources shipped as they are beside them; writes `build-info.json`, which an installed program reports in `data/project.json`; runs them on a copy of the example project; and writes the installer with Inno Setup (`woodcore.iss`: per user, without administrator, each version beside the others, the launcher owning `.woodcore`) or, without it, a zip. The launcher starts the engineer's sources where told to, else the version a project pins, else the newest — unless the project is newer than any installed. Checked here: the example project exported and compiled by woodcore-cli.exe with its own Typst, opened by Woodcore.exe (`--self-test`) and on its start page, a new project made by woodcore-cli.exe, the launcher's choice (`--dry-run`), and Musterprojekt's `data/` identical to the byte from the sources and from the built program (37 files). The installer was written once Inno Setup 6 was installed, the morning after: `dist/Woodcore-0.83.0-setup.exe` (62 MB); until then the build had written `dist/Woodcore-0.83.0-win64.zip` (91 MB) in its place. `--installer-only` compiles the script again from the program the last build left, no PyInstaller rerun -- which is what an edit to `woodcore.iss` needs, and the first attempt needed one: `[Registry]` has no `uninsneveruninstall` flag, Inno refuses the **whole** script over one unknown flag, and it says so only when it compiles, on the build's last step; `tests/test_packaging.py` holds every entry's flags against its own section's set now. Installing it, and a double-click on a project, are the engineer's own test.

  **Suite.** 613 passed, 4 skipped, and the two deliberate failures of B25; new: `test_home.py`, `test_lock.py`, `test_new_project.py`, `test_packaging.py`.

- **v0.82** (2026-09-19) — Woodcore, step 4 of 6: the program and the project live in folders of their own, the engine is copied into the project with a record of what produced the export, and the code's history was cleaned (§2, §4, §4bis, §10bis, §10quater, §10sexies, §11, §11bis, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **The go.** The engineer validated step 4 in advance and asked for it to be done after the commit of steps 0 to 3. It was done in two halves: the code first, in the tree as it stood, then the move.

  **The engine's copy, guarded.** `project.engine_state` compares a project's `_woodcore/` with the program's engine: *absent* and *stale* are written, *current* is left, *older* and *edited* are replaced only with `--update-engine` or once the Katalog's dialog (`engine_consent`, the difference one click away) is answered, *newer* is never replaced. The copy is checked before the export reads anything and written after it succeeds, read-only. `tests/test_project.py` holds every state and the command line refusing to overwrite an edited copy.

  **What produced an export.** `data/project.json` (`project.record`, `schemas/project.schema.json`): the project's name and its optional details — number, client, address, company, author, checker, which the `.woodcore` schema gained —, Woodcore's version and `git describe --always --dirty`, Typst's version and the SHA-256 of every workbook read; no date, so an unchanged export stays unchanged. In Typst, `project-info()` reads it and `woodcore-document`, a show rule, gives the PDF its title, author and keywords; the title page is built from it, and «Projektname» became the project's name.

  **One zip per archive.** `archive_export.py` writes `<timestamp>.zip` and a flat copy of the PDF into the project's `_Archiv/`, the manifest carrying `project.json`; the folders of hundreds of files had met Windows' 260 characters. The workbooks stay out, their hashes in.

  **`woodcore-cli`.** `calc/cli.py` and `woodcore-cli.bat`: `export` (the export, the PDF, the archive; `--level`, `--uniform`, `--lang`, `--all-levels`, `--skip-data`, `--update-engine`), `check` (the whole export, writing nothing) and `sync-engine`. It replaces `build.ps1` and the `Makefile`; the window's menu builds its flags through its `input_args`. `Woodcore.bat` opens the Katalog on a project — its argument, else `WOODCORE_PROJECT`'s, else the last one opened, else the one beside the code, else a file dialog — and the window's title names it. The examples compile against `examples/`' own `config.typ`, and the commands tab's preview is handed to Typst on standard input.

  **The move.** `Musterprojekt/` (the project: its workbooks, `Musterprojekt.woodcore` now stating no layout, `Statischer Bericht (typst)/` with its `_woodcore/`, `Woodcore-Datei/`, `_Archiv/` with every earlier archive as it was, `.vscode/`), `Woodcore/` (the program, the one repository, with `Woodcore.bat`, `woodcore-cli.bat`, `.gitignore` and a new `.gitattributes`) and `ExternalFiles/` (the reference library). The report's `commands.typ` imports the engine's copy, `_woodcore/lib/`. Checked against the reference of step 0: `data/` identical but for the new `project.json`, the PDF's text identical but for the title page. The norms' `_source`, the glossary's sources line and every comment naming the old layout point at the new one; `project.checkout_project` looks in the folders beside `Woodcore/` only.

  **The history, cleaned.** The whole repository was bundled first, `ancien-depot-v0.82.bundle`, and verified; `git filter-repo`, in a clone, kept the code's paths renamed to the root and dropped the reference library, the workbooks, the project's report, sources, export and archives, the architect's plans and the examples' snapshot from every commit; the result became `Woodcore/.git`, and the old `.git` was renamed `_ancien-git/`, not deleted. The examples' snapshot and pages were added back in the move's commit, their plan backgrounds blank pages — the architect's plans do not go into a shared repository — and the show-plan pages regenerated.

  **Suite.** 582 passed, 4 skipped, and the two deliberate failures of B25, on the example project and, for the workbooks, on `Musterprojekt/`.

  **Next.** Step 5 — the start page, the *Fichier* menu, the lock, `woodcore-cli new` — and step 6, the `.exe` (§14).

- **v0.81** (2026-09-19) — the program and the project come apart: Woodcore, steps 0 to 3 of 6 (§2, §10quater, §10sexies, §11, §11ter, §12, §13, §14).

  **The request.** The engineer wants the code and the Katalog to work as a program installed on the computer — an `.exe` opening on a start page, a file with an extension of its own in each project folder, projects switched like documents in Word — so that a folder of structural work holds the project and nothing else. Proposals were weighed with the engineer and decided (§13): Woodcore, an `.exe` built on demand and run from sources daily, the engine copied into each project, one project per window, only the code in git with its history cleaned, and the names of the folders. The work is in six steps; this version is the first four of them that move nothing on disk.

  **Step 0, the reference.** Before anything changed: the export hashed and the report compiled at a fixed timestamp. Every step after it was checked against both — the export and the PDF are identical to the byte — and against the suite's own result (566 passed, the two deliberate failures of B25).

  **Step 1, a project named by its file.** `calc/project.py` and `schemas/woodcore-project.schema.json`: a `.woodcore` file names a project, its folder is the project's, and `layout` says where the report, the PDF, the sources, the export and the archives are, a key left out taking the new layout. Twelve modules derived their paths from `__file__` and five default arguments bound one at import; every one of them now asks `project.current()`. `export_all.py`, the window, `build_reference.py`, `build_examples.py` and `archive_export.py` take `--project`; a process naming none takes `WOODCORE_PROJECT`, else the one `.woodcore` beside the checkout, which the provisional `Musterprojekt.woodcore` at the top of the folder is. An export without `Positionen.xlsx` says so by name. `tests/test_project.py` holds all of it, the guard against a path fixed at import again included.

  **Step 2, an example project for the tests.** `tests/projet-exemple/`, a real project anonymised without its workbooks (the engineer's choice): blank pages in place of the architect's, blank pictures of the same sizes, the introduction and the title page without the school, the city, the architects, the office or the engineer. `project.sync_engine` copies the engine into a project's `_woodcore/` with a `VERSION`, which is what lets it compile. Each run of the suite works on its own copy of it (`conftest.PROJECT`), compiles its probes there (`conftest.probe_dir`), and reads the engineer's own project only for a workbook (`conftest.real_project()`); the tests that patched module constants and `__defaults__` name a project instead (`project.using`, `conftest.project_copy`). The cells of `Verbindung.xlsm` are frozen in `tests/fixtures/verbindung.json`, and a full run skipping more than 15 tests fails. Found on the way: the stallwatch child left its copy behind, `os._exit` running no `atexit`.

  **Step 3, the examples on their own root.** `examples/config.typ`, the report's styling copied, and the preview handed to `typst compile -` on standard input with `examples/` as its root: nothing is written into a project, and `.preview/` and the prefix derived from its depth are gone. `build_examples.py` keeps `config.typ` when it sweeps. The 183 pages, regenerated, are byte-identical.

  **Next.** Step 4 — the move into `Musterprojekt/`, `Woodcore/` and `ExternalFiles/`, the history cleaned, the engine copy's prompts, `build-info.json`, zipped archives, the title page from the project — waits for the engineer's go (§14).

- **v0.80** (2026-09-17) — one command prints one value, and a row of declarations fits on one line (§3.3septies, §4, §4bis, §10bis, §10ter, §10quater, §10quinquies, §10sexies, §11ter, §13).

  **The question.** Five `#var` calls take five lines, each printing a row of a grid; the engineer asked how to set them side by side, and whether `#val` could print a variable's name beside its value. What came out of answering it is a decision about the command surface, taken by the engineer point by point.

  **`var-line`.** `var-line[ … ]` joins its declarations the way `show-calc` joins the vars of a `group` — `R_k = 7 kN; k_dens = 0.806; γ_M = 1.7` — with `label:`, `norm:`/`norm-ref:`, `level:` and `sep:`. A **container** and not an `inline:` flag on each command: only a container writes the separators (so a value a level hides takes its separator with it), keeps a `;` from being typed after a `#var(...)` where Typst swallows it, and carries one citation in the right-hand column, which is a grid. `#var`, `#var-unit`, `#eq`, `#val` and `#verify` read one state, `var-line-state`, in the `context` they already print from; in a row, an item's own label and citation become one footnote. A row with `level: 1` declares its variables and prints nothing at all.

  **`val`, and three commands gone.** `val` takes a name in the registry, a name in a `var-domain`, a name in the export, a var object or a bare number, and prints it — with `domain:` (a string for a domain of the report, a **pair** for a calculation and its instance), `type:` (`val`, `unit`, `val+unit`, `expression`), `unit:`, `symbol:`, `level:`, `label:`, `norm:`/`norm-ref:`. `fmt`, `inline-value` and `live-load` are **removed**, folded into it at the engineer's decision, with nothing lost and no call longer than before. `"number"` / `"number+unit"` are refused naming the four parts; the v0.61 two-positional form is refused with its rewrite. Levels govern the extras only: 1 the value, 2 the label and the choices, 3 the citation, all as one footnote.

  **An overridden unit is orange.** `#val("R", unit: "N")` prints the value converted, leaves the registry untouched, and adds a footnote — «Die Einheit ist für die Darstellung überschrieben: der Wert steht in N statt in kN» — whose marker is orange too (`override-colour` in `lib/calc-engine.typ`, the Katalog's own `OVERRIDE_COLOR`; `formula.unit_override` in the glossary, three languages). A unit of another dimension is refused.

  **`descr:` is `label:`** on `#var`, one word for one notion.

  **Everything that follows from it.** `Sections/005` sets its five declarations on one line and reads its two moments with `val(..., domain:)`; `build_reference.call_val` replaces `call_inline_value` and `call_live_load`, and the categories name `var-line`; the Katalog's copy buttons hand over `#val(…)`; `render-table`, `show-check` and the internals print through `fmt-number` / `fmt-decimals`, which stay; the frozen examples of the three deleted commands are gone and `val`'s and `var-line`'s are rendered in their place (183 examples); `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` is regenerated. **Tests**: twelve in `tests/test_formula.py` (the four parts, the three sources, a var object and a bare number, the orange line and that it changes nothing, six refusals, the row's separators, its hidden item, its label and citation, a hidden row that still declares, the footnote in a row, and that the three commands are gone) and one in `tests/test_constants.py` (the orange is one colour in the window and in the report). The suite is 566 passed, 4 skipped, and the two deliberate reds of §10sexies.

- **v0.79** (2026-09-17) — the periodic audit's decisions, taken by the engineer point by point and applied, each defect with a test that failed first (§3.3, §3.3ter, §3.3quater, §3.3quinquies, §3.3sexies, §3.3octies, §3.3nonies, §3.3decies, §4bis, §7bis, §10, §10bis, §10quater, §10quinquies, §11, §11ter, §13)

  **Export.** B2: `connection.calculation_state` — a polar moment with no schema, a schema the export does not list, a fastener or an element lost, or nothing ticked is exported with `calculated: false` and an English `reason`; `data/connections.json` 4.1 (`calculated`, `reason` required); `find-calculation` and `show-polar-moment` refuse it with the reason; `validation.py` checks the pair and no longer calls such a record fastener-less. A connector whose schema is gone or whose ticked markers the schema no longer carries: `calculated: false`, reasons `connector.schema_gone` / `connector.markers_lost`, the n_tot-against-drawing check only when calculated. B3: `dead_load` reads column K; a row with no material and a number typed there is counted as `Direkt eingegebene Last` (a term). B13: `connector_svg.section` word-free, `section_state`/`section_state_of`; the record's `section_scaled` (`""`, `enlarged`, `reduced`, optional in `connectors.schema.json`); `connector-figures` writes `connector.section_enlarged`/`_reduced` under the section; the Katalog's preview writes it in the window's language. B12: the «Lochleibungsfestigkeit» note is a list — a term and one `connector.provenance_position`/`_override`/`_stated` sentence per member (`ohne Festigkeitsklasse` a term); `render-table` and the loads tab join a list; `calculations.schema.json` `note` string or array; `connector.embedment_note` removed. B23: `connector.upgrade_source` reads 1.0 as 1.1, so the migration refusal fires.

  **Words.** B10: one word per notion across 76 Katalog strings and five glossary entries — «moyen(s) d'assemblage» / «fastener(s)», «charge(s) concentrée(s)» / «concentrated load(s)», «Step joint», «Crop», «Predrilling», «Dowel», «Directions des efforts», «Pièces», `plan.crop` «Detail»/«Détail»; German homonyms lifted («Tabellenblatt», «Verschiebung», «Element» for a schema's piece). B11: a template's `translate` list, honoured by `strings.translate` and `tl-of`, checked by `strings._check`; `snow.exposure_desc` translates the exposure. B14: Auflast = SIA 261 Tabelle 80 (p. 131) «Poids propre non-structural» / «Non-structural self-weight», «Eigen- und Auflasten» = chapter 2's title. B15: `norm-text` cites an annex without §, «Anhang» a term; the loads tab's norm field likewise. A 3-12: `colon-str`/`semicolon-str`, sixteen joints of `calc-engine.typ`. B22: `engine.Note` (a `str` with `code` and `args`) for the warnings of `plan`, `statics` (including `resolve`'s reasons and `drop_release_stiffness`), `schema` and `connection.schema_warnings`; `catalog_gui.note_text` and 27 `warn_*` keys in three languages; the four editor tabs show them in the window's language. A 4-1, 4-2, 4-6/4-7, 5-6: the «Schémas» pointer readout's snap kinds, a `QTranslator` (`install_qt_translation`), `sc_unreadable` and the plan background filter, five tooltips on disabled buttons. A 6-10: 40 STRINGS keys no code named, removed, and a test that names them.

  **Katalog and figures.** B5: *Modèles statiques* keeps the typed magnitude through a trip to *Variables* (`last_literal`). B16: `show-schema` defaults to `aids: true`. B17: a named point's `label` (the «Points» list's *nom* column) drawn with a leader once dragged clear, and a marker's and a dimension's label likewise (`schema_svg._leader`, `_left_home`). B18: `statics.drop_release_stiffness` — a stiffness on a shear or normal release dropped with a warning, at the export and when the tab opens the model (dirty). B19: `formula.typ` `check-printable-key` in `var` and `eq`. B20: `plan_svg.FONT = "Arial"`. B21: «Moyens d'assemblage» lists the draft of *Nouveau*/*Dupliquer* as «(nouveau, non enregistré)».

  **Workbooks and notes.** B1: the engineer corrected `WL!L70` in the three wind sheets of `Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` (`=J17*1*MIN(S78:S81)*J60`; template 3.2's `Wind` sheet already had it). B8: `Verbindung.xlsm` C62 `*Daten!O29`, C66 `*Daten!O24` (η_w for the stiffness), sheet protection restored with its options, with the engineer's authorization; `test_dowel.test_the_workbook_s_holz_holz_sheet_is_the_project_s_connector_cell_by_cell`. Notes in `000-TypstRapport/000-Audits/`: `260917-hinweis-vorlage-stabduebel-kser.md` (B8), `260917-hinweis-vorlage-biegesteifer-anschluss.md` (B9, and the one-row `#DIV/0!`), `260917-code-mort.md` (the 21 uncalled definitions, kept at the engineer's word). The engineer deleted `Positions Connectors`, `data_connector`, `data_assembly` and `data_steel` from `Positionen.xlsx` and renamed `VU-SD-10` to `SD-10`.

  **Removed.** `calc/tools/build_example_input.py` (B7), `calc/tools/migrate_connectors.py` (B28), `docs/Guide_prise_en_main.docx` (B29), `details/SD_01.json` and `details/SD_02.json` (B27); four `Thumbs.db` untracked. Left by decision: `Sections/004`'s hand-written case list (B4, comment corrected), `Sections/005`'s η_w and γ_M/η_M (B6), the frozen examples (B25). B24 unanswered.

  **Tests.** New: `test_statics.py`; `test_connectors` (section note, 1.0 to migration), `test_schema_consumers` (a lost schema never stops the export; the report refuses), `test_dead_load` (direct load), `test_i18n` (translate hole, note sentences, Auflast, editors' warnings, dialogs, Qt buttons, STRINGS keys named), `test_report` (annex, French colons), `test_formula` (printable key), `test_schema`/`test_schema_tab` (aids shown, labels and leaders, point names, snap readout), `test_editor_buttons` (magnitude round trip, connectors draft), `test_constants` (one font family), `test_catalog` (a disabled button says why), `test_dowel` (Verbindung.xlsm cell by cell). `test_stallwatch`: the planted stall and the whole-computer stop in a process of their own — under the full suite the posted-event backlog of earlier windows (1.56 s drained, measured) ran inside their loop; the partner file was not identified, a control run passing under another load. `test_schema_section.test_the_exported_figures_carry_the_label` reads every exported schema instead of the deleted `SD_01`. Full suite alone: 554 passed, 4 skipped, 2 failed — the two `test_commands` tests over the frozen examples (B25). `build_examples.py` run without `--freeze`: 8 pages re-rendered for the engine's changes.
- **v0.78** (2026-09-17) — the first periodic audit (`prompt-audit.md`, report in `000-TypstRapport/000-Audits/`) and the class-A defects it found, fixed with a test each: «Symétrie» moved no point, *Grille…* raised with «Sélection», *Supprimer* on an unsaved draft archived a saved entry, a refused *Ajouter* left an undo step, five copied commands lacked `context`; the connectors' warnings, `show-connector`'s words and the k_β rows in German in French and English; the forces note's semicolons; `show-position`/`show-calc` refusals listing the valid ids; `verify` `==`; a lost centre printed «festgelegt»; a schema's crop cutting the figures drawn on it; `schemas.json` naming the wrong source folder; the glossary checked first by the export; the dangling-example check reading schemas and comparing ids by kind; `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` rewritten only when its content changes, so the suite no longer dirties the tree; `run_tests.py -k` by function name, `Skip` a `unittest.SkipTest`, a `SyntaxWarning`, a probe of a removed command; the stale banner and the commands tab's fallback language; η_t and the truss factor read from `calc/norms/`; glossary terminology and French typography. More than a hundred documentation drifts of CLAUDE.md, README.md and tests/README.md put right, the §11ter test table completed (§3.3quinquies, §3.3octies, §3.3nonies, §3.3decies, §3.5, §3.6, §4, §4bis, §5, §7, §7bis, §8, §10bis, §10ter, §10quater, §10sexies, §11, §11bis, §11ter, §12, §13, §14)
- **v0.77** (2026-09-16) — the Katalog: Nom · Description · ID everywhere, folders that stay, a schema renamed is followed, construction lines over everything and to the edges, a canvas that no longer stalls, the strength class in a column of its own (§3.3quinquies, §3.3septies, §3.3octies, §3.3nonies, §3.3decies, §7, §7bis, §10quater, §11, §11ter, §13)

  One message of the engineer's, taken point by point; built alongside v0.76 by another session, which took the polar tab.

  **The «Schémas» module.** *The freeze*: `SvgLayer.paint` rendered the whole SVG on every paint and a paint follows every movement of the mouse — about 300 ms a paint at a device pixel ratio of 2 on a 1900 × 1000 view, measured offscreen, Qt's DeviceCoordinateCache not reused between paints. It renders once per view into an image now, re-rendered only when the SVG, the transform, the size or the pixel ratio change, and the grid is one `drawLines` per pen without antialiasing: ≈ 20 ms a paint. The whole computer stopping was not reproduced, so `calc/tools/stallwatch.py` writes the Python stack of any stall past 1.5 s to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\typst-katalog\stalls.log`. *Construction lines*: over everything and to the edges — the canvas draws every one in `drawForeground` across the view, heavier where `shown`, and renders its SVG with `aid_lines=False`; the figure draws the aids last (points, then lines) and clips a shown line to its frame (`schema_svg.clip_line`); `AID_REACH` is gone. *Snapping*: `schema.snap_geometry` — band corners and edges, and every crossing of axes, band edges and construction lines; a point slides along a construction line; kept once per state of the schema by the canvas; crossings rounded to 1e-6 mm.

  **Identity.** `calc/tools/identity.py`: `slug_id` (moved from `connectors_tab`, still importable there) and `IdentityRows`, used by the schemas, static models, plans and connectors tabs — Nom, Description (`label`; a connector's `description`), ID. *Groupe* removed from every form. *Connecteurs*' *Identité*: Schéma, Nom, Description, ID; *Variante*, *Désignation*, *Éléments reliés* and *Longueur* removed, `elements` now written from the schema's member positions, `label` folded into `description` on save. Optional `name` documented in `schema-source`, `statics-source`, `plan-source`; the lists show it. The polar tab's own identity is v0.76's.

  **Folders.** Four tabs connected `foldersChanged` to `reload_list` and so rebuilt the list from a stale copy of the empty folders: a folder made vanished at once. `on_folders_changed` in each. `grouped_list.title_row` adds a *Nouveau dossier* button at the right of every list's title, the polar tab's included.

  **Schemas renamed, tabs shown.** `SchemaTab.rename_references` rewrites `schema` in `connectors/*.json` and `connections/*.json`, exports both, and calls `schema_renamed` on the two tabs; `Catalog.on_tab_shown` calls `on_shown()`, with which *Connecteurs* and *Moments polaires* re-read the schemas keeping their drafts.

  **Connecteurs.** `BOX_STYLE` for every box and a `hot` property (the frames jumped; the lit box's selector reached the boxes inside it); the innermost watched box under the pointer is lit (`update_hot`); each *Pièce* box is watched with its member's results and lights its element on the pattern. `connector_svg.pattern_of` passed its `(collection, id)` highlight as `overlay`'s fastener index and never lit anything — an element goes to `mark`, a marker to its index. *Reprendre du dessin* restored to the markers row; `cn_no_markers` says a schema with no marker has nothing to tick. The section figure's widths were checked for 30/100, 1000/100, 100/30, 20/200 in both timber assemblies and are exact; no defect was found there.
