  **Moments polaires takes construction points.** The engineer, on reading that VU-Skizze's four points were not markers: «Moments polaires» reads connector markers **and** points. `connection.fastener_places(detail)` — the markers, then the aids of kind `point`, never a centre — is what `fastener_points` and `missing_markers` resolve against; the polar tab lists both (a point's tooltip says what it is), its schema line counts points, *Nouveau* ticks the markers or, on a schema without any, the points, and the warnings and `connection-source.schema.json` name both. A connector keeps markers only, its calculation reading the fastener's kind. Tests: +3 in `test_schema_consumers.py` (a group of points gives the markers' own I_p; a centre is not a fastener and markers and points mix; a connector ignores points), +1 in `test_polar_tab.py` (the list offers points, ticked by id), and `test_new_opens_on_a_schema_with_every_marker_ticked` follows the markers-or-points rule. The stall watch re-arms with `repeat=True`: once-only, a first stall used it up and a second one before the next tick went unwritten.

  **Where a value comes from, and what a member is.** The engineer: a value taken from a position says so in its own field, and a line under it appears only when it is written over, in orange — «vérifie que c'est le cas partout». *Connecteurs*: the grade combo's items carry the grade as data and read «C24, repris de T001» while it follows (`cn_followed`; every compare reads `currentData`, the text being display), the moisture class's «follow» item reads «2, repris de S001, T001», and the lines under both appear only for an override or a lost position, both orange; `cn_from_position` is gone. «Schémas»: b and h carry «mm, repris de T001» in their spin boxes while they follow, and the line under them — sc_overridden, sc_lost_position, sc_no_section — is shown only when there is one, always orange; `sc_from_position` is gone. Those are the only fields of the Katalog that follow a position. And *Essence*, ρ_k and a new mean unit weight are filled for every member whether or not the connector calculates (`fill_member_values`): the position's `material` (Fichte), SIA 265 Tab. 8/9's ρ_k for the grade in the field (the 5 % fractile), and SIA 261 Tab. 28's Raumlast for the grade's family and species; until then all three read «—» on a connector with no marker ticked. Read-only cells now show the number alone, their unit being the row's column. **Norm tables**: the engineer placed the mean values in «SIA 260 tableaux 28, 29, 30»; they are **SIA 261:2020 Anhang A** (pp. 77-78 — SIA 260 has no such tables), transcribed off a render of the pages into `calc/norms/sia261/tab28_raumlasten_baustoffe.json`, `tab29_flaechenlasten_bedachungen.json` and `tab30_raumlasten_lagergueter.json`, one file per table, read and checked by `calc/norms/sia261/__init__.py`; they are mean unit weights per kind of material, not densities per grade. Tabelle 8 (Nutzlasten) stays in `norms/sia261_tables.json`. Tests: +4 in `test_norms.py`, +2 in `test_connectors.py`, `test_schema_tab.py`'s two panel tests follow the rule, and `test_every_row_of_the_form_carries_a_unit` loads the project's first connector rather than `SD_01`, which the engineer renamed.

  **calc/norms/, flat, and the one source.** The engineer: every JSON restating a norm sits in `calc/norms/` as `<norm>-<year>-<table or clause>-<designation>.json`, no value twice, the only source for Python. The three packages `sia261/`, `sia263/`, `sia265/` and `sia261_tables.json/.py` are gone; in their place 23 files — `sia261-2020-tab08-nutzlasten`, `-tab28-raumlasten-baustoffe`, `-tab29-flaechenlasten-bedachungen`, `-tab30-raumlasten-lagergueter`; `sia263-2013-tab01-baustaehle`, `-tab02-schrauben`; `sia265-2021-tab01-gamma-m`, `-tab03-feuchteklassen`, `-tab04-eta-w`, `-tab08-vollholz`, `-tab09-brettschichtholz`, `-tab22-lochleibungsfestigkeit`, `-tab24-abstaende`, `-tab25-kser`, `-tab45-kbeta-holz-holz`, `-tab46-kbeta-stahl-holz`, `-2.2.6-eta-t`, `-5.3.6.2.2-fachwerk`, `-6.1.4.2-k-red`, `-6.1.4.4-fliessmoment`, `-6.2.1.1-k-alpha`, `-6.2.1.3-schlupf`; `sn-en-14592-2012-fu-k-stabduebel` — and four readers, `sia261.py` (Tabelle 8 merged in), `sia263.py`, `sia265.py`, `sn_en_14592.py`. Old `tab04_eta_w.json` was split into Tabelle 3, Tabelle 4 and 2.2.6, `tab25_kser.json` into Tabelle 25 and 6.2.1.3, `clauses.json` into one file per clause, and every value checked equal before the old files were deleted. **The formula coefficients moved out of `dowel.py`** (Tabelle 22, equations (86), (87), Tabellen 45/46, the (130)/(131) conditions, the diameters of (88)), verified against renders of pp. 63, 64, 95, 96; `dowel.py` composes its formula strings from them, `^(1\/4)` printing as `^0.25`. `validation.MOISTURE_CLASSES`/`CONNECTOR_SPECIES` → `moisture_classes()`/`connector_species()` reading Tabellen 3 and 22; the `510` of `connector.new_source`, `connectors_tab` and `migrate_connectors` → `sn_en_14592.f_u_k()`. `sia261.timber_row` removed with the row that used it.

  **Connecteurs, again.** *Poids volumique moyen* removed («INUTILE pour les calculs»). *Acier de la broche* removed — SIA 265 reads f_u,k only — and a grey note under f_u,k: S355, 510 N/mm² after SN EN 14592, the only value available, always the one used; the dowel block no longer writes `steel`, and the plate's grade row is hidden (and saved empty) for a Holz-Holz joint. **p and k_β** are shown while no marker is ticked (`connector.preview`, `dowel.preview`; never exported). The two saved connectors lost their `dowel.steel` and their `S235` plate grade. The **section figure** draws the first timber piece lower (`connector_svg.piece_offsets`): einschnittig the left piece below the right, zweischnittig both outer pieces below the middle, as SIA 265 and the classeur draw them.

  **Schémas: a «Points» list.** The named points left the *Aides* list for a *Points* tab right of *Connecteurs* — id, x, z, *visible*, **connecteur** — and a point ticked «connecteur» (`connector: true`, documented in `schema-source.schema.json`, additive) is the only point `connection.fastener_places` offers «Moments polaires»; *Ajouter* under *Points* puts a point down, under *Aides* a centre. VU-Skizze's A12–A15 are unticked, so tick them to use them.

  **The freeze every ~50 s.** Not reproduced: four minutes of a 20 ms latency probe and per-process counters on the engineer's machine, the Katalog open, saw no stop of the machine longer than 200 ms and the Katalog idle (0-5 % of a core, no I/O). What was found is the stall watch itself: `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later`, re-armed four times a second, makes CPython start a new operating-system thread at each re-arm — measured, 14 000 an hour. `stallwatch.py` is one watch thread now, beating against a `QTimer`, and it also writes «System hitch» when it wakes more than 0.75 s late, which tells a freeze of the whole computer from a stall of the window.

  **Schémas: points are green crosses in front of everything.** The engineer: «les points doivent être des croix vertes, affichées devant tout, même les lignes d'aides, afin d'être toujours visibles». `schema_svg.body` draws the centres, then the lines, then the points as crosses in `POINT_COLOUR` (`_point_cross`, `aid_points`); the canvas renders its SVG with neither lines nor points and draws both in `drawForeground`, the points last as a cross 8 px each way at every zoom (`aid_point_marks`); the *Point* tool's swatch is the green cross. A connection's figure, drawn on the same body, shows them green too. Tests: `test_schema.py` +1, `test_schema_tab.py` +1.

  **Tests** for these: `test_norms.py` 51 (+ the naming and no-orphan rule, a dowel.py without coefficients, the coefficients against the pages, SN EN 14592, three planted defects), `test_constants.py` (the two lists read, not restated), `test_connectors.py` (+2: p and k_β before a marker, no dowel steel and no plate row), `test_schema_tab.py` (+1: the points list and its tick; six lists), `test_polar_tab.py` and `test_schema_consumers.py` (an unticked point is not a fastener), `test_stallwatch.py` (+2: no thread in two idle seconds, a suspended watch thread logged as a system hitch).

  **Positionen.xlsx.** `material` (Fichte, Stahl) and `resistance class` → `strength_class` (C24, S355); `Wood moisture class` → `moisture class`, the old header still read. `connector.strength_class_of` is what reads a strength class, falling back to `material` only for a record without the key. `position.schema.json` documents `strength_class` (optional, 1.2). `show-position` prints «Fichte C24». `build_example_input.py` writes the new header. ⚠️ The engineer's own sheet holds `$` as T001's moisture class, which stops the export naming the cell — a fix in Excel.

  **Tests.** `tests/test_katalog_identity.py` (13) and `tests/test_stallwatch.py` (2) new; `test_schema.py` (+6: a line to the frame's edges, a line missing it, the aids drawn last, the canvas SVG without lines, band crossings and construction-line crossings as snap points), `test_schema_tab.py` (+2: the figure rendered once over four paints and again on a zoom; every line drawn across the view, shown or not), `test_export.py` (+3: the strength class's column, both moisture headers and `$` refused, a record from before the split).

  **k_β, Figur 45, points for every module, no white windows, centred ticks** — the last message of the version. *k_β*: the engineer wrote that k_β «vaut 1.34», then that it is calculated; it is — interpolated on Figur 45 between the corners of Tabellen 45/46 — and the workbook was read again: `Verbindung.xlsm` is unchanged, its zweischnittig cells were already reproduced, and `test_dowel.py` now holds its einschnittig row (M34…S34: 26.885, 76.990, k_β,1 1.335, k_β,2 1.414, k_β 1.335) and its outer-plates row (O23 68.868, P23 0.9035, at the workbook's t = C18 = 44 mm). *Figur 45*: `dowel._k_beta_table` carries a `curve` per row, `connector.figures(…, record)` draws each with `connector_svg.k_beta_curve` into `data/connectors/<id>-kbeta-<i>.svg` and turns the curves into the table's `figures`; `render-table` sets a table's `figures` under it, two abreast, named through the glossary (new terms `Seitenholz`, `Mittelholz`, `Scherfuge`, `Scherfuge A`, `Scherfuge B`: SIA 265 FR «bois latéral», «bois médian», «section cisaillée»; EN 1995 «side member», «central member», «shear plane»). Every corner and the point read are written «symbol = value unit»; no word is on the figure. *Points*: `schema.fastener_places` and `schema.default_fasteners`; `connection.fastener_places`, `connection_svg.marker_points`/`missing_markers` and `connector.marker_points`/`missing_markers` go through them; «Disposition des broches» lists the ticked points with a tooltip, «Schéma» counts them («… 0 repère(s), 4 point(s) connecteur»), `cn_no_markers` rewritten, `test_a_connector_still_takes_markers_only` replaced by its opposite. *White windows*: `connectors_tab._clear` detached shown rows, which Qt showed as top-level windows on the same turn of the event loop — six a switch, measured with an event filter; `hide()` first, and in `PolarTab.set_member_count` and `statics_tab.clear_layout` too. *Ticks*: `calc/tools/check_cell.py`, `CentredCheck`, set on the «visible» and «connecteur» columns of the «Schémas» lists. *On the way*: `statics_tab.member_properties` unpacked RELEASES as three fields where it has had four since v0.55, so selecting a member in «Modèles statiques» raised a `ValueError`. Tests: `test_dowel.py` +2, `test_connectors.py` +3, `test_schema_tab.py` +1, `test_schema_consumers.py` one replaced.
  **Save, halo, leader, and the connector's report — a later message.** *Moments polaires*' *Enregistrer* wrote nothing and kept its dot: `PolarTab.save` still read the fastener table as the typed x/z of v0.31 (a tick and a marker id since v0.74), found every row bad and raised a `KeyError` on `polar_bad_rows`, a message removed with that table — lost under pythonw. `read_table`/`table_rows` are gone, and `install_error_box` puts any exception raised in a slot into a message box (`slot_error`, three languages). `connection._table` unpacked a `None` centre (no fastener resolving, a schema gone) and stopped the connections' export with a `TypeError`; it takes build_record's own fallback. *Modèles statiques*: a label's halo is a white rectangle of its own box, turned with it, not the text stroked twice. *Schémas*: `frame["leader"]` — a piece's label dragged back onto its own piece (within a linear element's band or an area's outline) loses its leader. *Connecteurs*, report and Katalog: Tabelle 24 is laid out as `Verbindung.xlsm`'s A47:E53 — value and multiple of d under «in Faserrichtung» and «senkrecht zur Faserrichtung», the minimum length both ways — without «vorhanden», «Nachweis» or the ✔/✘; *Lage der Verbindungsmittel* left the record (`dowel.record` takes no `fasteners`); the *Verschiebungsmodul*'s K_ser column is a `value` column, so its totals row prints K_ser,Verb and not the var's name; every symbol a connector's tables, notes and warnings carry is written between dollars (`Hilfswert $k_beta$`, `$K_("ser","Verb")$`, `$k_"red"$`, `$eta_w$`, `$n_"tot"$`), glossary keys renamed with them and `connector.pattern_note` removed. `render-table` sets a title and a text cell carrying a dollar through `render-prose`, and a column named only by its group shows its unit alone. `show-connector-resistance` sets the pattern and the section side by side from level 2, each a figure with its caption (`connector.pattern_caption`, `connector.section_caption`), `width` now the pair's and 100 % by default. The Katalog's results show both tables through `record_table`, with `_math_html`/`_prose_html` setting subscripts (the warnings too). The frozen examples' glossary was refreshed alone (`examples/calc/i18n/strings.json`), the engine needing its new captions — the data snapshot was not re-frozen. Tests: `test_polar_tab` (+1), `test_schema` (+1), `test_connectors` (+3, one compiling the report and reading the PDF back); the two failures left in `test_commands` are examples still naming `SD_01-8mm`, which the engineer deleted.

  **The left panes, and a button broken for eight versions.** The error box of the paragraph above showed its first exception within the hour: *Modèles statiques*' `taken_ids()` unpacked `list_models()` as `(id, label)` when v0.69 had made it `(id, label, folder)`, so *Nouveau*, *Dupliquer* and *Enregistrer* of that tab had raised since v0.69 — fixed, and `tests/test_editor_buttons.py` clicks every list button of the five editor tabs on a copy of the project (planted back, five buttons named). Then four requests for the left panes. *Ctrl+click* lost the selection, the list being rebuilt on every load: `GroupedList.set_entries` restores it and `select` keeps it (`NoUpdate`). *Supprimer* archives: `calc/tools/source_archive.py` moves `<id>.json` into `archives/` in its own folder, `id` and `group` removed and `archived: {id, at}` noted, and `Actions` wires *Supprimer*, a drop on «Archives», a drop out of it (restore, ID from the name via `identity.slug_id`, unique) and *Supprimer définitivement…* into the five tabs (`delete` in each reduced to `self.archive.delete()`). «Archives» is the list's last row, its entries greyed, never opened, with the old ID in a tooltip. A folder stays when its last entry leaves (`_dropped` holds what the menu removed or renamed). `drawBranches` draws the tree's lines. The widget takes `lang=` and looks its `grp_` words up itself — a tab's own STRINGS carry none, so the menu was French in every language — with nine words more in three languages. Tests: `test_groups` (+5), `test_editor_buttons` (new); `test_connectors`' five form tests open the first connector that calculates instead of `SD_02`, which the engineer renamed `VU-SD-10`, and the take-back test skips where no member follows a position.

- **v0.76** (2026-09-16) — a polar moment names no connector, and its identity is Schéma · Nom · Description · ID (§3.3quinquies, §10bis, §10ter, §10quater, §11, §11ter, §13, §14)

  The engineer's decisions on the four points v0.74 reported. On the polar moment's connector: «remove the link completely». On *Grille…* living in the schemas tab, the two angles per member, and *Nouveau* opening on the first schema: kept as they are. And, relayed by another session of the same work, the polar tab's identity: the *Identité* box with the rows Schéma, Nom, Description, ID and nothing else.

  **The link.** `connection-source` 3.1 → **4.0**: no `connector`, no `variant`. A calculation has a `name` in free text, and its id is made from it by `connection.id_from_name()` — the connectors' NFKD slug with no variant after it, `Anschluss Nord` → `Anschluss_Nord`, `Élément 2` → `Element_2` — and may be written over. `compose_id`, `name_of`, `variant_of`, `link_problem`, `read_connectors` and `CONNECTORS_FILE` are gone from `connection.py`; `prepare(source_dir, schemas, positions)` no longer takes the connector ids, and `export_all.py` no longer hands them over. A 3.0 or 3.1 file is **read as 4.0** by `upgrade_source()`: its two fields dropped, its name the one it states or else its id, its id kept — so nothing is written, and `check_source` refuses a 3.x source handed to it directly, naming `upgrade_source`, and a 4.0 source still carrying either field. The project's two files were rewritten at 4.0 anyway, each named after its id. `data/connections.json` → **4.0**, its records without `connector`; `schemas/connections.schema.json` and `connection-source.schema.json` follow.

  **The report.** `linked-connector()` is deleted from `lib/calc-engine.typ`, with its two callers: `find-calculation` no longer refuses a connection of no listed connector for `show-calc` and `var-of`, and `show-polar-moment` no longer prints the connector's line under its heading. With `title: none` the citation stands beside the first line the level prints — F_max at level 1, n at level 2, the rotation centre at level 3 — and with a heading it stays beside the heading. Signature and ids unchanged, `Sections/005`'s call included. ⚠️ **One defect fixed there, from v0.74**: at level 3 a centre drawn in the schema (`center.mode` `aid`) was printed as `x_c = Σx_i/n = …`, which is not how it was found; it is printed as a fixed point now, as a manual one is.

  **Two notes that still described the frame before v0.74.** The table *Lage der Verbindungsmittel* under a polar moment said «x, z: Koordinaten ab dem Knoten (Schnittpunkt der Stabachsen), x in Faserrichtung des Bezugselements, z rechtwinklig dazu», and a connector's pattern table «x in Faserrichtung des Bezugselements, z rechtwinklig dazu, ab dem Knoten». Since v0.74 the coordinates are the schema's: its origin, x to the right, z down. Both now say so — «x, z: Koordinaten im Rahmen des Schemas, x nach rechts, z nach unten» — in `connection.py`, in `dowel._pattern_table` and in their three glossary entries, and the connector's smallest spacings read «in Faserrichtung (x)» and «rechtwinklig zur Faser (z)». ⚠️ That (x) states an assumption rather than hiding it: `dowel.pattern_spacings` takes the grain to run along x, which holds on every detail this project draws and not on one drawn at an angle; its docstring says so. Checked: the connections and connectors exported alone, both notes compiled in French and English and read back, and `test_dowel` (26), `test_connectors` (45), `test_connection` (9), `test_i18n` (6), `test_export` (9) and `test_arrows` (4) passing.

  **The reference workbook.** The `Connections` sheet names each connection's schema where it named its connector; the `Connectors` sheet's `calculations` column is `polar_moments_on_schema`, the polar moments drawn on that connector's schema.

  **The tab.** The *Identité* box holds four rows: *Schéma* (the combo and its note, moved up — the separate «Schéma» box is gone), *Nom* (free text, no validator), *Description* (was *Désignation*, the same `label`) and *ID* (monospaced, validated, made from the name until written over). *Groupe*, the connector combo and its summary, *Variante*, and the reference with its clause left the form; `group` is still set by a drop (`on_moved`) and `norm`/`norm_ref` are carried as the file has them, a missing reference falling back to the bauforumstahl handbook. *Nouveau calcul* is no longer gated on a connector: it opens on the schema of the calculation selected, else the form's, else the first, named after that schema (`next_free_name`), its members the schema's linear elements and every marker ticked. *Dupliquer* takes the next free name made from the form's. The list lost its orange `[SD_01 ?]` marker, the form its orphan notice, *Enregistrer* its refusal, and the copied calls their `show-connector`. Fourteen strings left the three languages; `polar_connectors_hint` became `polar_list_hint` and `polar_orphans_status` `polar_warnings_status`, which counts the export's schema warnings. ⚠️ **One defect fixed there, from v0.74**: *Retirer cet élément* was built with `layout_widget()`, which was never defined, so a calculation of three members or more raised a `NameError` as its form was drawn; the row is a layout handed to the form directly.

  **Tests.** `tests/test_polar_tab.py` rewritten around 4.0 (11): the id made from the name, a 3.x file read as 4.0 with its id kept, a leftover `connector` or `variant` refused, the box holding exactly Schéma · Nom · Description · ID, the folder and the reference travelling unseen, *Nouveau* ticking every marker. `test_connectors.py`'s check that a connection finds its connector became one that it names none; `test_schema_consumers.py` pairs a connection with the connectors drawn on its schema; `test_constants.py` lost the `CONNECTORS_FILE` restatement it held.

  **Checked**: the full suite, run while another session's work (strength classes, shared identity rows) was landing beside it — 437 passed, 2 skipped, 1 failed, the failure being the frozen examples' copy of `lib/` no longer matching `lib/`, which `calc/tools/build_examples.py` then regenerated (175 pages; the `show-polar-moment` ones lose their connector line) and `tests/test_commands.py` passed again (19). `export_all.py` itself could not be run end to end: `Positionen.xlsx` carries a literal `$` in T001's moisture class cell, which only the engineer can correct, so `test_export.test_the_export_is_reproducible` skipped and the connections were exported alone with `connection.export()` — schema 4.0, no warning. A Typst probe of `show-polar-moment` on both connections at levels 1, 2 and 3, with a heading and with `title: none`, and `show-calc`/`var-of` on the domain `connection`, compiled and was read page by page; so did the whole report at `--input uniform=1` and `uniform=3`.

- **v0.75** (2026-09-16) — the «Schémas» bar never scrolls up and down, an element knows its b and h, its label reads «T001 C24 100/200»; and the five plans are audited and their gaps filled (§3.3decies, §3.3nonies, §3.3quinquies, §7, §10quater, §11ter, §13, §14).

  Asked in four sentences: check that everything of the five plans was done and works, limits included; no vertical scroller in the schemas' tool bar, only a horizontal one; the label must read «T001 C24 100/200»; the schema must know the element's width and height.

  **The bar.** It sat in a bare `QScrollArea` whose vertical bar was hidden, which is not the same as absent: measured on the tab, a pane one row tall left 437 px of the bar to scroll up and down under the wheel, clipped. `statics_tab.ToolBarArea` (v0.66) is exactly the behaviour asked for, so it moved to `flowbar.py` and serves both tabs; a vertical wheel now scrolls it sideways. `tests/test_toolbar.py` runs all its checks on both tabs and holds the outcome — a vertical range of zero at every height, no group cut, 200 px and 3000 px wide — rather than the scroll bar policy. The six construction-line tools show their symbols alone: named, the *Aides* group was 950 px wide by itself, wider than the centre pane.

  **b and h.** `schema-source` 1.0 → **1.1**: an element states `b` and `h` or not; absent, a side follows the position's `dimension`, read afresh at every export («100/200» mm, cm or m, `/ x ×`); present, it is written over and stays written over, orange with *Reprendre*. The band is the side the view shows, and `width` is gone — a 1.0 file is read with its width made that side, its drawing unmoved. §7's «never parsed» has its one exception, lenient: a «180» panel has no b/h, a warning says so, nothing stops. The export (`schemas` **1.1**) carries b, h, where each came from and the drawn width. `connector.known_positions` carries `dimension`, `units` and `type`, and every caller of the drawing — the export, the connections' and connectors' figures, the three tabs — hands the positions over.

  **The label.** `schema.element_caption`: «T001 C24 100/200» by default, the element's own label where it states one. It is set on the middle of the longest stretch of the piece no other piece covers. The first draft put it on the middle of the whole length and the rendered figure showed why not: on a T-joint that is the node, under the column's label and the ⊕ of a connection's rotation centre; v0.72 to v0.74 had put it on a two-point element's far end. `data/connectors.json` and `data/connections.json` came out **unchanged to the byte**: only figures and labels moved.

  **What the audit of the five plans found**, and what was done about it — all of it, at the engineer's choice. Two were defects: a construction line with `shown` false was **invisible in the schemas tab itself** (the layout dropped it, and the canvas drew the layout), and **«Reprendre» on a connector's grade raised a KeyError** (`widgets["position"]`, gone with v0.74's member form). The rest were promised and partial: *Cadrage* (`crop`, the exported frame, a veil outside it in the canvas); *Ajouter* under every list; `shown` a check box; a row hovered lit on the drawing, and a connector's marker row lit on its pattern; a construction line selectable on the drawing, without which *Trancher* needed the lists; *Tourner* by Tab and a typed angle; a marker drawn at its stated diameter, and a connector's pattern drawing its dowels at d with «Agrandi, pas à l'échelle : Ø 1 mm» where a floor bites; `show-schema(…, highlight:)` first among the calls; outer plates showing b, t₁ and k_β only and no Stahl-Holz form showing an ignored t; *Reprendre* for the moisture class; the unresolved-position advice naming an element of the schema rather than «a position from the list» (German, French, English); the empty folders of all five editor tabs written to `settings.json` (`Catalog.save_folder_settings`) where they had lived in memory only; and *Moments polaires* with **Nom · Variante · ID** — `connection-source` **3.1**, the id composed of a name (the connector's id by default) and a variant and writable over, `check_source` and `validate_connections_business` no longer binding it to `<connector>-<variant>` — and a `GroupedList` of calculations that takes a drop, in place of its tree of connectors.

  ⚠️ **The tests no longer read the engineer's settings.** `TYPST_KATALOG_SETTINGS` points the window at another file and `tests/conftest.py` sets it for the run — `tests/.settings-<pid>.json`, seeded in English, beside `tests/.tmp/<pid>` and not inside it, since `test_harness` empties that directory mid-run and the file went with it the first time. That surfaced a language dependence nobody had seen: the position plan tab's panes leave 320 px of play in French, below `test_panes.py`'s 400 (§14).

  **Checked**: the full suite before (384 passed, 1 skipped) and after (437 passed, 1 skipped); `tests/test_schema_section.py` (24) and `tests/test_polar_tab.py` (8) new, `test_toolbar.py` rewritten for both tabs, `test_schema_tab.py` +11, `test_connectors.py` +7. Seven planted defects each caught by its guard: the label at the element's end, under the node, reduced to its number, an override lost at import, a band ignoring the view, a line not selectable on the drawing, a 1.0 file refused. The export run; `build.ps1 -AllLevels -SkipData` (the report as written and the three uniform levels) and the French report at uniform level 3 compiled; the three figures rendered and the report's polar-moment page looked at.

- **v0.74** (2026-09-16) — *Connecteurs* and *Moments polaires* consume a schema: one joint, one drawing (§3.3nonies, §3.3quinquies, §3.3decies, §10quater, §11, §13, §14).

  The **fifth and last** of the parts v0.69 named, and the half of the schemas module that gives it a reason to exist. v0.72 drew a detail and v0.73 let it be modified, but nothing read one: the two tabs that draw the same physical joint each went on carrying their own form of pieces and their own table of fastener coordinates — the duplication §2 rule 1 forbids everywhere else in this project, and the defect the whole five-plan sequence was written to remove. They name a schema now, and read the pieces and the coordinates off it.

  **The two sources lose their geometry.** `connector-source` 1.1 → **2.0** and `connection-source` 2.1 → **3.0**: the member form — `b`, `h`, `view`, `angle`, `through`, `against`, `overhang` — and the x/z table are gone, replaced by `"schema": "<id>"` and `"fasteners": ["C1", "C2", …]`, the ids of the schema's own connector markers. A member names an **element** of that schema (`"element": "E1"`), and it is through that element that it reaches its position, and so its strength class and its moisture class. `orientation` goes with them: a schema is drawn as it was drawn, so there is no page to turn. ⚠️ `reference` stays, because the report still names a Bezugselement and prints the other members' angles from it — but it no longer fixes the frame.

  **The frame needed no conversion, which was the point.** A schema has been written in the connections' own frame since v0.72 — millimetres, x right, z down, angles from x towards z — so a marker's (x, z) is already where a polar moment wants it. What *did* change is that x is the schema's rather than the reference member's, and that parts two numbers which were one by construction until v0.73: a member's record now carries **`local`**, its grain from the reference member's, which is what θ_(r,j) prints, and **`axis`**, its grain from the schema's x, which is what a force-grain angle is measured against — the force's own components being in that frame. On every detail this project draws they agree to the digit, the main piece running along x; a detail drawn at an angle parts them, and a record carrying one number for both would then be wrong for one of them. `tests/test_schema_consumers.py` holds exactly that, by turning a whole detail by 30° and asserting that `local` does not move and `axis` does.

  ⚠️ **`show-polar-moment`, `show-connector-resistance` and `show-connector` keep their signatures and their ids**, which was the plan's own condition. Two lines of `lib/calc-engine.typ` moved from `local` to `axis`, and the β_r line prints its `arctan(|F_z| / |F_x|)` decomposition only where the reference member's grain **is** x — printing it otherwise would be a formula that does not produce the number beside it. Everything else the report reads is unchanged, `figure`'s placement block included, which is why `tests/test_arrows.py` — the element-by-element comparison of the Python and the Typst arrows — needed no edit at all and is the guard this switch rested on.

  **One drawing, two layers.** `connection_svg.py` is the schema's own drawing plus what belongs to a fastener group: the numbered dots, the members' axes, the rotation centre, the x/z pair, the moment's arc, the legend and the force arrows. `schema_svg.render` was split into `render` and **`body`**, so the pieces, the aids and the dimensions are drawn by one routine wherever they are seen; `connector_svg.pattern_of` goes through the same `overlay()`, so the connector's pattern and the connection's figure are now the same detail with different layers on it rather than two drawings to keep in step. ⚠️ Doing it closed an import cycle that had been latent: `connector_svg` read `connection_svg`, which reads `connection`, which now reads `schema`, which reads `connector` for `TYPES` — and it broke on **two of five import orders**. It is acyclic now: `connector_svg` takes the schema's constants, and `connector` takes `connector_svg` and `schema` lazily, which the module already did for `dowel` and for the same kind of reason.

  **The rotation centre may be a centre drawn in the schema** — `{"mode": "aid", "aid": "A1"}`, a third mode beside the centroid and a fixed point, and what the schema's own «Centre» tool is for. One the schema no longer carries falls back to the centroid **with a warning** rather than putting the whole calculation out of reach over one renamed point.

  **What the schema does not carry warns and never stops** — the v0.33 rule, applied to a fifth and sixth editor. A schema renamed, a marker deleted, an element that no longer resolves, a centre that is gone: each is a warning naming what to do, the report refuses to print what it names, and the Katalog stays able to open the file and point it somewhere else. ⚠️ Two rules loosened to make that true, both deliberate: an **empty** fastener list is no longer a malformed source but a drawing with nothing ticked yet — `check_source` lets it through and `build_record` says `calculated: false` with its reason — and `validate_connectors_business` no longer refuses a record whose markers all failed to resolve. Refusing either would have made *Nouveau* open on an error, which v0.70 fixed, or stopped an export over a drawing only the Katalog can repair.

  **The Katalog.** *Connecteurs* gains a **Schéma** row in *Identité* with a line saying what that schema holds; its «Position» combo becomes a combo of the schema's **elements**, and choosing one lights that piece on the drawing beside the form — the very `highlight` argument `show-schema` takes, so the window and the report mark the same thing. *Disposition des broches* becomes the list of the schema's markers with a tick against the ones this connector carries, their coordinates shown and read-only. *Moments polaires* loses its member form and its coordinate table entirely: it names a schema, its members name elements, its fasteners are ticked markers, and its «Calcul» box is **Identité** — with `group`, the folder field the other three tabs got in v0.69 and this one did not. ⚠️ *Nouveau* opens on the first schema there is, with every marker ticked and the first elements named, so v0.70's rule — «Nouveau» must open on something that calculates, not on a refusal — stays true; where the project has no schema at all it says so, which is honest, the detail being drawn first now.

  ⚠️ **«Grille…» moved to the «Schémas» tab**, where the markers are placed, rather than staying in the tab that consumes them. The plan asks that it write points *into the schema*; having a second tab write `details/<id>.json` would let it overwrite an unsaved draft of that very schema in the tab that owns it, which is the one failure this project treats as worst. It is a **button and not a tool** in the *Connecteur* group — it places a whole group at once and has nothing on the grid to point at, exactly as *Trancher* and *Fusionner* do — and it places markers of the kind the tool bar has chosen.

  **The migration ran once.** `calc/tools/migrate_to_schemas.py` lays each old source out with `connection_svg.layout()` — the very code that drew it — in the connection's own frame, and writes each member out as a `linear` element whose points are the centre line of the band that was drawn for it. ⚠️ **A connection reuses its connector's schema where the two draw the same joint**: the plan says one schema per source, and writing two identical ones for `SD_01` and `SD_01-8mm` would have recreated, in the new folder, exactly the duplication this refactor removes. On this project that gives **two** schemas for four sources. It also repaired something: `SD_01-8mm` named position `D001` for its second member — a position `Positionen.xlsx` has not carried since v0.59, and the standing warning §3.3nonies describes — and now names `S001`, through the schema its connector already drew correctly.

  ⚠️ **What the figure lost, and it is a real loss.** A member's label read `T001 100/400`, the section drawn set bold; it reads `T001` now, because a schema element carries the width it is *drawn* at and not the other dimension. The section is still in the report's own text (`show-connector` prints it) and the drawing is at its real size, so nothing is unmeasurable — but a checker reading the figure alone no longer sees the section on it. What the schema's own label should carry is an open question (§14), not something to invent here.

  **Checked**: `tests/test_schema_consumers.py` is new and holds the seam — that both calculations read one set of coordinates, that moving a marker moves it in both, that a member reaches its position through its element and an override still says what the position holds, that `local` and `axis` are the two different numbers they are meant to be, that every missing thing warns rather than stops, that a source still carrying its own drawing is sent to the migration by name, and that nothing in this project points at a schema that is not there. `tests/test_connectors.py` was rewritten against the new shape. `tests/test_constants.py` gained the one constant the acyclic import graph forced to be restated, `connector.ENTITY_ID`. **384 pass, 1 skipped**; the export runs with only the warnings it had before; the report compiles at all three uniform levels; and the 175 frozen examples were re-frozen — the snapshot had to move, `data/connections.json` having changed shape, which is what `--freeze` is for.

- **v0.73** (2026-09-16) — a schema can be modified: move, duplicate, turn, mirror, split and merge (§3.3decies, §10quater, §11ter, §13, §14).

  The fourth of the five parts v0.69 named, and the second half of the schemas module. v0.72 drew a detail and could not change one: a piece put down in the wrong place was deleted and drawn again, and a beam running past a column could not be made into the two pieces the position plan has allowed to share a number since v0.44.

  **Six verbs, in one group.** Four take **two clicks** — *déplacer* and *dupliquer* (from where, to where), *tourner* (the centre, then the direction) and *symétrie* (the two points of the axis) — and go through `place()` like every tool this canvas already had, so Shift's constraint, the snapping and the tracking rays apply to them without a line of it being written twice, and **Tab + a distance + Enter** works for the same reason. ⚠️ *Trancher* and *fusionner* are **buttons and not tools**: they act on what is already selected, so there is nothing on the grid for them to point at and nothing for the exclusive tool group to hold checked.

  **The multiple selection is the genuinely new interaction**, and the one piece of this that existed nowhere in the project: neither `plan_canvas` nor `statics_canvas` nor v0.72's own `schema_canvas` has one. Ctrl toggles on the drawing, the five lists take Ctrl and Shift the way the left pane's folders do (v0.69), and the two are kept in phase. ⚠️ The selection is **a focused entry and the rest**, not a flat list — the flat list is the tidier structure and the wrong model, since the properties panel shows one entry's width, one list row scrolls itself into view and a drag grabs one thing, so all three would have had to invent a "first" anyway. A Windows explorer selection has a focused item for the same reason. ⚠️ **A table holds one collection**, so a selection of an element *and* the construction line it is to be cut on cannot be shown in one list at all: each list marks its own share and the tab holding the focused entry comes to the front. That is the tabbed layout's limit, not a state the two got into.

  **A cut piece keeps its position number** — the engineer's own rule, and legal downstream rather than merely convenient: the position plan has allowed identical elements to share a number since v0.44. It keeps its width, its view and its label besides, a cut changing where a piece ends and not what it is, and **the first piece keeps the element's own id**, so a dimension anchored to it still resolves. **Only elements of the same number merge**, a mismatch refusing **naming both numbers**.

  **Split and merge are an exact pair**, which is the property the tests hold rather than the six verbs' arithmetic one at a time: cutting a piece and joining it again gives back the shape it started from — collinear vertex and all, the vertex the cut left behind being taken out again, without which a merge gives the right shape carrying two vertices nobody drew. A linear element is cut at **every** crossing, so a polyline cut twice gives three pieces; an area is cut where the line **enters once and leaves once**, and anything else is refused **naming the count**.

  **Four quarter-turns are the identity, to the digit**, and so is a mirror applied twice. What makes it exact is that `round_place` is the rounding a click already applies and both sides go through it; a rotation rounding its own way would walk a schema off its grid over an afternoon's editing. ⚠️ A **direction under a mirror is folded about the axis** (2·axis − angle) rather than turned by a constant, which is why `reflection` returns a function and not a number: mirroring a vertical line across a vertical axis has to leave it vertical.

  ⚠️ **A modification is one step of *Annuler*, and a refusal is none.** The first is obvious once stated and was not free — six entries moved in six brackets would be six presses to get back. The second is the one worth recording: `begin_change()` is what *pushes* a step, so refusing after it leaves an undo that undoes nothing, and a cut that misses is the common case here rather than the rare one. *Trancher* and *fusionner* are therefore tried on a **copy** first.

  ⚠️ **`duplicate` does not copy a dimension**, even one in the selection: a dimension is anchored, and a copy would either measure the original from the copy's place or name nothing at all. The copies **become the selection**, so a second duplicate copies the copy — which is what laying out a row of identical pieces is.

  **Every refusal carries a code.** `schema.Refused` holds both halves on purpose: a sentence that stands on its own in English, for a caller with no window and for a test that asserts what went wrong, and a `code`/`detail` pair the Katalog turns into its own three languages — so the French window says «Seuls des éléments de même numéro de position fusionnent : ceux-ci portent T001, S001» while the model's own sentence is what a test reads. Neither is derived from the other; parsing the sentence back into a code is the fragility the code exists to avoid.

  Two things written down because they were got wrong first. An **L** is not a shape that defeats an area cut — whichever way a straight line is laid across an L it enters once and leaves once, so an L splits perfectly well, and it takes a **U** to cross an outline four times. And `tests/test_schema_tab.py`'s own swatch guard **restated** the mapping from a tool to its symbol; the restatement broke the moment a tool arrived whose spec carries a `verb` and no `kind` — a guard failing because the thing it guards grew correctly. The mapping is one named function now, `schema_tab.swatch_kind`, and the test asks it.

  ⚠️ The **polyline a split produces** is the shape §3.3decies said only a split would produce, and until v0.73 nothing had ever drawn one: the canvas draws, hit-tests and cuts one now.

- **v0.72** (2026-09-15) — the «Schémas» module: one drawing of a detail, drawn once (§3.3quinquies, §10bis, §10quater, §11, §11ter, §13, §14).

  The third of the five parts v0.69 named, and the first half of the schemas module itself: **drawing** one. *Moments polaires* and *Connecteurs* each carry their own form of pieces and their own table of fasteners, for the same physical geometry — the duplication the rule «one source of truth» forbids everywhere else in this project — and neither form can draw what a connection detail actually needs: construction lines, centres, dimensions, areas. A schema is that drawing, and nothing else yet consumes it: what makes the two tabs name a schema instead of carrying geometry is the fifth part, not this one.

  **The source.** `000-Python/details/<id>.json`, `schemas/schema-source.schema.json` 1.0, written by the Katalog and read by `calc/domains/schema.py` alone — the arrangement `connections/`, `plans/` and `statics/` already have (§13). The frame is the connections' (§3.3quinquies): **millimetres**, x right, z down, angles from +x towards +z, so a connector or a polar moment will take a copy of a schema's geometry with no conversion at all. Four collections and no more: `elements` (a piece, `linear` seen edge-on or `area` seen face-on), `connectors` (fastener markers, their kind being `connector.TYPES`' own), `aids` (construction points and lines) and `dimensions` (statics.py's own four kinds, taken as they stand).

  ⚠️ **The folder is `details/`, not `schemas/`** — the one place the plan this was built from had to be departed from. `000-Python/schemas/` has held every `*.schema.json` contract since v0.1, `schema.py`'s own two included, and `source_paths()` globs `*.json` without asking what kind of file it found: pointed at that folder it read `calculations.schema.json` as a detail and refused it for having no `schema_version` — the first export after the first real example was written. `details/` is the plan's own word for what this draws («un schéma est un détail à l'échelle réelle»), so nothing else had to be renamed: the module, `show-schema`, `data/schemas.json` and the two contracts keep their names. `tests/test_schema.py` holds it from both sides.

  ⚠️ **A named point is an aid, not a fifth collection.** A point worth naming — a centre, a reference point — is drawn as an aid of kind `centre` or `point`, and a dimension's `{"point": id}` anchor names one; there is deliberately no flat `points` collection beside the four. ⚠️ **A construction line is not exported unless it is asked for**: a point or a centre carries `shown: true` by default, a line `false`, and a line marked shown is drawn more heavily so it reads as deliberate rather than as clutter left behind.

  **The drawing.** `calc/domains/schema_svg.py`, one SVG per language under `data/schemas/`, kept to SVG Tiny 1.2 like every other figure here. ⚠️ A schema is a **detail at its real size**: its coordinates *are* millimetres, scaled to `PAPER_MM` only when the SVG's width and height are set — `connector_svg.section()`'s own arrangement — and **not** renormalised to 1000 figure units the way a static model's scale-free sketch is. That is what will let the report's force arrows land on this drawing later without a conversion between them.

  **The tab.** `calc/tools/schema_tab.py` and `calc/tools/schema_canvas.py`, ported from `statics_tab`/`statics_canvas`: the grouped list on the left, the tool bar / canvas / lists as three panes to drag in the middle, the properties on the right, a grid that follows the zoom (1000 / 100 / 10 / 1 mm), AutoCAD-style tracking, snapping, draggable labels, and everything enterable twice over — by clicking on the grid or by typing into one of the five lists. Six tool groups: Navigation, Historique, Éléments, Connecteur, Aides, Cotation. *Modification* — move, duplicate, rotate, mirror, split, merge — is the fourth part, not this one.

  ⚠️ **The three ways to draw a rectangle are two constructions, not three.** Two diagonal clicks are their own shape; three clicks and «one click + Tab» both end as an edge and a signed width off it, so both call `rectangle_from_edge` and land on the same box to the digit — which a test holds, because the engineer would otherwise see the rectangle jump when switching mode.

  **In Typst.** `show-schema(schema, width:, highlight:, aids:, caption:)` and `schema-ids()` in `lib/calc-engine.typ`, with the halo `show-plan` and `show-static-model` already insert at their own marker, and `aids: false` stripping the construction aids at a marker of their own. `build_reference.py` gains `call_show_schema`, `call_schema_loop`, a `Schemas` sheet and the two commands in its categories.

  ⚠️ What this does **not** do: nothing computes. A schema is a drawing, like a static model — no resistance, no polar moment, no check anywhere behind it — and it carries no `{vars, steps, checks}` record, so nothing addresses one with `show-calc`. An element's `position` is a reference resolved afresh at every export, and one that no longer resolves is a **warning** (§13's v0.33 rule, applied to a fifth editor).

  `tests/test_schema.py` (26) holds the source's own checks, the three rectangle modes, an aid line absent from the export by default, a label's leader appearing exactly when it leaves its element, and the drawing being identical when nothing moved; `tests/test_schema_tab.py` (15) builds the tab off-screen and draws through the canvas's own `place()`, so that **a schema the tab can draw is one the export accepts**. What no test reaches is the pointer itself — that is the engineer's own walk through the window.

- **v0.71** (2026-09-15) — every export is archived (§11, §12, §13).

  `Statischer Rapport.pdf` was overwritten at the same place on every export, and its own state — `data/`, the connector/connection/plan/static-model sources the Katalog writes, the code of `calc/`/`lib/` at that moment — was only ever findable again by knowing which Git commit produced it, which several exports in a row (a level tried, a typo fixed) need not share at all.

  **One script, called identically by all three routes.** `calc/tools/archive_export.py` derives its paths from its own location the way every module of `calc/` does (§11), needs no argument to find the PDF, and on every successful `typst compile` copies it flat as `000-Archives/<timestamp>-Statischer Rapport.pdf` and a full snapshot of `000-TypstRapport/` as `000-Archives/<timestamp>/` — everything the Katalog needs to be reopened on exactly that state. `build.ps1` and the Makefile's `report` target call it as a subprocess (wrapped in `try`/`catch`, and with a leading `-`, respectively); the Katalog's *Exporter PDF* calls its `run()` directly from `compile_done()`'s success branch, so a failure reaches the status line rather than a console it does not have when launched from `Typst-Katalog.bat`. The timestamp is `AAMMJJ-HHhMM`; a second export inside the same clock minute is told apart with `-2`, `-3`, ..., never silently overwritten.

  **What is excluded, and why.** The input Excel workbooks need no filter of their own — `Positionen.xlsx` and the Belastungsannahmen workbooks live at the project root, one level above `000-TypstRapport/`, so copying only that subtree already leaves them out. `000-ExternalFiles/` (the norm library, ~900 MB, never read by any module of `calc/`, never changes — §11bis) is excluded by path, and so are `000-Python/examples/` and `000-Python/out/` (documentation cache and AllLevels build output, neither specific to one export) and the usual build/cache debris already kept out of Git. `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` is deliberately **not** swept out by an extension filter — it is generated, not an Excel *input*, and belongs in the snapshot.

  ⚠️ Archiving is a convenience and never part of the validated pipeline (§3.6): a failure — a full disk, a slow sync — warns and never fails a build that has already succeeded. ⚠️ `000-Archives/` is excluded from Git (`.gitignore`): a fast local index by timestamp, not a second traceability record beside the one Git already keeps, and it grows without bound — nothing here prunes an old archive.

  Each archived folder also carries `archive-info.json`: the timestamp, which route wrote it, and the exact `--input` flags the compile was given, forwarded verbatim from the same call each route already builds for `typst compile` itself rather than reconstructed — otherwise a PDF's own name would never say which level or language it was compiled at.

  `tests/test_archive.py` (3) holds the exclusion rules (by location, never by extension), the collision policy, and that a fresh checkout with no PDF yet is a quiet no-op rather than a warning.

- **v0.70** (2026-09-15) — the Connecteurs tab is laid out on the classeur, and reads in three languages (§3.3nonies, §10quater, §11, §11ter, §13).

  v0.68 got the calculation right and left the form around it wrong. The engineer listed what was wrong, and every item of the list is here.

  **The boxes are his own, in the classeur's order**: Identité · Assemblage · Géométrie et matériau · Stabdübel · Disposition des broches. «Géométrie», which held steel plates, is gone; the plate fields sit inside «Géométrie et matériau» where the classeur has them, beside the b and the t₁/t₂ they feed. The middle two boxes are rebuilt per assembly, because what they hold depends on it: a Holz-Holz joint enters two timber members **side by side**, as `'Stabdübel (Holz-Holz)'` does in its columns C and D, and a Stahl-Holz joint one member and its slots.

  **Every row reads `sentence | symbol [entry] unit`.** The symbol sits immediately left of the entry — a symbol written after the sentence is read as part of the sentence, and the eye cannot find it while typing; b and h in *Moments polaires* were already laid out that way. The sentences are the classeur's, literally: «Anzahl der in Faserrichtung des Holzes hintereinander angeordneten Verbindungsmittel», not «Broches en file». **Every row carries a unit**, «–» where the quantity has none, so a missing unit is a decision and not an oversight. The formula cells of the classeur are read-only cells here, filled by the calculation: t₁, t₂, k_β, k_β,₂, p·k_β, ρ_k, Holzart, **n_quer** (new: `ROUNDUP(n_tot / n)`, `C31`/`C29`) and M_u,k.

  **The results are rich text**, so `R_d` is R with a real subscript, and the **Minimale Abstände** table is under them — equation (88) is conditional on those spacings (6.2.1.1), and until now they were in the report and nowhere on screen. **Hovering a box lights the results it produces**, the correspondence read off the classeur's structure, where a formula cell closes a run of manual entries.

  **One moisture class for the connection, not one per member** (schema 1.1). SIA 265 applies one η_w to R_d,Verb and the classeur states one per sheet; one per member had left the calculation to decide which governed, and it took the worst — a rule the norm states nowhere. A 1.0 source is upgraded as it is read, the worst of its classes becoming the connection's, which is the value it was already using.

  **A value taken from a position and written over stays written over.** `material` takes a third shape, `{"position": …, "grade": …}`: the position is kept, the grade wins, and *Importer Excel* does not undo it. The line under the field says in grey where a value came from and **in orange** that it has been overridden, naming what the position holds, with *Reprendre* beside it. ⚠️ That line is composed by the window from structured fields, not translated: the two glossary templates that tried to carry it swallowed each other's text, a hole at the end of a template matching anything (§4bis's own warning).

  **The steel is two fields, out of a new `calc/norms/sia263/`** — the plate's grade (Tabelle 1) and the fastener's (Tabelle 1 or Tabelle 2's bolt classes), which fills f_u,k without locking it. Both tables are graphics in the PDF and were read off a render of pages 18 and 19. The norm gives **S460**; `Verbindung.xlsm`'s `Daten` sheet does not — which is §2 rule 13 in one line. ⚠️ Tabelle 3 is deliberately not transcribed: its «Stifte» are the pins of fastenings and supports, by its own Anwendungsbereich column.

  **`plates.thickness` is removed.** Nothing ever read it; it belongs to the plate's Lochleibung check, which is not built. The section figure draws the plate at the Schlitzbreite instead — the classeur's own `t`, and the one thickness the source carries.

  **The section figure is repaired**, all three defects the engineer named: the pieces step up and down as the classeur draws them instead of standing in one bar, the dowel is bounded to the timber instead of sticking out of both ends, and its drawn thickness **follows its diameter** at the widths' own scale. ⚠️ Only the width is to scale — the height of a piece is the member's depth, which a section does not model — so the diameter is held between a floor and a share of the piece, and the figure says «Ø vergrössert / verkleinert, nicht massstäblich» when a bound bites.

  **The glossary gained ~100 terms and ~12 templates** for this domain, the four assembly names among them: a French window said «Stahl-Holz, Schlitzbleche» because none of the connector's words had ever been put in. The French and the English are quoted from SIA 265:2021 wherever it gives them — above all **annexe G, «Index des termes» (tableau 51, pp. 110-111)**, which is the trilingual index: Broche / Dowel-type fastener, Résistance à la pression latérale / Embedment strength, Densité / Bulk density. The four configurations are tableaux 45 and 46's own French («Bois – bois à double section»), and «tôles latérales» is p. 97's. What no edition in the library gives is a translation and is marked as such.

  Two defects of the window found and fixed while building it, both the kind that only show when a box is rebuilt: a `QLabel` Qt had deleted was still a key in the map of read-only cells, so the next refresh raised «Internal C++ object already deleted»; and a rebuilt widget was `deleteLater()`'d without being detached, so the old rows went on painting under the new ones until the next turn of the event loop and the box was drawn twice over. A third, in the harness: a tab left dirty makes `new()` open a modal, and a modal off-screen never returns — the test run hung with no output at all.

  `connectors/SD_01.json` and `SD_02.json` are repaired: they were migration artefacts, carrying `elements: ["test"]`, a `ratio` of 0.05 and a member drawn from a position `Positionen.xlsx` has not had since v0.59.

- **v0.69** (2026-09-15) — the wheel scrolls, and the left panes have folders (§10quater, §11, §11ter, §13).

  Two things the engineer met every day in the Katalog, and the first of five parts of a larger redesign (the «Schémas» module and what consumes it are the other four).

  **The wheel never changes a value.** Qt turns a wheel into a value change on a `QAbstractSpinBox`, a `QComboBox` and a `QSlider` whether or not the widget has the focus. In a window that is mostly forms inside scroll areas — which every editor tab is — a wheel meant to scroll the properties panel silently retyped whichever field happened to be under the pointer: an angle five degrees more, a strength class one down, and nothing anywhere to say so. `calc/tools/nowheel.py` is one event filter on the **application**, installed in `main()` before any window exists, so it reaches the 28 spin boxes, the 25 combo boxes and the style sliders of `catalog_gui.py`, `connectors_tab.py`, `plan_tab.py` and `statics_tab.py` — and the spin boxes a delegate builds on the fly for a table cell, which are written down nowhere, and every widget added later. ⚠️ The event is eaten **and re-sent to the viewport of the enclosing `QAbstractScrollArea`**: eating it alone would leave the wheel doing nothing at all over a field, so a panel could not be scrolled by pointing at the middle of it, which is worse than the defect. ⚠️ A `QScrollBar` **is** a `QAbstractSlider`, and is the one exclusion — the naive rule gets exactly that case wrong and would disable the scrolling the guard exists to protect. The canvases are untouched: `plan_canvas` and `statics_canvas` handle the wheel themselves, to zoom about the pointer.

  Measured, since a guard that has never fired is a guard nobody has tested: without the filter a `QDoubleSpinBox` holding 30.0 comes out at 29.0 on one notch; with it, 30.0.

  **The left pane sorts into folders.** `calc/tools/grouped_list.py` is the list the editor tabs share: folder rows and entry rows, drag and drop, the **Ctrl/Shift selection of the Windows explorer**, and *Nouveau dossier · Renommer · Supprimer le dossier* on the right button. ⚠️ **A folder is a field, not a directory.** Each source carries a `group` string — the «Groupe» the engineer's own *Identité* box already names — so nothing moves on disk, the ids stay flat, the rule that *the id is the file name* is untouched, and no `show-*` call changes: a folder renamed cannot break a report. The field is optional and additive, absence meaning the root, so **no `schema_version` was bumped** and no file needed migrating; `domains/connector.py` carries it into `data/connectors.json` besides. ⚠️ A folder holding nothing has nowhere to live in the project — folders are *derived* from the values in use — so the set of them is kept per tab in `settings.json`, where a view preference belongs, and does not travel with the repository. A drop is not Qt's own move: the widget computes the target, emits `moved`, and the tab writes the field, exports and rebuilds, so a drop whose export fails leaves nothing half-moved. Removing a folder never removes what is in it.

  ⚠️ *Moments polaires* **shows** the folders its connectors are filed in and takes no drop yet: its left pane is a tree of connectors with their calculations beneath, which the schemas module replaces, and building the drag into it twice would be work thrown away.

  `tests/test_wheel.py` (5) and `tests/test_groups.py` (10) hold both, the second written on the decision rather than on the widget: every source schema documents `group` and **none requires it**, a multiple selection is carried whole by a move, and removing a folder leaves every id unchanged.

- **v0.68** (2026-09-15) — the Katalog calculates a connector, and the connectors leave Excel (§3.3nonies, §7bis, §10bis, §10quater, §11, §11ter, §13, §14).

  The *Connecteurs* tab had said «implémentation future» since v0.28, and the resistances of this project's connections were computed in `Verbindung.xlsm` — a classeur the engineer judges correct and finds needlessly hard to read and to use. This version replaces it for the first kind of fastener, the **Stabdübel**, and settles the five decisions underneath.

  **The calculation.** `calc/domains/dowel.py`, SIA 265:2021 6.2.1, equation (88) with `M_u,k` (87), `f_h,k` (Tabelle 22), `k_red` (86) and **k_β from Anhang A, Tabellen 45 and 46** — the more accurate bilinear form 6.2.1.1 offers as an alternative to the simplified Tabelle 23, and the one the engineer chose. Also `K_ser` (Tabelle 25), the minimum spacings and the connection's minimum length (Tabelle 24), η_w (Tabelle 4), η_t (2.2.6) and the truss reduction (5.3.6.2.2) as a second answer beside R_d,Verb rather than a factor folded into it. Four configurations, exactly the four the norm gives a row for; the others are refused **naming 6.1.1.11**.

  **The norm's own values move into `calc/norms/sia265/`** — seven files, one per table, plus `clauses.json` for the scalars the norm states under a symbol of their own (`k_α = 0,73`, the 0,75 of a truss node). Tabellen 1, 4, 8, 9, 24 and 25, each transcribed from `SIA265-2021-DE.pdf` at a page that can be turned to, each checked before anything is built from it. The line the package draws, stated in its docstring: *a number the norm gives a symbol and a value to lives here; a coefficient that only exists inside a formula stays beside that formula.* The two-column formula tables do not extract as text and were **read as images**, rendered through QtPdf.

  **The connectors leave `Positionen.xlsx`.** `connectors/<id>.json`, written by the tab and read by `domains/connector.py`, on the arrangement of `connections/`, `plans/` and `statics/`. This reverses v0.33, whose argument — a connector is a position on the plan, so it belongs where positions are entered — was right and is outweighed by a fact it could not have known: a resistance needs thicknesses, grain angles, predrilling and fastener coordinates, none of which is a column. `data/connectors.json` goes to schema **2.0** and keeps every one of its ten descriptive keys, so `show-connector`, `connector-ids()` and `build_reference`'s Connectors sheet did not change a line. `calc/connectors.py` is deleted and `calc/tools/migrate_connectors.py` wrote `SD_01` and `SD_02` out once, taking their geometry from the polar-moment calculation that names each.

  **A member's timber is a reference, resolved afresh at every export** — the static model's magnitude pattern applied to a position. The engineer asked for it in those words: *«c'est le bouton Importer Excel déjà disponible»*. A reference that no longer resolves warns and never stops, and so does a kind with no module, a calculation the norm has no answer for, and a dowel pattern below Tabelle 24 — each setting `calculated: false` with the reason the report refuses with.

  **The tab.** `calc/tools/connectors_tab.py`: the connectors on the left, a **foldable bar of the eight kinds of fastener** drawn with the figures' own symbols in the middle — only *Broche* live, the rest disabled with the reason in their tooltip — the form under it, and on the right the two figures, the results, the warnings **in yellow inside the pane and never as a pop-up**, and the calls to copy. **Two figures**: the fastener pattern, which is `connection_svg`'s own drawing called with `legend=False, show_center=False`, and a new **section through the joint**, `connector_svg.section()`, its pieces proportional to the real thicknesses and its dimensions written as `t₁`, `t₂`, `Ø d` until they are known. `show-connector-resistance` prints it all at the three levels; level 3 delegates to `show-calc("connector", id)`, so the detail cannot drift from the data.

  **Three places the classeur differs from the norm**, all settled for the norm and all held by tests: the `t₂,ᵢ` bracket of Tabelle 45 eq. (125), invisible at β_f = 1 and 9 % out on C24 against D30; `k_red` at n = 1, which Tabelle 21 puts at 1,0 and eq. (86) alone at 0,915; and the validity conditions of Tabelle 46 eq. (130)/(131), which nothing in the classeur checks. Both of its sheets are otherwise reproduced **to the last printed digit**, and its transcription of Tabellen 8 and 9 agrees with the new one on **89 of 90 values** — the exception being D30's bracketed `f_c,90,d`, which the norm does not give.

  ⚠️ **A latent defect of the engine, found and fixed at the source.** `substituted-rhs` replaced each operand's symbol by plain string replacement, so a var whose symbol is `d` — the norm's own symbol for a diameter — rewrote the `d` of every `dot` in the formula and `0.3 dot f_(u,k) dot d^2.6` came out as `0.3 12ot …`, which does not evaluate. Longest-first does not help, `dot` not being an operand. A symbol made only of word characters is now replaced on word boundaries; one carrying anything else keeps the plain replace. All 167 frozen example pages were regenerated and **not one changed**, which is what says the fix altered nothing that was already right.

  **Tests**: `test_norms.py` (32), `test_dowel.py` (26) and `test_connectors.py` (22), plus six lines in `test_constants.py`. `test_dowel` holds both sheets cell by cell and **all 28 published cells of Tabelle 21**, a free oracle inside the norm itself; `test_norms` holds the transcription against the classeur's independent one. The suite is 239 passing, 1 skipped.

  ⚠️ Left undone from the agreed scope: the **steel plate's Lochleibung**. It was blocked on SIA 263, which was not in the library when the plan was approved; the engineer added `SIA263-2013-DE/FR` and `SIA263-1-2020` while this was being built, so it is now unblocked rather than open (§14).

- **v0.67** (2026-09-15) — the position plan's panes can be dragged, because its tool bar no longer sets the width of the tab (§10quater, §11ter, §13).

  The engineer reported that the left and right panels of the plan tab could not be resized by dragging, and that the display jumped. Measured off the window, off screen, the cause was one number: the centre pane's **minimum width was 892 px**. The bar was one `QGridLayout` of two rows, its minimum the sum of the columns' maxima — 880 px with the default font, **1098 px with the engineer's own** — and the pane handed that to the splitter. Beside 258 px on the left (its three buttons) and 202 on the right (its two), that left **108 px of play in a 1468 px splitter**: a handle either refused to move or took its width from the pane on the far side, which is the jump.

  | pane | minimum before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | plans and elements | 258 px | 258 px — its own buttons |
  | the plan | **892 px** | **114 px** — the widest single button |
  | properties | 202 px | 202 px — its own buttons |

  **The bar is two rows that wrap.** Each row is a `FlowLayout` of its own, so a row too wide for its pane takes a second line and no tool is ever cut off. Two things came with it, neither cosmetic:
  - the two rows sharing one grid's columns is what had been **eliding the labels** — the bar was being drawn below its own minimum, so *Axe vertical* read *Axe ...ical* and *Accrocher aux axes* read *Accroc...x axes*. They are set in full now.
  - a **hidden** item takes no place. Qt's own flow-layout example lays every item out, hidden or not, which would leave a hole where the variant combo sits while the tool chosen has no variants.

  **`calc/tools/flowbar.py` is new, and nothing was written twice for it.** `FlowLayout` and `FlowWidget` moved out of `statics_tab.py`, which imports them from there now; `ToolBarArea` stays in that tab, being its own (its bar scrolls sideways because its height is draggable, §10quater). `statics_tab.py` lost the four Qt names the moved classes used.

  ⚠️ **Scrolling sideways was written first and rejected on sight.** It is v0.66's answer for the static models' bar, and it is wrong here: that bar's height is the engineer's to drag and this one's is not, so at the window's own default width six controls sat off the right edge — M, L, the variant combo, *Annuler*, *Rétablir* and *Ajuster*. Wrapping costs the plan about 26 px a row and loses nothing.

  ⚠️ **The side panes still stop at the width of their own buttons**, 258 px and 240 px. That floor is their contents' and not the bar's; a narrower list would mean the three buttons under it wrapping as well, which nobody has asked for.

  New `tests/test_panes.py`, 6 checks: three panes, not collapsible; the play between the minima; the bar's own width against the pane's minimum; **a drag moves its own two panes and not the third**; the bar wrapping with every tool inside its row; and the hidden combo leaving no hole. Verified against the defect as the engineer described it — with the centre pane's minimum forced back to 892 px, `test_a_drag_moves_its_own_two_panes_and_not_the_third` fails with `the pane on the far side moved: [258, 892, 310] -> [396, 892, 172]`, and the play test with `138 px of play`. The whole suite passes, 160 checks with the 6 new ones.

  ⚠️ Found while running it, and left for the session that owns it: `test_toolbar.test_a_shorter_pane_gives_fewer_rows_and_scrolls_sideways` (v0.66) fails when the off-screen run is given the machine's real fonts (`QT_QPA_FONTDIR`), where a five-row pane and a three-row pane both come out at three rows. It passes with Qt's own font, which is what the suite uses.

- **v0.66** (2026-09-15) — the static models tab gives its height back to the drawing (§10quater, §11ter, §13).

  The engineer drew a 0.5 m bench and sent a screenshot with two panels circled: **the tool bar took four rows above a drawing area 400 px tall**, and the lists took a third of what was left. Neither could be resized, so the drawing got what those two did not want.

  The centre column is a **vertical splitter** now — the bar, the drawing, the lists — and the three heights travel in `view_state()` with everything else. The lists needed a floor of their own before that was any use: a `QTableWidget` asks for its header and several rows, which would have stopped the splitter at 252 px, and the floor here is a tab bar, a header, one row and the two buttons, 112 px.

  **The bar is the half that needed thought, and it reverses a settled decision.** v0.51 chose wrapping over scrolling and §10quater said so in those words. What the engineer asked for is the opposite, and the reading that makes it coherent is that *a short bar must not be a clipped bar*: given room it wraps exactly as before, and dragged shorter it lays its groups into the rows that still fit, grows to the right, and scrolls left and right.

  | pane | bar | rows | scrolls |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 439 px — as it opened | 640 × 350 | 4 | no |
  | 273 px | 1089 × 172 | 2 | yes, 449 px more to the right |
  | 107 px — dragged flat | 2154 × 83 | **1** | yes, 1514 px |

  ⚠️ **The vertical scroll bar is off, and that is the rule the whole thing rests on.** Reaching a wrapped row by scrolling down is the obvious alternative and it is the failure this exists to prevent, wearing a control: a tool bar scrolled up and down hides a group silently, where a horizontal bar is visibly saying there is more to the right. Off, the only way the bar can fail to show a group is by being too narrow — and then the bar that says so is on the screen.

  ⚠️ **The layout is now computed twice**, once by `_lay` to draw and once by `wrap_width` to size, and that is exactly where this version's own defect lived. `wrap_width(1)` returned the sum of the groups' widths; `_lay` breaks on `x + w > right`, where `right` is the **last pixel** rather than the width, so one row needs `total + 1`. The bar came out **one pixel short**, was drawn in two rows inside a pane holding one, and the second row — four tools — was clipped away with nothing to say so. `_rows_at` is written to break on the same pixel `_lay` breaks on, and the stop is past `total` rather than at it.

  New `tests/test_toolbar.py`, 7 checks, measured off the **drawn geometry** rather than off the layout's own arithmetic, that arithmetic being the thing under test: three draggable panes; a tall bar wraps and does not scroll; a shorter one uses fewer rows, grows wider and scrolls; one row is one row; no tool lies outside the bar at any of four heights; the vertical bar is off and the pane cannot go below a whole row; the lists go below what they ask for. ⚠️ It takes the bar **out of its splitter** to test it — a widget a layout manages ignores `resize()`, so the first run of this measured the same height three times and passed while proving nothing.

  **154 passed, 0 failed.**

- **v0.65.1** (2026-09-14) — an example may not cite something the project has dropped (§10sexies, §11ter).

  v0.65 froze the worked examples against a snapshot of `data/`, and said of the snapshot that it "is designed to age but not to be born stale". That is a state of mind, not a check — and it was tested the same hour: the engineer consolidated the load workbooks while v0.65 was being written, **eight instances vanished**, and two fences were left citing `EL+AL-Fassade`. It was caught by hand, minutes before the commit, by looking rather than by anything saying so.

  👉 **The check is narrower than the obvious one, and that is the point.** Comparing the snapshot's ids against `data/` is easy to compute and is the wrong property: the snapshot is *meant* to drift, so that check would fire when nothing is wrong. A test that goes off on correct code is worse than no test — the same false positive we had already declined an hour earlier when widening an import scan over generated files was proposed. What actually hurts is a **dangling reference**: an example naming an id the project no longer has, so the reader studies a page built on something that is gone while the two copy buttons beside it name something else.

  `build_examples.dangling()` reports them, the generator prints them as warnings, and `tests/test_commands.py` fails on them. The check is itself verified against a **planted defect** — one instance removed from a copy of the export, and the two fences that cite it must be named — because a guard that has never fired is a guard nobody has tested.

  *Verification: `python tests/run_tests.py` — **147 checks pass**, 0 fail. The planted-defect check names `fmt-2` and `show-calc-2`, which are the two that were actually wrong.*

- **v0.65** (2026-09-14) — the commands tab's examples are frozen, and its pages are rendered once (§10sexies, §11, §13).

  **Two complaints with one cause.** The engineer asked why an example should change when the project changes, and whether the output could be a PDF or an SVG so the Katalog would not regenerate it each time. Both come back to the same thing: the examples were computed from live data, at view time.

  **They read a snapshot now.** `000-Python/examples/data/` is a frozen copy of `data/`, with `calc/i18n/strings.json` beside it because `lib/i18n.typ` reads that too. So renaming a sheet no longer changes what the documentation shows, and deleting an id no longer breaks an example.

  👉 **The folder's shape is forced by the engine, not chosen.** `lib/calc-engine.typ` reads `json("../data/…")` and `lib/i18n.typ` reads `json("../calc/i18n/strings.json")` — relative paths — so *where the engine file sits* is what decides which data a command sees. The only way to point it at the snapshot is to put a copy of the engine beside the snapshot. That copy is refreshed from the live `lib/` on every run and is **not versioned**: the examples read frozen data on purpose but must exercise today's code, or the compile check proves nothing. A test compares the two byte for byte. The alternative — a `--input data=…` knob in the engine — was rejected as a global switch added for documentation's sake, where a typo would silently read the wrong data. Verified by hiding the snapshot: the examples stop compiling, which is what says they were reading it.

  **The pages are rendered ahead of time and committed.** 167 examples, 14.5 MB, written by the new `calc/tools/build_examples.py`. Opening an arrow now **loads a file** — twenty examples opened in **271 ms**, thirteen milliseconds each, with no process started — where each first view used to cost half a second to a second and to cost it again on every machine. Checked with `typst` removed from the PATH: the tab still shows every page.

  ⚠️ **The speed never came from the format**, which is the answer to the question as asked. An **SVG cannot be displayed at all**: Typst writes every glyph as a `<use>` of a `<symbol>`, and QtSvg implements SVG Tiny 1.2, which has no `<symbol>` — the text would not appear. A **PDF** would work and buy nothing: QtPdf would rasterise it at display time, still a render, where a PNG is a load. What buys the speed is doing the work once.

  **The MWEs are generated from the fences and committed**, one `.typ` per example, never hand-edited — §2 rule 14 is untouched, the example is still written once in its `///` block. Chosen over hand-written example files, which would have taken the examples out of the editor's hover and given rule 14 two texts to keep in step.
