  **New: §11bis**, which lists what is in the folder — 53 norms (SIA 260/261/265 with their supplementary and referenced parts in three languages, the Eurocodes and the FprEN 1995-1-1:2025 draft, the EN test and product standards, the VKF/AEAI fire directives, the two STE Compact booklets), 5 Lignum handbooks, eleven topic folders, three HTML calculators, a decompiled CLTdesigner and a React *Statik-Rechner* — and states **the two rules that govern it**. *A value taken from a norm is read from the norm itself*, at a named edition and a page that can be turned to: that is §2's new **rule 13**, and it is what finally makes §1bis's normative traceability whole, the edition being named in the data *and* now sitting in the repository. *The tools are unverified*: read for their method, never for a number; nothing in `calc/` may import, copy or depend on one; a disagreement with the norm is settled by the norm. The position-plan editor was already the precedent (§13) — its symbology was taken, its code was not — and `107-Contreventement/003-Rapport.md`, which prints a shear flow in kN/mm, is the reminder that the rule is not a formality.

  **Elsewhere, pointers rather than changes.** §2 gained a paragraph putting the norm above Excel as a source and rule 13 under the twelve; §11's tree gained the folder and its `norms/` bullet the place those values are read from; §3.3septies names the PDF Tabelle 8 was transcribed from and §4bis the editions its French and English are quoted from; §3.3quinquies names the bauforumstahl Arbeitshilfe and the HB2 method sheet, both now in `104-MomentPolaire/`; §3.3sexies's reference to the position-plan editor, which pointed at `302-WoodCore/.ExternalFiles/`, points at the library instead (the v0.34 changelog entry keeps the path it was written with); and §14 records that every value still waiting on a norm — the ψ factors of SIA 260's Tabelle 2, the SIA 265-1 grades, the embedment strength of SIA 265 6.2–6.5, the moisture class's factors — now has its edition at hand, which settles none of those decisions and unblocks the looking-up.

  Verified: 57 of the 58 norm and literature PDFs carry extractable text under the `pypdf` the pipeline already depends on (`SIA-343-2014-FR.pdf` is a scan, as are three course documents), and p. 38 of `SIA261-2020-FR.pdf` is indeed «Tableau 8 Catégories de surfaces utiles et valeurs caractéristiques des charges utiles», the table §3.3septies transcribes and §4bis quotes; every first-page title in the table above was read from the file rather than from its name. ⚠️ The folder takes the repository to about 950 MB — 570 MB of norms, 186 MB of `000-JonasHalter/node_modules` — which is a slow first clone and no change at all to the build.

- **v0.52** (2026-09-12) — The report is written in French, German or English (§2, §4bis, §10bis, §11, §12, §13, §14). The engineer asked to write structural reports in the three languages, with a command in `Statischer Rapport.typ` setting the report's language and the display following it — and drew the line himself: what he enters in Excel or in the Katalog, and the German names of the variables he writes by hand, are his and are not translated; the commands, their arguments and the hidden Python names stay English.

  **The command.** One line in the entry point, beside the base level and the decimals: `#set-report-language(sys.inputs.at("lang", default: "de"))`. `--input lang=fr` forces it for one run, as `--input level=` does for the level — `build.ps1 -Language fr`, `make report REPORT_LANG=fr` (not `LANG`: the shell's own variable, which make would take from the environment and choke on).

  **New:** `calc/i18n/strings.json`, the glossary — 63 `engine` keys, 21 `templates`, 139 `terms`, each in three languages; `calc/i18n/strings.py`, which reads and checks it and gives Python `text()`, `template()`, `term()` and `translate()`; and `lib/i18n.typ`, which gives Typst the state, the command, `t`/`tl`, `t-str`/`tl-str`, `t-label`, `t-pattern` and `t-of`/`tl-of`.

  **Three decisions shaped it** (§13). The language is a setting of the *document*, not of the export, so `data/` stays language-free and the Katalog never has to guess one. The glossary's `terms` block keys **the German by itself**, gettext-style, so the labels, step names, group labels, table titles, column headings and notes Python composes are translated where they are printed — and `data/calculations.json`, `plans.json`, `connectors.json` and `positions-index.json` came out of the whole change **byte-identical**, with no schema, no validator, no Katalog and no reference-workbook edit behind them. And the eighteen sentences that interpolate a sheet's name or a chosen value are **matched by the literal segments around their holes** rather than exported as a key and its arguments.

  **What changed in the engine.** 44 sites in `lib/calc-engine.typ` now take their words from the glossary, and no signature moved: Typst's state is readable in any helper called from a caller's context, as `number-digits` already proved, so `var-row`, `render-table`, `step-rows` and `share-note` needed no `lang` parameter. Four functions gained their own context (`show-position`, `show-connector`, `show-static-model`, and `connector-summary` through them). A var whose value is *text* goes through `value-word`, and a symbol that is nothing but a quoted word (`"Geländekategorie"`, `"Lastmodell"`) through the same rule — so the norm's wording follows the language and «Flachdach» does not. `config.typ` sets `text(lang:)` from `report-language.final()`, which brings Typst's own «Abbildung»/«Figure», «Tabelle»/«Tableau», the outline's title and the right hyphenation. The fifteen standard headings of `Sections/` became `= #t("section.actions")`; not one sentence of the engineer's prose was touched.

  **The figures.** A figure's frame is sized from the width of its own text, so each is drawn once per language: `data/connections/<id>-de|fr|en.svg`, `data/statics/<id>-de|fr|en.svg`. `figure.file` became `figure.files` and a static model's `figure`/`view`/`notes` became `figures.<lang>.{file, view, notes}` — the frame travels with the file, because `show-static-model` sizes the image from the record and `fit: "stretch"` would quietly distort a figure whose frame did not match. `connection_svg.render()` and `statics_svg.layout()` take a language; the Katalog passes the window's own, so a preview reads like the report that language would set. `statics_svg`'s `ARIAL_WIDTHS` gained French's accented letters (a width the default guessed would size the frame around a note wrongly), and `connection_svg`'s `font-family` became the bare `"Arial"` the statics figure already used — QtSvg takes the whole string as a single name, and the report writes the same one name into the same figure.

  **The French and the English are quoted, not invented,** wherever the norms give the word: SIA 261's French edition (1.2 for the symbols' names, 5 and 6 for snow and wind, **Tabelle 8 word for word from p. 38**, annex H's Tabelle 80 for German → French → English), SIA 265's French for timber and connections, SIA 260's French and English, STE Compact 02's French for the plan legend, EN 1995-1-1 for the rest. Each block of the glossary names its source, and what no norm gives says it is a translation.

  Verified:
  - **The German report is unchanged.** All twelve pages identical in text to a build of the same data with the previous engine, and three antialiased pixels apart on one line of one table, out of 2.9 million.
  - `python calc/export_all.py` passes and is idempotent; `calculations.json`, `plans.json`, `connectors.json` and `positions-index.json` are byte-identical to the previous export; `statics.json` differs by `figures` in place of `figure`/`view`/`notes` and `connections.json` by `figure.files` in place of `figure.file`; the German figures are byte-identical to the old ones once the deliberate `font-family` fix is normalised away, and the stale single-language SVGs were swept.
  - The report compiles in all three languages, as written and at uniform levels 1–3, and `build.ps1 -AllLevels` and `make` build it; `build.ps1 -Language xx` and `make REPORT_LANG=xx` are refused, and `make` ignores a `LANG` set by the shell.
  - **859 calls** — every `show-*` the Katalog offers for the current data, at every level — compile together in each of the three languages.
  - The French report was read against the norm: Tabelle 8's rows in the French edition's own words, «Catégorie de surface utile H, Toitures non accessibles», the layer build-up as *Composition des couches* with *Épaisseur/Largeur/Poids/Charge*, the wind tables as *Forces globales/locales du vent* with their notes, the connection as *Assemblage* with its legend, *Déterminant : Moyens d'assemblage 1, 3* (plural when several carry it), *Efforts intérieurs*, and both footnotes with their math intact — while «Träger», «Stabdübel, Holz-Holz einschnittig», «Flachdach», «IV grossflächige Stadtgebiete», the material names, the sheet names and the handbook's title stayed exactly as entered.
  - A scan of the French and English PDFs for every German string in the glossary finds none left but the engineer's own prose and the architect's plan text; 11 guards fired on purpose (an unknown language, an unknown key, a hole with nothing given, a language missing from an entry, an empty string, a template whose holes differ, a term with no English or with a German of its own, a changed language list, an empty key, a figure missing a language, a figure named wrongly, an empty frame in one language).
  - The Katalog starts and rebuilds in all three languages, the live switch carries the language into the statics canvas, and QtSvg reads all twelve exported figures.

- **v0.51** (2026-09-12) — Static models, drawn in the Typst-Katalog (§2, §3.3octies, §10bis, §10ter, §10quater, §11, §13, §14). The engineer asked for a module that **draws** a static system and computes nothing: point, appui (encastré, fixe, roulant), barre, câble (efforts normaux positifs seulement), articulation (libre au moment, à l'effort tranchant, à l'effort normal, ressort linéaire, ressort de rotation), charge ponctuelle (angle, intensité, verticale/horizontale/relative à une ligne de référence, nom, décalage) et charge linéaire, constante ou triangulaire; quatre modes d'affichage pour une charge; la saisie par liste de coordonnées ou par clic sur une grille magnétique; une liste à quatre onglets; le comportement d'AutoCAD (survol, verrouillage 0/45/90° ou prolongement, Tab + distance + Enter); zoom, déplacement, zoom sur tout; et une grille dont le pas suit le zoom. In the same breath they added the piece that shapes the data: **les actions sont en kN/m², les systèmes porteurs en kN/m**, so a load must be able to carry an influence width b_e, with a description, shown on the figure.

  **New, and all of it new:** `calc/domains/statics.py` (the source's checks, the register, the geometry, the snapping, the AutoCAD tracking, the values and the influence width, the record and the export), `calc/domains/statics_svg.py` (the drawing), `schemas/statics-source.schema.json` and `schemas/statics.schema.json`, the source folder `000-Python/statics/`, and in the window `calc/tools/statics_tab.py` and `calc/tools/statics_canvas.py`.

  **Three decisions shaped it** (§13). A model is a drawing and not a calculation, so its record carries no `{vars, steps, checks}` and `show-calc` cannot address one. A load's magnitude may name a **variable of the export** — `{domain, instance, var}` read afresh at every export — rather than being substituted by a Typst argument, since a label's box is sized by its own text and the number already exists in Python. And the **influence width** is applied here: the magnitude times b_e, with one metre off the unit (kN/m² → kN/m, kN/m → kN), the value before the width, the width and the product all kept in the record.

  **Two things came out of the drawing itself.** The figure is **normalised** — the model's longer side is 1000 figure units — after QtSvg was found to drop a `<text>` of 0.26 user units while still painting its white halo, which is exactly what a figure drawn in metres asks it for; that also makes a 2 m model and a 20 m one render to the same digits. And a load is scaled within its own kind, never below a third of the largest, the figure saying so in its notes — `connection_svg.SHARE_MIN`'s rule.

  **Elsewhere:** `export_all.py` builds the models with everything else and prints their warnings; `validation.py` gained `validate_statics_business` and the two constants it restates; `lib/calc-engine.typ` gained `load-statics`, `static-number`, `static-model-ids`, `static-halo` and `show-static-model`; `Sections/005-tragwerksanalyse.typ` loops over the models under a *Statische Systeme* heading; `build_reference.py` gained `call_show_static_model`, `call_static_model_loop` and a `Statics` sheet; `catalog_gui.py` gained three lines. `statics/Beispiel.json`, an Einfeldträger mit Kragarm whose snow load is read from `SL-Dach1` over a b_e of 2.5 m, is there as a worked example to adjust or delete.

  Verified:
  - **88 checks on the model**, among them 25 guards fired on purpose, each naming what to fix: a member to an unknown point, a duplicate id ignoring case, a member of no length, two releases of one dof at one end, a magnitude that is neither a value nor a variable, an empty unit, an unknown direction, `member` with no reference and an angle on a vertical load, a spring in the wrong unit or of zero stiffness, `from >= to`, a linear load with one value and a uniform one with two, a width on a unit it cannot divide and a width in centimetres, two supports on one point, an id that is not the file name, and an unknown schema version. Also the register (`next_id`, `used_by`, a cascading delete, a rename following every reference), the geometry, the snapping, and the tracking without any Qt — the eight polar rays, a member's axis winning over the polar ray at the same angle and over another member's normal, a lock dropped past the tolerance, the crossing with a line extended, and `typed_point("3,5")`.
  - **63 checks on the drawing**: it parses; a list of forbidden constructs (`<marker`, `clipPath`, `<pattern`, `<style`, `paint-order`, an arc command, a typographic minus) appears nowhere; every box placed lies inside the viewBox; `loads: false` takes every arrow, every load label and every note and leaves the structure standing; a model ten times the size draws the same figure to 0.002 of a unit, and byte for byte where nothing carries an offset; the two scales; a small load held to its minimum and named in the notes; b_e on the load's second line and its description under the drawing; an unreadable value drawn grey, written `?` and named; and `QSvgRenderer` reading the figure and all 14 tool icons.
  - **58 checks on the window**, driven off screen: a click putting each thing down and refusing where there is nothing to put it on; the grid giving 5 / 1 / 0.5 / 0.1 m at four zooms with the snap following; `zoom_at` holding the anchored point to a fiftieth of a metre; the tracking from hover to Tab to Enter, with the chain to the next anchor; a coordinate typed into a list landing in the source, a bad cell keeping the old value and being painted, an id renamed following every reference; the influence width and the variable combos; undo and redo; the draft surviving a language switch in all three languages; and *Enregistrer* writing the source and exporting it in the same process.
  **After the engineer's first use, eleven things changed**, all in the same version. The frame turned over: x right and **z down**, the connection module's (§13). A **translational spring became a member kind** beside the bar and the cable, with its own stiffness, and left the articulations, which are now four words (§13). The **shear and normal release symbols were the wrong way round** -- the lines say which way the halves may move, so they are along the member for a normal release and across it for a shear one -- and both are now drawn **on the node** rather than beside it. A **subscript is set as one**: `b_e` prints as b with a small e, and the pen's advance counts a space as half a letter, a `<text>` dropping the spaces at its own ends. The Katalog draws the figure **without its white ground**, so the grid shows through. The **wheel always zooms and the middle button always pans**, between absolute limits -- limits taken from the model's own size moved with it, and a first point left the view zoomed in past use. A **support just put down is turned by the pointer**, in steps of 45°, until the next click sets it. The **four lists moved under the drawing**, where they are as wide as it is; a **point's support and its angle joined its list**; and the cell clicked in a list keeps the focus instead of being taken back to the first column. The **pointer's coordinates, an x/z axis pair and the tool's hint are written inside the drawing**. And the **tool icons** are drawn in their own proportions, heavier, a release's cropped to its node.

  Verified after the review: the three suites pass (92 model, 68 drawing, 70 window), the export and the report build, and both figures -- the example and the engineer's own -- read correctly with their subscripts.

  **A second review, seventeen more things, all in this version.** The engineer used the module and asked for:

  - **a node under an articulation is no longer drawn** — the articulation's symbol is the mark there, and the dot only thickened it;
  - **a point's row carries its articulation**, in a dropdown of the four tools, where exactly one member end meets at that node;
  - **an angle starts at 90°**, for *Angle / barre* and for *Angle / x* alike: a load given at an angle is across its member or straight down, never along it;
  - **a uniform line load has no *Valeur fin* cell** — the column is closed on that row rather than left to be typed into;
  - **a value is optional**: it is there to be printed and to scale the arrow, and a system sketched before its actions are known has neither. Such a load is drawn at the full arrow length, in its own colour, and shows its name alone — it is a load, not a load that failed to resolve, and the two must not look alike;
  - **several loads on one member stand one above the other**, not on top of one another, and their labels go **inside** their bands, a label above a band being read as the next load's;
  - **the right panel widens further**, which the tool bar's own width had been holding back;
  - **the grid offers 5 m** as well;
  - **the whole model may carry a b_e**, with a description, said once at the foot of the figure, and a load's own overrides it;
  - **a referenced variable may be taken with a factor and a constant** — `0.8 · q_k11 + 0.2` — with a description that goes into the notes;
  - ***Variable de l'export* is renamed *Variables***;
  - **a point load takes two clicks**, the point it acts on and then its reference member, which its angle is counted from; the list gains a *Barre* column for it;
  - **and a *Décalage* column**, how far along that member it is drawn, in metres or as a fraction — `0.5l` is midspan; it replaces the dx/dz offset and its `eccentric` flag, which are gone from the source, the schema and the drawing;
  - **the tool bar is grouped into named boxes** — Éléments, Appuis, Articulations, Charges, Navigation, Historique — with every button the same height and no name cut short.

  The bar took four changes to get there, and the last two were not obvious. Every button is the same height whether it carries a name or its symbol alone. A name is set **under** its symbol, not beside it: beside it each button was 139 px and the six groups needed four rows. In the bar a tool is named by what tells it from its neighbours, the box above it saying the rest — under *Charges* a button reads *Ponctuelle* — and the full name stays as its tooltip. And the bar **wraps** instead of scrolling, so the middle panel demands only the widest single group and the panels beside the drawing widen as far as the engineer likes. ⚠️ The symbols are drawn in the figure's ink, which is black: on Windows' dark theme they were all but invisible, so the button now takes its ink and its fills from the palette.

  **Three defects the review's own figures exposed**, each fixed here. A **subscript was placed where a flat 0.56 em a letter said the run before it ended**, which is 10 to 20 per cent out on a real word: over `q_k` invisible, over `g_k = 2 kN/m² × b_e = 3 m` it put the second subscript on top of the text after it. `ARIAL_WIDTHS` holds Arial's own advance per character now, measured once from the font. That alone was not enough: **`FONT` named a list**, `Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif`, and QtSvg takes the whole `font-family` string as one name, so it matched nothing and fell back to a face whose advances are not Arial's — `FONT` is the bare `Arial`, which resvg reads too and which Liberation Sans substitutes with the same advances. (A `<tspan>` would let the renderer do the layout itself, and was measured: QtSvg honours its advance but neither its `dy` nor its `baseline-shift`, so the subscript would sit on the baseline.) A **tapering load printed the value at its near end alone**, labelling a triangular load rising to 12 kN/m as `0 kN/m`; it is written from one end to the other now, `0 … 12 kN/m`. And a **note naming the variable a load was read from did not give the number it stood for** — `q_k = q_k11 kN/m² [snow-load/SL-Dach1] × b_e = 2.5 m` reads as though `q_k11` were the value — so the note gives the expression in letters and then the number, this report's own rule everywhere else (§10bis): `q_k = q_k11 = 1.383 kN/m² [snow-load/SL-Dach1] × b_e = 2.5 m`, and 1.383 × 2.5 is the 3.458 on the arrow.

  One thing was decided rather than asked: a **model's** influence width passes silently over a load in kN, which it cannot divide, while a **load's own** width on such a unit is still refused by name. Refusing both would make a single global b_e impossible on any model carrying one point load; the engineer who sets a width on one load set it deliberately.

  Verified after the second review: **291 checks** pass — 111 on the model, 81 on the drawing, 99 on the window — among them the stacking, the label inside its band and outside a band too shallow to hold it, the hidden node, the two-click point load, `parse_along` in metres and as a fraction, the optional value, the model-wide width with a load's own overriding it, the factor and the constant with their note, the closed *Valeur fin* cell, the six tool groups with every button one height, and the bar's minimum width. The tool bar was grabbed with Windows' own fonts in all three languages: six groups, nothing cut, one button height. `python calc/export_all.py` passes and is byte-identical run twice. `.\build.ps1 -AllLevels` builds the report and its three variants, and the figures were read at 130 ppi through Typst's own renderer: every subscript sits under its letter, the notes are checkable, and the stacked bands and their labels are clear.

  - `python calc/export_all.py` passes and prints the model; run twice, the figure is not rewritten; a source deleted sweeps its figure; a reference to a var that is gone prints a warning and still exits 0. `.uild.ps1` builds the report, where §5.1 *Statische Systeme* now carries the figure with `q_k = 3.458 kN/m` over `b_e = 2.5 m` — 1.383 kN/m² read from `SL-Dach1` — and the note *b_e = 2.5 m – Einflussbreite Dach*.

- **v0.50** (2026-09-12) — A member's label lies inside its band on a connection's figure (§3.3quinquies). The engineer found the label of a vertical member half on the band's border, and asked for it to read like a horizontal member's. The cause: a label is set half a font inside one edge, and its letters grow from their baseline away from that edge — but only when the label is not turned. A vertical member's reads upwards, and its letters then grow to the left, while the baseline stayed on the left edge, so the text crossed the border. `connection_svg.render()` now takes the turned text's own upward direction, `(sin ρ, −cos ρ)`, and puts the baseline along whichever edge that direction points away from, so the letters always grow towards the member's axis. Nothing else moved: same distance from the edge, same place along the member, same rotation, and a horizontal member's label is drawn exactly where it was. Verified on the project's two connections — the vertical member's label now sits inside its band, mirroring the horizontal member's, at x = +93.24 against its y = +93.24 — and on a synthetic six-member connection at 0°, 35°, 90°, 125°, −60° and 160°, where every baseline and every capital's reach reads inside its own band's half-width. The figures were re-exported and the report compiles.

  Asked for next, on the same figure: the moment's arrow crossed a yellow resultant, and it was to be drawn clear of the other arrows, extended to run from south-west to south-east, with a limit past which an overlap is accepted again. `MOMENT_SPAN` is now 135° to 45° on the drawing, a quarter turn instead of 30°, and `_moment_radius()` places it: the report's arrows leave their fastener in any direction and reach `arrow` with a label beyond, so every fastener claims a disc of that radius, and the arc runs beyond the farthest point those discs reach along the directions it spans, with `MOMENT_CLEAR` (0.8 font) to spare. It is never shorter than 0.45 of the widest member, so a small group still shows a readable arc, and never longer than `MOMENT_MAX` (0.95) of it — the limit asked for: a group whose arrows reach further than its members are wide would otherwise push the arc, and the whole frame, far past them, and there the arc may cross an arrow again. The radius is computed from `arrow`, the longest arrow the figure can carry, not from N, V and M, which the figure never sees: the report inserts its arrows into a drawing already made. The arc moved out to where the members' labels are, so it is now drawn before them, and a label carries a halo of its own band — its fill over the white page (`_over_white`), the text drawn twice as `plan_svg.py` does, SVG Tiny having no paint-order — so the arc passes behind the text and both stay legible. Breaking the arc behind the label instead, as a plan's slab diagonals break behind a number, was tried and dropped: it left the arrowhead adrift from its arc. The labels' placement moved into `layout()`, which now settles it once for the drawing and for the arc. Verified: on the project's two connections the arc clears every arrow by exactly the 0.8 font asked of it (radius 181.9 and 167.8, under the cap of 190), and the label is read over it; on four synthetic cases the cap holds a widely spread group on thin members, a single fastener at the node takes the floor, a connection with no fastener draws no arc, and a tall group is capped. The figures were re-exported and `.uild.ps1 -SkipData` rebuilt the report.

  Third on the same command, and this one in the report rather than the drawing: at level 3, `show-polar-moment` split N and V into x and z on four lines whichever way they acted, and with the members at right angles those lines said nothing — `N_x = N · cos(0°) = 0 kN` restates the N above it. The engineer asked for N and V alone in that case. The rule is on each force's own direction rather than on the angle between the members, which is what makes it true: a force along x or along z is its own component, and a force of zero has none, so its two lines are left out; a force at any other angle keeps them. With the members at right angles and the forces along them — the usual case, and the one the engineer was reading — both splits go. Verified on the project's own figures (N along x, V along z: the level-3 block runs from the Schnittkräfte straight to *Massgebend* and the F formulas) and on a connection with N at 30° and V at 120°, where all four lines are printed as before. The report was rebuilt.

  Fourth, from the engineer's own reference, bauforumstahl's *Arbeitshilfe 2.9*, whose Bild 3 sets out the three actions on one screw and their resultant. **The force the moment puts on each fastener is now drawn**, a green arrow beside the blue N/n and the red V/n: `M·r_i/I_p` at right angles to the lever arm, the article's V_b,i. It is read off the record rather than recomputed — each fastener's force less the shares of N and V, which every fastener carries alike (`_moment_parts`, `moment-parts`) — so the four arrows on a fastener are the three the figure draws and the sum it draws them for, and nothing can drift. It joins the common scale, and the scale now measures every arrow including these, a resultant being able to come out shorter than the forces it adds where they oppose each other. The legend gained an `F_M` line, and its `M` is drawn as the arc itself rather than a straight line, so that two green entries cannot be read as the same thing. **The widths were tied**: `ACTION_WIDTH` (1.6 strokes) for every action — N/n, V/n, F_M and the moment's arc, which was 2.4 before — and `RESULTANT_WIDTH = 1.5 × ACTION_WIDTH` for the resultant, mirrored in `lib/calc-engine.typ` as `action-width` and `resultant-width`; measured in the drawing at 1.42 against 2.12 units, the resultant outlined at 3.61 besides.

  **The signs were checked against the article**, which is what prompted it. With N, V and M acting together on both of the project's connections: the force the moment puts on a fastener equals `M·r_i/I_p` to machine precision (3.6e-15 kN) and is exactly at right angles to its lever arm; the fastener forces are statically equivalent to the actions (ΣF_x = N_x + V_x, ΣF_z = N_z + V_z, and the moments about the centre sum to +M with the anticlockwise convention, to six decimals). The one place the article's signs differ from ours is its `max V_Schr`, whose two terms are both added: that formula is the unfavourable superposition, its terms magnitudes, and it is what the second table at level 3 already prints (v0.37) — a true vector sum must carry opposite signs on the two moment terms, or the force would not be perpendicular to the lever arm. So nothing was wrong. Python and Typst draw the new arrows identically, element for element and colour for colour, over eight cases on the two connections, M = 0 among them, where no green arrow is drawn at all.

  Fifth, and it is what the fourth uncovered: with the moment's arrow on the drawing, the engineer added blue, red and green by eye and did not get the yellow. The figure was right about direction, sense and size — at true scale the three add to the resultant exactly, to 0.00e+00 kN on every fastener — but the blue and the red were not at true scale. v0.32 held them to a minimum length so they would not vanish under a moment, and on the report's own figure that made the blue 86 times and the red 17 times too long (N/n = 0.06 kN and V/n = 0.31 kN beside an F of 10.7 kN, drawn 35 units where they measure 0.4 and 2.0). Given the choice, the engineer took one scale for everything. `SHARE_MIN`/`share-min` are gone; `SHARE_SHOWN`/`share-shown` replace them, and they change nothing about the drawing — a share shorter than about the fastener's dot is simply named in the legend with its value, *Zu klein zum Erkennen: N/n = 0.06 kN, V/n = 0.31 kN*, and drawn at its true length all the same. Leaving it out was tried first, as the lesser evil, and dropped on measuring it: it breaks the same addition by the length left out (13.8 drawing units on one of the cases). Verified by reading the arrows back out of the SVG and adding them: on both connections and three sets of forces — the report's own, N 12 / V 8 / M 5, and a case without a moment — blue + red + green minus yellow is 0.000 drawing units, where it was up to 47.8 before. Python and Typst still draw the same figure over the eight cases. §13's decision on one scale now records the whole of it.

- **v0.49** (2026-09-12) — The Nutzlasten, read off SIA 261's Tabelle 8 (§2, §3.3, §3.3septies, §10bis, §10ter, §10quater, §11, §13, §14). The engineer asked for a Python module for the live load, callable from Typst by category, with the three levels they specified: the category with its two values; the same line with the category described; and Tabelle 8 entire with the chosen row set apart. Four things were agreed before writing: a command of its own rather than the generic `show-calc`; a shorthand for the value and the unit; category E refused rather than invented; and level 3 cumulative, the table above the line. The name is the SIA's own English, *live load* — the Eurocode's *imposed load* would have collided with `dead_load.py`'s `KIND_IMPOSED`, which is an Auflast.

  **The norm's numbers, once.** New `calc/norms/sia261_tables.json` holds Tabelle 8 as the German edition gives it: the edition, the table's title and citation, twelve categories with their Art der Nutzfläche, the norm's example, q_k, Q_k and the footnote references of each, and the five footnotes. New `calc/norms/sia261_tables.py` reads it and checks it — a category twice, a value that is neither a number nor absent, half a row open, a load that is not positive, a footnote referenced but not written. `norms/` had been described since v0.1 and never written.

  **The domain.** New `calc/domains/live_load.py`: `DOMAIN = "live-load"`, `LABEL = "Nutzlasten"`, `ACTION = "variable"`, `ENGINE = "norm"`, `SHEET_PREFIX = None`, and `build_instances()` in place of `read_instances()`. One instance per category, carrying `kategorie`, `nutzflaeche`, `beispiel` (absent for Büroflächen, which the norm gives none), `q_k` and `Q_k` — each value's footnotes as its `description` — plus Tabelle 8 entire as its one table, level 3, with its own row in `highlight`. Category E carries no values, only the sentence of 8.2.2 as a `hinweis` var. Every var's `source` is `norm:SIA 261:2020, Tabelle 8`.

  **Registry, validation, schema.** `registry.py` skips a module without a sheet prefix in its discovery and takes its instances from `build_instances()`; `DOMAIN_MODULES` gained `live_load` last. `validation.py` gained `ENGINES` (`excel`, `python`, `norm`), checked on every domain for the first time, the rule that a norm domain has no sheet prefix and no workbook or sheet on its instances while the others must have all three, and the `highlight` rules. `calculations.schema.json` made `workbook`/`sheet` facultative and gained `highlight` and `label_math`; `schema_version` is **1.4**.

  **Typst.** `lib/calc-engine.typ`: `render-table` reads a table's `highlight` and sets those rows bold on `table-highlight` (`#fff2b2`), `column-header` sets a column's `label_math` as real math, and a new section holds `live-load-record`, `show-live-load` and `live-load`. `Sections/004-einwirkungen.typ` fills its empty *Nutzlast* heading with `#show-live-load("H", level: 2)` — the roofs of the Velo-Unterstand and the Pavillon are reachable for maintenance only.

  **Katalog and reference workbook.** `build_reference.py` gained `call_show_live_load` and `call_live_load`, marks a norm instance's `source_type` `norm`, and gives a category's `example_call` the dedicated call — except E's, which the report refuses. `catalog_gui.py` translates the engine `norm` and a `norm:` source in the three languages, drops the sheet-prefix line where there is none, offers `#show-live-load(...)` and `#live-load(...)` first for this domain, and shows a `math` cell of a table as its value with its footnote reference rather than as Typst source.

  Verified:
  - The export passes, and the three sheet domains are **byte-identical** to what the pre-change registry builds from the same workbooks; `live-load` comes out with its twelve categories.
  - 13 guards fired on purpose, each naming what to fix: a category listed twice, an unwritten footnote, a value that is not a number, half a row open, a negative load, three malformed `highlight`s, an unknown engine, a norm domain with a sheet prefix, a norm instance naming a workbook, and a workbook domain missing its prefix or its instance's file.
  - In Typst: the three levels for A1, B (no example), F (two notes, said once) and H (the category's own note); `title:` as a string; `E` and an unknown category refused with their message; `live-load()` in its three `type:`s; `value-of` in a hand-written equation; `show-calc` on the same record; and the 1230 calls the Katalog offers for the current data compiled together in one file.
  - `.\build.ps1 -AllLevels` builds the report and the three variants. The Nutzlast section reads *Nutzungskategorie H, Nicht begehbare Dächer (Dächer mit Zugang nur für Unterhaltsarbeiten): q_k = 0.4 kN/m²; Q_k = 1 kN¹*, and the level-3 variant sets Tabelle 8 with H's row on its tinted ground. The wind tables (column groups, `value` cells) and the dead load's build-ups (a `wide` column, `totals`) render as before — `render-table`'s new `fill` returns nothing for a table without `highlight`.
  - A scripted off-screen run of the Katalog passed 21 checks: the domain under the variable actions, its engine in words, twelve cases, the details naming Tabelle 8 and saying the value was read from the norm, *Ouvrir le classeur* disabled, the dedicated calls first for a value and for a case, none of them for E, no sheet-prefix line, and the language switch.

- **v0.48** (2026-09-12) — Changing plan in the Katalog without waiting (§10quater, §13). The engineer found every change of plan slow, architects' PDFs being heavy. Measured on this project's plan set (`01_Pläne/02_Total Plane - 29-07-26.pdf`, 6.4 MB, 25 pages, the four plans drawn on it): loading the file takes 0.07 s, rendering a page at 2500 px 3.5 to 8 s, at 1000 px 0.6 to 1 s, and the part a plan's crop shows 0.3 to 0.8 s. The file's weight costs nothing; rasterizing a dense sheet costs, and faster than its pixels. Three faults added up in `plan_canvas.py`, and a fourth in `plan_tab.py`:
  - the whole page was rendered first, at 2500 px, the view behind it, so nothing was sharp for 4 to 9 s;
  - `load_background` made a new `QPdfDocument` and loaded it for every plan, even on the same file; QtPdf runs every PDFium call under one lock, the renders in its thread included, so the load waited for the render on its way — **7.9 s of frozen window, measured** — and requests already sent could not be taken back. `clip_bias()`, at the first `sharpen()`, waited the same way;
  - nothing was kept: a plan seen before was rendered again;
  - in `PageDialog`, each page's size was read after the previous thumbnail had been asked for, and a size is read under that same lock: the dialog opened only once the thumbnails were nearly all rendered.

  What changed:
  - **the view first, the whole page after and smaller.** `BASE_RENDER_PX` is 1200, and a plan just opened asks for the part in view without a margin (`first_view`, `tile_job`), before the whole page. `clip_bias()` is measured when the canvas is built.
  - **one render at a time, the newest wanted first** (`PdfPages`): a render is asked for only when the one before has come back, the view before the whole page, and a newer request takes the place of the one of its kind waiting. A document is loaded or closed only while nothing renders (`collect`, `busy`): it is kept when the next plan is on the same file, and a plan on another file waits for the render on its way. A render of a plan left meanwhile is kept rather than thrown away.
  - **every render kept**, in memory (`RenderCache`, 256 MB) and on disk (`DiskCache`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\typst-katalog\renders`, PNG written in a thread of its own, 300 MB at most), under the file, its size and time, the page and the part rendered. Where the window has changed size, the nearest render kept shows at once while the view's own is rendered (`kept_tile`). The scroll bars are always shown, so that fitting a plan gives the same view every time.
  - **pypdf off the way to the screen**: `fill()` takes the page count from the canvas's own document (`page_count`, `pages_known`, `show_page_count`) instead of reading the file; `plan.py` keeps its reading and its hash of a file while the file is unchanged (`_read_pdf`, `_hash_file`, `_file_state`), so the export at *Enregistrer* reads a plan set once and not once per plan; and the Katalog imports pypdf in a thread of its own at startup (`plan.preload`), the import taking most of a second that the first page turned used to wait for.
  - `PageDialog` reads every page's size before asking for a thumbnail.

  Verified by a scripted off-screen run of the tab and of the whole window, 30 checks, among them: each of the four plans sharp in 0.5 to 1.3 s from cold, the view rendered before the whole page; a plan seen before shown in under 0.1 s without a render, in the session and, from the disk cache, in a window built afresh; switching plans every 0.1 s while a render is on its way, the longest stall of the event loop 88 ms against 7.9 s; a plan on another PDF opened mid-render deferred and no freeze; five pages turned in half a second rendering the last page and at most one between, the event loop never held up beyond 0.16 s (a page turned is 13 to 23 ms of work in the window, 53 ms the first time, which reads the file); a window of another size showing the nearest render at once and the sharp one 0.4 s later; the part in view identical to PDFium's render of the whole page at the same scale (the v0.39 check); a PDF written since rendered afresh; the disk cache trimmed to its limit; *Pages…* open in 0.26 s for 25 sheets; `plan.prepare()` reading the plan set once and giving files identical to `data/plans/`, byte for byte. The Katalog itself was run off screen: its plan tab shows its plan, and a language switch in mid-render rebuilds it in 0.24 s. Nothing changed in what is drawn, in the sources, in the export or in the report.

- **v0.47** (2026-09-11) — A position plan's tool with no position to draw says so in an alert (§3.3sexies, §10quater). With Positionen.xlsx holding only T001, S001 and S002, the engineer found that V, H, W, A, D, M and L drew nothing and added nothing to the element list, and took the tools for broken. The cause was v0.45's rule, which they had asked for: only the sheet's positions are drawn. It explained itself only in the hint under the plan and in the status bar. Offered two fixes, greyed-out tools or an alert, the engineer chose the alert alone.
  - `plan_tab.py`: `on_refused()`, called by the canvas's `refused`, also opens a warning with the same text (`plan_no_position`: the letter, the position to add, *Réexporter depuis Excel*). It is opened by a `QTimer.singleShot(0)` rather than inside the canvas's mouse press, which holds the mouse. `refusal_open` keeps it to one at a time, so a double-click opens one. Choosing the tool, by its button or its letter, opens nothing, and the tool stays chosen, drawing as soon as the sheet has a position of its letter.

  Verified off screen against the engineer's positions, 15 checks: choosing V opens nothing; a click and then a double-click with it open one alert, naming V001 and Positionen.xlsx; nothing is drawn, and the hint keeps the text; a later click alerts again. H, W, A, D, M and L alert and draw nothing. T still draws T001 without an alert, and S still draws.

- **v0.46** (2026-09-11) — The wind load's level 2 is the Geländekategorie, the global wind forces' table and the local wind forces' table (§3.3quater). The engineer asked for exactly these three. What changed, all in `calc/domains/wind.py`:
  - `_local_table`'s `level` went from 3 to 2, so the local forces' table follows the global one from level 2.
  - q_p0, z_g, c_red,b/h and c_red,d/h went from level 2 to 3, so level 2 no longer prints them.
  - The module's docstring, `_local_table`'s docstring and the comments on the level split follow.

  `show-calc` needed no change: it prints each var and each table from the level the data gives it (§10bis). Level 1 and level 3 print what they printed before.

  Verified:
  - The re-export's `data/calculations.json` differs from the previous one in exactly 20 values, five per WL sheet: the local table's level and the four vars' levels. `connections.json`, `connectors.json`, `plans.json` and `positions-index.json` are byte-identical.
  - WL-Velo rendered at the three levels. Level 1 shows the Geländekategorie and the global table, as before. Level 2 shows the Geländekategorie with its choices as a footnote, the global table and the local table, on one page. Level 3 shows the whole sheet, then both tables, the local one kept whole on the next page.
  - `.\build.ps1 -AllLevels -SkipData` compiles the report and its three uniform variants. Checked in their text, for the three wind cases `Sections/004` prints:
    - `out/report-1.pdf` has no local table;
    - `out/report-2.pdf` has the local table but neither z_g nor c_red;
    - `out/report-3.pdf` has all of them.

    `Statischer Rapport.pdf` itself shows no wind case at level 2: `Sections/004` calls WL-Velo with `level:3` and the two others with `level:1`.

- **v0.45** (2026-09-11) — A position plan draws only positions Positionen.xlsx has, and a beam or a bracing member is one straight line (§3.3sexies, §10quater, §13). The engineer asked for both. No position the Excel lacks may be drawn, and the T and A tools draw a line of two points, not a polyline. What changed:
  - `plan.py`: `TYPES` gives T and A `"points": 2`, read by the new `point_count()`, and `check_source()` refuses a T or A without exactly two points, naming the element. `auto_label()` returns only a position of the sheet. That is the last number of the type and floor on the plan that the sheet has, else the sheet's first of that type and floor, else its first of that type on another floor, else "". The fallback `type + floor + 01` is gone. The new `unknown_labels()` gives a plan's numbers the sheet lacks.
  - `plan_canvas.py`: a click with a numbered type's tool draws nothing when the tab proposes no number, and emits the new signal `refused`. The T and A tools end on their second point. A second click on the first end is let go, instead of ending the line empty.
  - `plan_tab.py`: `propose_label()` takes the tool's held number only while the sheet has it. `set_label()` refuses a number the sheet lacks, before it becomes the tool's. The number field is a list, no longer editable; a number the sheet lacks shows as `T009 (?)`, and no number as «(aucune)». `save()` refuses a plan carrying such a number. `choose_tool()` shows a two-ends hint for T and A, and says when the sheet has no position of the tool's letter; `on_refused()` says it again in the status line. `plan_number_unknown`'s text follows, and four keys are new in the three languages. The module's docstring states the rule.
  - The export is unchanged: a number the sheet lacks is still only a warning there (§13).

  Verified:
  - The four saved plans still pass `check_source()`, all their beams having two points. A T or an A of three points is refused with its message, and a V of three is not.
  - A scripted off-screen run of the window passed 41 checks:
    - T: one click puts down its first end, a second click on it is let go, and the second end ends the line, with two points and a position of the sheet. The tool stays T, a third click starts the next line and Esc drops it. A click, then a double-click, make one beam and begin nothing after it.
    - The sheet has no A, V or L. The A and V hints say so, and clicks with A and with L draw nothing, the status line saying why. With A001 in the sheet, A ends on its second click; with V001, V is still a polyline ended by Enter. S takes the sheet's S001, and B, which carries no number, is drawn.
    - A T renumbered T009: outlined, the list not editable, showing `T009 (?)` and the sheet's T. Saving is refused naming T009, and nothing is exported. T001 chosen from the list, `set_label("T009")` refused, then saving passes and exports.
    - `auto_label()` with no position of the letter, with only another floor's, with the plan's floor first, and with a number on the plan the sheet lacks.
  - Session 35's checks of v0.44 pass, but for the ten its test sets on purpose with numbers the sheet lacks (S009, a cleared number, S101, V001).
  - Screenshots in French and German, with Windows' fonts: the new hint fits its two lines.

- **v0.44** (2026-09-11) — A tool of the position plan draws the same number every time (§3.3sexies, §10quater). The engineer asked for it. With the column tool chosen, every column drawn is the same position (S001), and once one of them is renumbered S003, every column drawn after it is an S003; the same for every tool. Until now a new element took the next number no plan showed yet, so ten identical columns came out S001 to S010. What changed:
  - `plan.py`: `auto_label(type_, floor, elements, position_ids)` gives the number a tool starts from. That is the last number of its type and of the plan's floor already on the plan, else the first position of that type and floor in Positionen.xlsx, else `type + floor + 01`. It no longer looks for a free number.
  - `plan.py`: `warnings()` no longer reports a number drawn twice or drawn on another plan, since identical elements share their position, and says a thing about a number once. Its `other_labels` parameter and `prepare()`'s list of which plans show which number are gone. `plan_labels()`, and with it a plan's `labels` in `data/plans.json`, lists each number once.
  - `plan_tab.py`: `tool_labels` holds, per type, the number an element of that type was last renumbered to (`set_label()`), and `propose_label()` draws it. A number cleared does not become the tool's, and *Annuler* takes back a renumbering but not the tool's number. It is emptied when another plan is opened or the floor is changed, and it survives a language switch (`view_state()`).
  - `plan_tab.py`: a number drawn several times is no longer outlined in red. `taken_labels()` and `other_labels()` are gone.

  Verified by a scripted off-screen run of the tab, 29 checks, among them:
  - three clicks with the column tool give S001 three times;
  - a column renumbered S003 in the selection panel makes the next two S003;
  - beams, slabs and columns each keep their own number, and an opening still carries none;
  - a number cleared leaves the tool's, and so does *Annuler*;
  - nothing is flagged for a known number drawn often; an unknown one drawn three times is flagged, warned about and offered for Excel once;
  - the numbers survive `view_state()`/`restore()`; floor 1 starts again at S101; another plan's column tool goes on from that plan's last column;
  - the export of two plans each showing S001 twice gives no warning and lists S001 once per plan.

- **v0.43** (2026-09-11) — The page picker of the position plan tab marks a chosen page in blue (§10quater). The engineer found a chosen page hard to tell from the others: the windows11 style framed it in a grey hardly lighter than the dark dialog, and they asked for a blue frame. `PageDialog`'s pages are now painted by a delegate of their own, `PageThumbDelegate` in `calc/tools/plan_tab.py`: the thumbnail, its number under it and, for a chosen page, a 3 px blue frame (`#2f8cff`, whatever Windows' accent colour, which a palette's highlight follows) over a blue tint, its number set bold. The page under the pointer is lit in grey and the current page carries a dotted frame. Every page also gets the same cell, sized from the list's font, so a number can no longer be cut off under a thumbnail of any format — the other fault the engineer reported, which v0.39's square thumbnails had already cured in the code. Nothing else changed: the selection, `pages()`, «Créer (n)» and the single-page mode are the list's as before. Verified off screen on `01_Pläne/02_Total Plane - 29-07-26.pdf` (25 pages), 12 checks: clicks choose and unchoose pages, *Tout sélectionner* and *Aucune*, the OK button's count and state, the single mode opening on the current page and keeping one, every page one cell within the grid, four to a row. Grabs were checked in the dark and the light palette.

- **v0.42** (2026-09-11) — A position number can be moved by hand on a plan (§3.3sexies, §10quater, §14). The engineer asked for it: a number's own place sometimes falls on something else, as on the Velo-Unterstand plan, where the number of column S001 and that of beam T001 beside it met. §14 had listed it as open. What changed:
  - **Source.** An element may carry `label_offset` [dx, dy], in sheet units from the number's own place. An offset, not a point, so the number follows its element when the element is moved or reshaped. `check_source` requires it to be two numbers and refuses it on a type without a number. Sources are `schema_version` 1.1 (`SOURCE_SCHEMA_VERSIONS` reads 1.0 too, which has every number in its own place), and the tab's *Enregistrer* stamps 1.1.
  - **Drawing** (`plan_svg.py`). `number_frame()` computes where a number is drawn — its centre, half-sides, angle, own place and whether it was moved. `_number`, the Katalog's hit-test and the export all read it, together with `number_frames()`, `on_number()` and `number_corners()`. `_leader()` ties a number that has left its symbol back to it: from the box's edge to the nearest point of the symbol (`plan.nearest_point`), in the number's colour, ending on a dot except at a column. It is drawn once the gap is more than the box's height, or than a column's number sits above the column by itself, and never for a number within its slab. The leaders are drawn under all the numbers. `_slab` breaks its diagonals behind the number where it is, and an Auflager's square follows `number_frame`'s `along`. A wall's length does not move.
  - **Checks** (`plan.py`). `hit_label()` gives the element whose number is under a point, the one drawn on top winning, with half a click's reach beyond the box. `warnings()` names a number moved out of the crop.
  - **Export.** `build_record` gives every numbered element `label_at`, its number's centre. `data/plans.json` goes to 1.2, and `validate_plans_business` checks that `label_at` is [x, y]. In `show-plan`, a detail's window takes in each highlighted element's `label_at`; a 1.1 export, without it, frames the points alone as before.
  - **Katalog.** `plan_canvas.py`: `press_select` takes a number before the element under it, after the selected element's corners. A drag of kind `label` moves the offset, with Shift constraining it, and drops it within a click's reach of the number's own place. The pointer shows the move cross over a number (`label_under`, frames kept per state as `_label_frames`), and the selection outlines the number's box. `plan_tab.py`: under the number, «à sa place» or «Remettre à sa place»; the select hint mentions the drag. There are three new keys in each language.

  Verified:
  - Without a moved number, `plan_svg.render()` gives byte-identical SVG before and after the change. That holds for the real Velo-Unterstand plan and a synthetic plan with every type and subtype, over the whole sheet and the crop. The engineer's four current plans export with no warning and SVGs identical to their current export.
  - 63 checks of the drawing and the checks, among them:
    - where each kind of number is drawn and moved;
    - a leader absent along a beam, beside it and inside a slab, present and correctly placed when moved away, black for a W, without a dot at a column;
    - the diagonals broken where the number now is, whole when it leaves the slab;
    - the Auflager's square following it, the length staying;
    - `hit_label` on turned boxes and on the number on top;
    - `check_source` accepting 1.0 and 1.1 and refusing five malformed offsets and one on an opening;
    - the warning, and `label_at` in the export.
  - A scripted off-screen run of the tab on a copy of the Velo-Unterstand plan passed 36 checks: the move cross, a click that moves nothing, a drag, undo and redo, the return to the number's own place, a leader, Shift, «Remettre à sa place» and its undo, the beam dragged with its moved number, a German rebuild keeping the draft, and save and export to 1.1 and 1.2 with `label_at`. It ran against the code as merged with the parallel sessions' changes.
  - Typst compiled `show-plan` on the moved numbers, with `highlight`, `detail`, `zoom` and a `calc-block(plan: auto)`, and the real report compiles against the current 1.1 export.

- **v0.41** (2026-09-11) — The moment's arrow on a connection's figure is shortened (§3.3quinquies). The engineer found it too long: an arc of about 270° (from −25° to −295° on the drawing) around the rotation centre, crossing the fasteners and their force arrows. They asked for 30°, starting south-south-west and ending south-south-east. `connection_svg.render()` now draws it from 105° to 75° on the drawing, where 90° is south and a falling angle turns anticlockwise. That is 30° centred on south, at the same radius (`moment_radius`) and with the same head. A strict SSW-to-SSE arc would span 45°, and the 30° asked for was kept. The "M" moved from the upper left to the arc's tail, left of it. Under the arc it had covered the label of a lower fastener's resultant, which under a moment points there too. Nothing else changed: the legend, the margin kept around the centre, and the Typst side, which never draws M. Verified on the project's two connections, SD_01-8mm and SD_02-10mm, with N 20, V 16, M 20 kN/kNm: the arc sits under the centre, the M beside it, and every F label is readable. `.\build.ps1` re-exported both figures and rebuilt the report.

- **v0.40** (2026-09-11) — One import for every command (§4, §10bis, §10ter, §10quater, §11, §13). The engineer asked for it: each section began with its own list of the functions it used, `#import "../000-Python/lib/calc-engine.typ": show-calc, …`, to be edited in every section concerned whenever a command was added or renamed (v0.38's rename was the latest case). What changed:
  - New `000-TypstRapport/commands.typ`, one line of code: `#import "000-Python/lib/calc-engine.typ": *`. A Typst module exports every top-level binding, those an import created included, so a file importing `commands.typ` with `*` gets every binding of the engine.
  - `Sections/001`–`007` start with `#import "../commands.typ": *`. 002, 003 and 004 had their import under the heading, and 006 and 007 had none. `Statischer Rapport.typ`'s three imports, of `report-level`, `uniform-level` and `number-digits`, became one `#import "commands.typ": *` beside the `config.typ` import.
  - `build_reference.py`: `import_line()` writes `#import "../commands.typ": *` from `COMMANDS_IMPORT_PATH`; `ENGINE_IMPORT_PATH` and `IMPORT_NAMES` are gone. The Katalog's loads, polar moments and plan tabs show and copy that line with no edit of their own. `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` carries no import line, so it did not change.

  Verified:
  - A copy of the report taken before the change and one taken after render pixel-identical at 50 ppi: its 12 pages as written and the 14 of its uniform level-3 variant. (Both copies use `Images/Situation.png`, which had just replaced the `.jpg` `Sections/001` still named.)
  - A file importing only `commands.typ` reaches all 87 top-level bindings of `calc-engine.typ`, `moisture-class-of("T001")` among them. A name the engine does not have still fails, with «unknown variable».
  - The real report compiles as written and at uniform levels 1 and 3.
  - None of the 87 names is a Typst global; `fmt`, the only one without a hyphen, is not one.
  - `import_line()` returns the new line, and `build_reference.py`, `catalog_gui.py` and `plan_tab.py` compile.

- **v0.39** (2026-09-11) — The position plans, reworked after the engineer's first use of them (§3.3sexies, §10bis, §10quater, §11). Six requests, each fixed where it arose.

  **In the Katalog** (`plan_canvas.py`, `plan_tab.py`):
  - *The zoom went to the sheet's corner.* Qt's AnchorUnderMouse anchors on the last point its own `mouseMoveEvent` saw, and the canvas handles its moves without calling it, so that point stayed (0, 0). `zoom_at()` now keeps the point under the pointer where it is, the anchor being `NoAnchor`.
  - *The PDF was blurred.* The whole page is rendered once at 2500 px, and the part in view at the screen's resolution each time the view stops (`sharpen`, a `PageTile` drawn pixel for pixel); the whole page used to be re-rendered at each zoom, up to 9000 px. QtPdf 6.11 scales a clipped render by `right() - left()` of its rectangle, a pixel short of its width (read in its source). The rectangle is therefore asked a pixel larger, and `clip_bias()` measures once, on a line it draws in a PDF of its own, whether the Qt at hand needs that.
  - *A PDF of several pages.* *Nouveau* opens `PageDialog` on such a PDF, its pages as thumbnails with their numbers. Several ticked pages make as many plans, saved and exported at once (`create_plans`); one opens as the draft. *Pages…* opens the same dialog for another page of the open plan. A plan without a name is listed with its file and page.
  - *Axis order.* `plan.insert_axis()` replaces `next_axis_label()`: a new axis — drawn, or *Axe parallèle* — is named by where it lies. Axes after it move up to the next gap. The direction is read from the axes already there: letters left to right and numbers bottom to top by default, an oblique axis placed where it crosses the crop's middle. Names of another form, and a family already out of order, are left alone. The status line names the renaming; the element list shows the axes in name order.
  - Session 32 also made the page spin box turn as in a document, down to the next page (`PageSpinBox`), at the engineer's request.

  **In the report** (`plan_svg.py`, `plan.py`, `lib/calc-engine.typ`):
  - *The scale bar hid part of the plan.* It is gone from the symbols' SVG. `show-plan` draws it under the plan with `plan-scale-bar`, flush left, from the printed scale, not much under 2.5 cm, and gains `scale-bar:` (false in `calc-block`'s details).
  - *The legend was coarse.* `plan_svg.legend()` and `<id>-legende.svg` are gone. `legend_items()` gives each line's key, text and a 10 × 3.6 mm swatch in fine lines, independent of the plan's sliders. `plan-legend` sets them beside their text in the report's typeface, at 8 pt, in as many columns as fit without wrapping. The texts say on their own what STE's headings said (`Wand, aussteifend`), and a concrete slab gets its own line apart from a concrete wall. `show-plan-legend` gains `width:`.
  - `data/plans.json` is schema 1.1, its `legend` a list of `{key, text, swatch}` (`plans.schema.json`, `validate_plans_business`). A 1.0 record, from a Katalog still running the old code, is shown with its old legend. That Katalog cannot save until restarted, since it validates against the schema on disk.

  Verified:
  - `insert_axis` on 14 cases: the engineer's example A, B → A, B, C with B → C; a gap kept; a chain shifted; before the first; after the last; numbers both ways; an oblique axis; odd names; a family out of order.
  - An off-screen run of the window against a scratch project, 33 checks. The point under the pointer stays within 0.8 px over six zooms in and out. The view's render is at the view's scale, covers it and hides the whole page's. It equals PDFium's whole-page render at the same scale — mean difference 0.000 on the architect's page, 0.002 on a page turned by /Rotate 90 with a crop box. The 25 thumbnails of `02_Total Plane` arrive; three pages ticked make P01–P03, saved and exported; a page is changed from the thumbnails. Axes drawn and parallel are named as above, and undo restores the names. Screenshots with Windows' own fonts were checked for the dialog and the zoomed plan.
  - The report renders the plan with its scale bar and legend at full width, at 60% (one column) and as a detail (a 1 m bar); `calc-block(plan:)` shows no scale bar; a 1.0 legend is shown as before. The real report compiles with the engineer's plans.

As in v0.32, no alias is kept: two names for one command would both be typed in `Sections/` and both have to be kept working, so a section still calling the old name stops compiling on an unresolved import instead. What changed:

- **v0.37** (2026-09-11) — A connection's forces in the most unfavourable superposition (§3.3quinquies, §3.3, §10bis). The engineer asked for a second table of the forces in the most unfavourable cases, and set its rule: N, V and the force induced by M are added as if all three acted in the positive direction, never subtracted, so that SD_01's four fasteners carry the same force. That rule was read component by component, with magnitudes: F*_x,i = (|N_x| + |V_x|)/n + |M|·|Δz_i|/I_p, F*_z,i = (|N_z| + |V_z|)/n + |M|·|Δx_i|/I_p, F*_i = √(F*_x,i² + F*_z,i²). Adding the three magnitudes as if they were collinear would give 67.926 kN on SD_01, more than any sign of the three forces can produce; the componentwise sum gives 65.293 kN, the force of its governing fastener. **Typst.**
  - `show-fastener-polar-moment` prints the new table at level 3 after *Kräfte in den Verbindungsmitteln*: *Kräfte in den Verbindungsmitteln, ungünstigste Überlagerung*, with its three formulas in letters above it, the columns Nr., F*_x, F*_z, F*, the F* column and the governing rows bold, and a short note. There is no force-grain angle, the direction of such a force not being defined. Without a lever arm the M terms are left out, as in the formulas above it. Levels 1 and 2 are unchanged.
  - `render-table`'s `formula` may be a list, one row each, and a table without a `norm` sets it without a citation. Tables read from the data always carry a string and a norm, so they are unchanged.
  - A formula taller than an ordinary one-line formula — a fraction — is moved down by the difference plus 0.25 em. Before, a fraction's numerator ran into the title's descenders, since a line is measured to the text's cap height. Nothing else moves.

  Verified:
  - On SD_01-8mm (N = 20 kN, V = 16 kN, M = 20 kNm) the table gives 46.667, 45.667 and 65.293 kN in every row. That equals fastener 3 of the vector table, and an independent Python computation gives the same.
  - On a test connection with five fasteners laid out irregularly, a fixed centre, N and V at 23° and 131°, V and M negative, the five rows agree with that Python computation to the printed digit.
  - Without a moment, every fastener of that group carries the same force.
  - At level 2 the table is absent.
  - The scratch copy's uniform level-3 report is pixel-identical to the previous build on every page but the connection's, the wind tables with their formulas included.

- **v0.36** (2026-09-11) — Every element carries its moisture class (§3.1, §7, §10bis, §14). The engineer added a column `Wood moisture class` to `Positionen.xlsx`'s `Positions Element` sheet, between `units` and `description`, so that the calculations can be adjusted to the class the timber is used in; the project's three elements hold 1. **Python.**
  - `export_all.py`: `_moisture_class()` reads the column, pinned as `MOISTURE_COLUMN`, into `moisture_class`: 1, 2 or 3, typed as a number or as text, `null` for a blank cell. Anything else stops the export naming the position; a sheet without the column stops it naming the headers found. Positions are `schema_version` 1.2.
  - `validation.py`: `MOISTURE_CLASSES`, checked again in `validate_positions_business`. The schema walker gained an `integer` type, and refuses a boolean for it as it does for a number.
  - `schemas/position.schema.json`: `moisture_class`, integer or null, required.
  - `tools/build_example_input.py`: a new workbook's Positions Element gets the column, with a 1/2/3 dropdown. The engineer's workbook was not written to: openpyxl would drop the dropdowns of `Positions Connectors`, which are Excel extensions it cannot keep.

  **Typst.** `show-position` prints `Feuchteklasse 1` after the section, and nothing where the class is blank; `moisture-class-of(position)` returns it, for a calculation written in Typst.

  Verified:
  - ten inputs accepted (1, 2, 3, 1.0, 3.0, "2", " 3 ", an empty cell, "", spaces) and eight refused with their message (0, 4, 2.5, "x", "FK 1", "1.0", TRUE, −1); a sheet without the column refused; a record with 4, a boolean or the text "1" refused by the schema or the business rule;
  - the seed script, pointed at a scratch file, writes the column and its dropdown, and its rows export with their class;
  - the real export passes and writes `moisture_class: 1` for T001, S001 and D001;
  - the whole report compiles; its Baustoffe list and T001's calc-block header read «Feuchteklasse 1», a position without a class prints none, and `moisture-class-of` returns the integer 1 and refuses an unknown id listing the valid ones.

Until now the dialog was modal and filled the table only on OK. `GridDialog` now emits `changed` on every edit of a field. `PolarTab.grid()` puts each change into the table while `loading` is set, so a preview is not taken for an edit, and redraws the figure, n, I_p and the trial forces after an 80 ms pause, as the form does. OK keeps the group and marks the draft unsaved; *Annuler* restores the table and leaves the draft as it was. The dialog opens to the left of the figure (`place_beside_figure`), and from the last grid accepted in this window (`grid_values`); it opened on a 3 × 2 group every time before. Verified by a scripted off-screen run, 14 checks:

  - typing 4 × 3 gives 12 fasteners in the table, the figure and n;
  - a wider spacing changes I_p at once;

The form's id became a connector combo with its summary, a variant field and the id they make; choosing a connector for an unlinked calculation gives it that connector's first free variant.
