- **v0.32** (2026-09-11) — Connections in a reference member's frame, with any number of members, the report's forces drawn on the figure, and every table centred (§2 rule 7, §3.3, §3.3quinquies, §10, §10bis, §10ter, §10quater, §13). Asked for by the engineer after using v0.31 on the project's A01. Agreed with them before starting: level 1 unchanged; the origin at the node; angles from x towards z, M positive anticlockwise; the reference cited once, beside the heading. **Frame and source.** Coordinates are x along the grain of a reference member picked in the Katalog and z a quarter clockwise from it (down when x points right), every angle counted from x towards z, M positive from z towards x, so F_x,i = (N_x + V_x)/n + M·Δz_i/I_p and F_z,i = (N_z + V_z)/n − M·Δx_i/I_p. `connections/<id>.json` goes to `schema_version` 2.0: `reference`; `orientation` as `{member, angle}`, giving the drawing's direction by any member; two to six `members`, each with its `angle` from member 1 and, when it is not `through`, the member it stops `against` and an `overhang` in mm beyond that member's far edge; fasteners and a fixed centre in x/z. `connection.upgrade_source()` converts a 1.0 file exactly as it is read, into its member 1's frame. The project's A01 was converted and saved as 2.0. Its «SIA 265 §1» was replaced by the new default reference, `DEFAULT_NORM`, the bauforumstahl handbook *Tragwerksplanung im Stahlbau – Gelenkige Anschlüsse*. A document has no clause, so `engine.py` now requires `norm` alone, `norm_ref` being any string, and `norm-text` prints a citation without a clause without its `§`. **Python.** `connection.py`: `check_source` for 2.0; `local_angles`/`page_angles`; I_p,z in place of I_p,y; `fastener_forces` returning every fastener tied at the maximum (relative tolerance 1e-9); `number_ranges`; a record carrying the members with their `local` and `page` angles, the reference, and a `figure` block (file, fastener positions, the SVG's e_x and e_z, the arrow length and sizes); export 2.0. `connection_svg.py` rewritten: n members at their page angles, a stopping member cut parallel to the far edge of the one it stops against plus its overhang, labels with the dimension drawn in bold, the x/z pair at the start of the reference member, M's arc at the centre, a legend with N and V in their real directions, a `<!-- forces -->` marker, and `force_arrows()` for the Katalog's preview. `validation.py` and both schemas follow 2.0. **Typst.** `show-fastener-forces` is renamed `show-fastener-polar-moment`, with no alias (`Sections/005` and its `#import` updated). It inserts the arrows into the figure (`svg-arrow`, `fastener-arrows`, `svg-number`). It names ties (`number-ranges`). It cites the reference once, beside its heading, and sets its lines ragged. Its levels follow the engineer's order: level 2 has the figure, then n and I_p, the forces, *Massgebend* and β₁ … β_m. Level 3 opens on the rotation centre with its working (`x_c = (5·(−70) + 5·0 + 5·70)/15`), the positions table and the angles between the members, and adds β_r = arctan(|F_z|/|F_x|) in numbers in place of θ, and a forces table of F_x, F_z, β₁ … β_m and F, with the F column and the governing rows bold. `render-table` centres every table in the space it has, the calculation's column when it fits there and the full width otherwise; it gained `bold-columns:`, and a note kept to a narrow table's width is set ragged. **Katalog.** Member groups can be added and removed. There is a reference-member combo, an orientation member, against and overhang, and x/z in the table, the grid (columns along x by default, numbered from the top row) and the fixed centre. The preview shows ties, a β per member and the trial arrows in the figure, and the default reference has no clause. The copied call is renamed. **Elsewhere.** `build_reference.py` has `call_show_fastener_polar_moment` and the renamed import name, and its Connections sheet shows I_p,z. `README.md` follows. Verified:
  - The HB2 sheet's 3 × 5 group, in the Nebenträger's frame, gives 157 000 mm² and, for 7.5 / 25 kN / 6.6 kNm, 7.21 kN at dowel 13 (F_x = 5.545 kN, F_z = 4.609 kN), at β = 39.7° to the Nebenträger and 50.3° to the Hauptträger.
  - M alone names «1, 3, 13, 15» on that group and «1–4» on A01, with the working shown for the first.
  - A01 converted from 1.0 gives every |F_i| of v0.31 unchanged under the report's N = 20, V = 16, M = 20.
  - On a three-member test connection (reference 2, overhang 20 mm, fixed centre (70, 10), N 12, V 18, M −4), Typst and Python agree on every fastener, on the governing one (4, 12.818 kN) and on the three β.
  - Their arrows and legend notes are identical, coordinate for coordinate and word for word, on that connection and on A01 from M = 0 to 20 kNm, with N or V zero too. Every resultant's length is k·F, and every share's is k times its size or its minimum.
  - The real `export_all.py` passes, the loads and positions byte-identical and `data/connections.json` in 2.0.
  - The report compiles; its load pages render pixel-identical to v0.31's, the local wind table still breaking across pages with its header repeated.
  - The 801 load calls the Katalog offers and 70 connection calls compile together.
  - A scripted off-screen run of the window passed 28 checks. Among them: a 1.0 file converted on load and saved as 2.0, the trial preview, ties, the renamed call, a save without a clause, a refusal without a reference, the grid's order, members added and removed, reference and overhang, a draft with three members surviving the language switch, a fixed centre, and closing with unsaved changes.
  - `.\build.ps1` rebuilt `Statischer Rapport.pdf` with the renamed call in `Sections/005`.
  - One refinement came after the engineer's first use, folded in here: *as soon as a moment is added, the red and blue arrows disappear*. At one scale, A01's N/n of 5 kN beside resultants of 124 kN was 2 drawing units long, under a fastener dot of radius 10. Given the choice (§13), the engineer kept one scale with a minimum. N/n and V/n are never drawn shorter than half the longest arrow (`SHARE_MIN`/`share-min` = 0.5). Their heads are 0.7 of the resultants' (`SHARE_HEAD`/`share-head`), and they are drawn over the resultants rather than under them. The exported figure gained a second marker, `<!-- note -->`, in a new last line of the legend; the report (`share-note`) and the Katalog (`share_note`) write into it which shares are enlarged, with their values. Rendered in both: with M = 20 kNm the blue and red arrows show on every fastener and the legend names them, and with M = 0 nothing is enlarged.
  - A second refinement, also folded in here: *more vertical space before and after the tables, and the same font size in them as in the text*. `render-table` no longer sets its cells at 8 pt nor its title at 9 pt; both inherit the document's 11 pt, and the note stays at 7.5 pt. Its block keeps `table-spacing` = 1.6 em above and below, measured at 17.6 pt against the 6.6 pt and 8.8 pt it had. A table opening a calculation under its heading keeps that heading's 0.7 em, through a new `above:` parameter that `show-calc` passes. The larger text made the local wind table of `WL-Velo` miss the foot of page 6, and its title and formula were left alone there. So a table shorter than a page is now one unbreakable block, moving to the next page whole. The headings of `show-calc` and `show-fastener-polar-moment` became `sticky`, and the connection's rotation-centre lines one unbreakable block. Verified on the report and on its uniform level-3 variant: every table sits inside the margins at 11 pt, the eleven-column local wind table included. No table, title or heading is left alone at the foot of a page. The layer build-ups keep to their headings. The 801 load calls and 70 connection calls still compile together. The load pages are no longer those of v0.31, by design.

- **v0.31** (2026-09-11) — Connections: the Katalog's polar moments tab, and `show-fastener-forces` (§3.3quinquies, §10bis, §10quater, §13). The engineer asked for it with the course's method (HB2 sheet «Assemblage par effort tranchant», Maeder's slides on biegesteife Anschlüsse): the two sections and the angle between them drawn, the fasteners placed by coordinates, the rotation centre calculated or fixed, the directions of N and V as coloured arrows, I_p,x, I_p,y and I_p with the figure as the result, and a Typst command taking the connection's id, N, V and M that gives the force in the fasteners at three levels; plus four additions offered and accepted — a grid generator, the force-grain angle, M's arc in the figure, and a force preview in the window. New: `calc/domains/connection.py` (source checks, geometry, the record, `prepare`/`write`/`export`, and `fastener_forces` for the preview), `calc/domains/connection_svg.py` (the figure, as SVG text, Tiny 1.2-safe), `schemas/connection-source.schema.json` and `schemas/connections.schema.json`, and the source folder `000-Python/connections/`. `export_all.py` prepares the connections with everything else and writes `data/connections.json` and `data/connections/<id>.svg` before the reference workbook; a source that does not validate stops the export with `data/` as it was. `validation.py`: `validate_connections_business()`, and the table rules moved into one `_check_tables()` shared by the loads and the connections, a `value` column now also accepting plain numbers (a string must still name a var). `lib/calc-engine.typ`: `load-connections`, `connection-ids`, the `connection` branch of `find-calculation` (before the position branch, no scope guard), `instance-ids("connection")`, `show-fastener-forces` with its helpers (`number-in-formula`, `quantity-in-formula` — a real minus sign in quoted numbers —, `force-line-angle`, `grain-angle`, `angle-digits`), and an optional `bold-rows:` on `render-table`. `build_reference.py`: `call_show_fastener_forces`, `show-fastener-forces`/`connection-ids` in the import line, connections in `iter_sources()`, a `Connections` sheet. `catalog_gui.py`: `PolarTab` and `GridDialog` in place of the polar `FutureTab`, 72 new strings in each language (one retired), `Catalog.rebuild()` keeping every tab's `view_state()` by key (it kept the loads tab's only), and `closeEvent` asking about unsaved connections; the window now imports `PySide6.QtSvgWidgets` and, from `calc/`, `engine`, `validation` and the two connection modules. `README.md` and `Typst-Katalog.bat`'s comment follow. Verified: the HB2 example gives I_p = 49 000 + 108 000 = 157 000 mm² and, for N = 7.5 kN, V = 25 kN, M = 6.6 kNm, F_x = 5.545 kN, F_y = 4.609 kN, F = 7.21 kN at β = 39.7°, in the window's preview and in the report alike; on an irregular five-fastener group with a fixed centre, orientations of 17° and 121°, and N, V, M of mixed signs, Python and Typst agree on every fastener's F_x, F_y and F, on the governing fastener and on both grain angles; the report renders at levels 1, 2 and 3 and under `--input uniform=1`, which overrides a call's `level: 3`; each Typst guard fires with its message (unknown id, level 4, a var in kN/m², a text force, M on a single fastener at the centre) while a moment-free call on that group compiles; nine malformed sources, a file named after another id and broken JSON are each refused naming the file and field; `export_all.py` on a scratch copy of the project refuses a broken source leaving `data/calculations.json` and `data/connections.json` byte-identical, and on the real project passes with the loads and positions byte-identical and an empty `data/connections.json`; the whole report rebuilt with the new engine is byte-identical to the last build (10 September, 22:29) in every page's content, the only differences being the creation date's encoding and the document id derived from it, for the deliverable and the three uniform variants; the 801 calls the loads tab can copy and 169 connection calls compile together; QtSvg and Typst render the same figure; and a scripted run of the window off screen passed 35 checks — load, trial forces, the copied call, new with a suggested id, a section from T001, save refused without a clause and with a row that is not a number, save and export, a draft surviving the switch to German with its bullet, Cancel and Discard when choosing another connection, rename with its file and figure replaced, an id clash, a fixed centre, delete, and closing with unsaved changes cancelled then discarded — and the empty state on the real project. Not done here: nothing was entered as a connection of this project, so `connections/` does not exist until the first *Enregistrer*.

- **v0.30** (2026-09-10) — The Typst-Katalog moves from tkinter to PySide6 and gets an icon, in its title bar and on the taskbar (§10quater, §13). `calc/tools/catalog_gui.py` was rewritten from its `# --- The window` line on and not above it. `STRINGS` (one key added, `close_after_export`), the export reader, `table_text` and the `*_commands` functions are what they were, so the window lists and copies exactly what it did, v0.29's `tables` included. The mapping is one widget for one. `QMainWindow`, `QTabWidget` and `QSplitter` for the frame. A `QTreeWidget` whose rows carry their iid. A `QTextBrowser` for the details, in HTML now: the fields share one two-column table so their values align as the tk tab stop aligned them, formulas and tables are set in `<pre>`, and the field names are `nowrap`, since Qt otherwise squeezed them to a letter a line beside a long formula. `QLineEdit`s and *Copier* buttons for the calls, a `QMenu` for the right-click, `QShortcut`s for Ctrl+C and Return. The re-export runs in a `QProcess`, with the same interpreter, environment, folder and ten-minute limit, instead of a thread polling a queue. Removed with tkinter: the DPI call and the pixel scaling, which Qt does per screen. New behaviour: closing during an export waits for it; a search no longer folds the rows it hid; a size remembered by the tk window is clamped to the screen; secondary text takes the palette's grey, for Windows' dark mode; calls and formulas are set in Consolas, found by asking the font rather than by listing every family, which alone took a second here. The icon is `calc/tools/typst-katalog.svg` (new). `main()` sets it on the application, sets the process's AppUserModelID (`APP_ID`) before any window exists, and `claim_taskbar_icon()` hands the icon to the window class before the window is shown. A missing PySide6 is reported in a Windows message box. `Typst-Katalog.bat` is unchanged but for its comment, and `README.md` says the window needs PySide6. Verified: all 801 calls, the 18 tables' text and every formatted value the PySide6 module produces for the current data are identical to the tkinter module's. A scripted run of the window passed 54 checks, off screen and with the export pointed at stand-in scripts so that nothing was written to `data/`: selection and details, double-click, Ctrl+C, Return, the right-click menu, search, results-only, the language switch keeping tab, search, open rows and selection, an export succeeding with a warning, failing, failing to start and closed during, remembered sizes, and missing data. Screenshots were checked in all three languages. The copied text outlived the process, and the export's child, started from `pythonw`, had no console window. Real launches showed the icon in the title bar. On the taskbar, alternating launches with and without `claim_taskbar_icon()` gave the generic icon three times out of three without it and the Katalog's three times out of three with it. `Typst-Katalog.bat` opened the window under `pythonw.exe`, with the icon on its taskbar button.

- **v0.29** (2026-09-10) — The wind load's checking report gains the local wind forces (§3.3quater): a level-3 table after the global forces giving, for each surface (walls A–D, roof zones E–H) and each opening case — *gleichmässig* verteilte Öffnungen, and a dominant *Öffnung* in wall A, B, C or D — the largest and the smallest `q_k = q_p · (c_pe − c_pi)` over every wind direction the sheet lists. Levels 1 and 2 are unchanged. The sheet's own «Lokale Windkräfte» block combines the coefficients for one selected direction and opening only, so the envelope is computed in `wind.py` — that module's first arithmetic, and the sheet's own formula applied to its own coefficient block (`B77:P81`), the directions read rather than assumed (figure 42 lists 180°, not 45°) and uniform openings taking both of the sheet's c_pi. `_check_against_sheet` compares it with the sheet's `F90:F98` wherever `G85`/`G86` hold a selection and blocks on any disagreement, naming the cell (a changed formula, or a workbook saved without recalculating); a selection the sheet itself shows as an Excel error is only a warning (§13). To carry a second table, **an instance's `table` became `tables`, a list** (`schema_version` `1.2` → `1.3`), each keeping its own `level` and `place` and printed in list order on its side: `show-calc` renders every shown table, `validation.py` checks each one and refuses the old singular key (the display layer would drop it without a word), and a table may carry a `source`, set on the new table and on the dead load's layer build-up, both computed in Python. `docs/typst-reference.xlsx`'s `Calculations` sheet counts `num_tables` instead of `has_table`, and the Typst-Katalog lists every table of an instance, a grouped column's header leading with its group. `render-table` marks its header rows as a `table.header`, so a table breaking across a page repeats them on the next — the new table's first render split after Wand C and continued as five rows of unlabelled numbers; a table that does not break renders byte-identically to before. Verified: the export passes on both workbooks with no warning, the two sheets holding a selection (`WL`, `WL-Velo`) agreeing with their own block to 1e-9; the local tables checked by hand against the coefficient block (`WL`, Wand A, gleichmässig: (0.7 + 0.1) · 1.363 = 1.090 and (−0.35 − 0.1) · 1.363 = −0.613); the report compiles at all three uniform levels, the table appearing at level 3 only and fitting the A4 text width; the dead-load build-ups render as before; and each guard fired deliberately against the real `WL` sheet — a disagreeing `F92`, a stale selection, `#N/A` under a selection (warning only), a moved header, a blank required c_pi, a text coefficient — with a blank `glm. (-)` skipped as intended. ⚠️ The formula is cited as `SIA 261 §6.3`, the chapter the global forces already cite; the exact clause of the local-pressure combination is worth confirming against the norm.

- **v0.28** (2026-09-10) — The Typst-Katalog (§10quater): `Typst-Katalog.bat` at the top of the project folder opens `calc/tools/catalog_gui.py`, a tkinter window listing every load value — grouped into permanent and variable actions, then by domain, case and var, with its value, unit, level, norm and the workbook/sheet/cell it comes from — and copying the Typst call that prints it (`show-input`, `inline-value`, a `var-of`/`value-of` binding, `show-calc` at any level or without title, the `instance-ids` loop, and the `#import` line they need): double-click copies the main one, right-click lists them all. French, German or English, switched live and remembered per user in `%APPDATA%`; a search box and a results-only switch; a banner when a load workbook is newer than the export; *Réexporter depuis Excel* reruns `export_all.py` and shows its own error on failure; *Ouvrir le classeur* opens the case's workbook. Three reserved tabs — load combinations, connections, polar moments — say they are not implemented yet (§14). Two changes outside the window. Each load domain module declares `ACTION` (`permanent` for `dead_load.py`, `variable` for `snow.py` and `wind.py`), `registry.py` exports it on the domain, the schema requires it (`schema_version` 1.1 → 1.2) and `validation.py` refuses anything but those two values (§3.3, §13). And `build_reference.py` gained the `call_*` functions every Typst call is now spelled by — its own `example_call` columns went over to them — plus an `action` column on the `Calculations` sheet (§10ter). Verified: the export passes, and `data/calculations.json` is identical to the previous export apart from `action` and `schema_version`, `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` apart from the new column; a misspelt or missing `action` stops the export naming the domain; all 801 calls the window can produce for the current data compile together in one Typst file; the report compiles; and the window was driven in all three languages — selection, copy to the clipboard, search, results-only, language switch keeping the selection, re-export succeeding and failing, and the stale banner.

- **v0.27** (2026-09-10) — The Makefile §11-§12 had described since v0.23 is written, as `000-TypstRapport/000-Python/Makefile`, now that the engineer's machine has `make` (GNU Make 4.4.1, winget's `ezwinports.make`). The tree had never received it: `b0868e0` deleted the two pre-v0.23 Makefiles, which still compiled `main.typ` and had no `.PHONY`, and added none, so every `make` line in the documentation pointed at nothing. It follows `build.ps1`'s v0.23 semantics rather than the snippet §12 carried, whose `report` forced `LEVEL = 3` — the very default v0.23 took out of the script for making every per-call override invisible: `make` is the report as written, `LEVEL=N` forces the base, `UNIFORM=N` renders uniformly, `report-1|2|3` write the uniform variants to `out/`, `all` is `-AllLevels` after one export, `SKIP_DATA=1` is `-SkipData`. `LEVEL` together with `UNIFORM`, a level outside 1-3, and a run from a folder its relative paths do not resolve from each stop make before anything executes. Every target is phony, because `data/` and `out/` are directories and `Statischer Rapport.pdf` has a space make cannot take in a file target, so like the script it is a command runner and not an incremental build. Recipes are plain `program "path"` lines, because make on Windows runs them with `cmd.exe` when started from PowerShell and with `sh` when started from Git Bash. §12 and `README.md` now show the real commands. Verified on a scratch copy of the project: `make all` from PowerShell and a compile-only run from Git Bash both succeed; the four refusals stop with their messages; and every PDF `make all` wrote is byte-identical to `build.ps1 -AllLevels`'s on the same copy, except for its `/CreationDate` and `/ModDate`.

- **v0.26** (2026-09-10) — The load workbooks are found by name instead of being one fixed file (§2, §3.3, §11, §13). Every `.xlsm`/`.xlsx` directly in the project folder whose name contains `Belastungsannahmen` is now read, and in each of them the sheets starting with `WL` (wind), `SL` (snow) and `EL+AL` (permanent loads) are the domains' instances, exactly as before. Prompted by the project folder gaining a real project's `02_Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` beside the example `Belastungsannahmen.xlsm`: the fixed path read only the second, and the first could not have been read without renaming it. `io_excel.find_workbooks()` applies the pattern — case-insensitively, skipping Excel's `~$` lock files (which match the pattern for as long as the workbook is open), not descending into subfolders, in file-name order — and `registry.open_workbooks()` opens what it finds, a folder with no match being a blocking error. `export_all.py` lost its `INPUT_XLSM` constant and prints the workbooks it took. `registry._discover()` walks the workbooks one by one and adds one rule: a sheet name found in two workbooks is refused with both file names, since the sheet name is the instance id the report addresses; the prefix collision it already refused is now checked per workbook. `read_instances()` is called once per workbook, so each sheet is read against its own workbook's lookup tables, and not at all for a workbook with no sheet of that domain (the dead load's material library would otherwise be demanded of a file that need not have one). **The workbook moved from the domain to the instance** in `data/calculations.json` (`schema_version` `1.0` → `1.1`, the schema's `required` lists following), and `docs/typst-reference.xlsx`'s `Calculations` sheet gained a `workbook` column. The domain modules had `Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` typed into every `source` and every error message — 16 places across `snow.py`, `wind.py` and `dead_load.py` — which would have credited the new workbook's cells to the old file; they all go through a new `CellReader.address(sheet, ref)` now (`02_Belastungsannahmen.xlsm!SL!H17`), and `dead_load.MaterialLibrary` holds the reader of the workbook it was read from. Verified: `export_all.py` exports 16 instances from the two workbooks — the eight of `Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` byte-identical to the previous export but for their new `workbook` field, the eight of `02_Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` (`SL`; `WL-Velo`, `WL-Fluchttreppe+Geräte`, `WL-Pavillon`; `EL+AL-Dach mit Wasser`, `EL+AL-Dach ohne Wasser`, `EL+AL-DachRand`, `EL+AL-Wand`) read by the unchanged readers with no layout difference to follow; `.\build.ps1 -AllLevels` compiles the report and all three levels, the new cases rendering like the old; and against scratch workbooks, a lower-case `.XLSM` and an `.xlsx` are matched while an `.xls`, a `.pdf`, a `~$` lock file and a copy in a subfolder are not, a sheet name shared by two workbooks and a sheet matching two prefixes are both refused by name, a folder with no load workbook fails the export, and a domain empty in every workbook only warns. ⚠️ Found on the way, left standing because it is in the workbook: the three WL sheets of `02_Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` carry the same `L70` defect as `WL` (§3.3quater).

- **v0.25** (2026-08-31) — A third load domain, `wind-load`, and the table shape its answer needed (§3.3quater, §3.3, §10bis, §13). `calc/domains/wind.py` reads the `WL-*` sheets of `Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` the way `snow.py` reads the `SL-*` ones — cell addresses, no arithmetic, `ENGINE = "excel"` — and `registry.py`'s `DOMAIN_MODULES` gained one entry, which is the whole of "adding a domain" (§11). Twelve values are read: q_p0, the building's b/d/h under one **Gebäude Geometrie** row, the Geländekategorie (its `choices` read from the workbook's own `Dropdowns` table, each category listed with the z_g and α_r it implies), z_g, α_r, z, c_h, the two reduction factors, c_d, the Staudruck q_p, and the six global wind forces. The requested level split lives entirely in their `level` fields: level 1 the Geländekategorie and the forces, level 2 adding q_p0, z_g and the two c_red, level 3 the sheet entire. **The answer is where this domain differs**: a global wind force is a maximum and a minimum for each of three reference areas, three loads on three faces and not three candidates to maximise over, so the calculation ends on a table — `q_k = c_red · c_d · c_f · q_p` printed once in letters, its six results the cells below it. That put four optional fields on `table`, each stating something the display layer cannot infer (§3.3): **`level`** (the dead load's build-up is the working, shown at 3; these forces are the answer, shown from 1), **`place`** (`above` the values it explains, `below` the values leading to it), **`formula`** with its `norm`/`norm_ref` (set in letters above the table, in the same citation column as every other formula), and columns of kind **`value`**, whose cells name a var rather than repeat its number — so the table cannot drift from what it shows, `show-calc` leaves those vars off rows of their own, and one that is a `result` comes out bold. A column may carry a **`group`** too, naming a header line spanning it and its consecutive neighbours (`Bezugsfläche` over b·h, d·h, d·b). On the step side, one field: **`bold`**, which says a step's numbers line is an answer though its result is not in `results` (q_p, which the sheet itself restates above the forces). `show-calc` reads *both* its bold line and its absorbed-operand rule from one `is-answer` predicate now, so a step the data calls an answer keeps its operands' rows — c_h and q_p0 stay visible — exactly as a `results` step does. `validation.py` gained the checks these need (an unknown `place`, a `level` outside 1-3, a `formula` with nothing to cite beside it, a `value` cell naming a var that does not exist) and **lost one it was wrong to have**: a result may be negative, a global wind force on a roof being an uplift and a wall's minimum a suction. `Sections/004-einwirkungen.typ` gained a `== Windlast` loop over `instance-ids("wind-load")`, so a copied sheet reaches the report on its own. Verified: the `WL` sheet renders at all three levels exactly as asked (level 1 = Geländekategorie, formula, table; level 2 adding q_p0, z_g, c_red,b/h, c_red,d/h; level 3 the twelve values in order, q_p bold), the whole report compiles at every level with the table fitting A4, and `snow-load` and `dead-load` render **byte-identically** before and after the display-layer changes — both SL sheets and all six EL+AL build-ups, at all three levels, compared as rendered images. ⚠️ One thing left standing, and it is in the workbook rather than in this code: `WL!L70` computes the d·b minimum as `J19 · MIN(S79:S81)` — the reduction factor c_red,b/h where the Staudruck q_p belongs, over a range one row shorter than its neighbours'. The −0.391 kN/m² in the report is therefore the sheet's own number rather than `q_p · min c_f`, and the fix belongs in Excel. (The v0.24 `SL-Dach2` guard blocked `export_all.py` for most of this work; the sheet was removed from the workbook while it was under way, so the full pipeline — `.uild.ps1 -AllLevels`, Excel through to all three PDFs — runs clean again and produced everything in `data/`, `docs/` and `out/`.)

- **v0.24** (2026-08-31) — `SheddachLM2` is read, and a Dachformbeiwert is no longer taken on trust (§3.3bis, §14). The load model's third characteristic load, q_(k,2m) in `I39`, was refused outright by `snow.py`; it is read now, together with its coefficient μ₂m, so `SL-Dach2` exports three results (`q_k11`, `q_k12`, `q_k2m`) and `show-calc` prints them as three formula-and-numbers pairs — no display change was needed, `results` having been a list since v0.18. μ₂m has no value cell on the sheet (`I35` labels it, the template leaves `K35` blank), so it is read from `K35` where the sheet states it and from the helper block where it does not, `source` recording which. The refusal is **generalized rather than removed**: `RESULT_CELLS` lists every cell an SL sheet can compute a load in, and one holding a number that this module does not read is refused by name. That closes the silent half of the same hole — `GiebeldachLM2`'s `N37`/`N38` were not merely unread, nothing was looking at them — and it asks the *cells* which loads exist instead of matching the load model's name, so the dropdown cannot gain an entry the exporter quietly ignores. Second finding, which is why the export still stops on `SL-Dach2`: **the workbook's μ block is not copy-safe.** `H26`/`H27` do not compute the Dachformbeiwerte, they read them off one helper block on `Dropdowns` whose formulas are pinned to `'SL-Dach1'!H23`/`H24` — so a copied sheet shows its own Dachneigungen and reports the original's μ, which no value read on its own can reveal. `io_excel`'s `CellReader` gained `formula()` (a lazily-parsed second read of the workbook, the only thing that says *whose* numbers went into a shared cell) and `_check_mu_helper` blocks unless the block is anchored to the sheet being exported — applied only where the sheet actually reaches into the block, so a sheet given its own μ formulas is checked against nothing and a correct repair cannot be blocked by a stale helper. It is not hypothetical: `SL-Dach2` is a Sheddach with α₁ = 90° and α₂ = 20° reading `SL-Dach1`'s 20.18/7.8, so μ₁₁ reads 0.8 where the roof gives 0, μ₂m reads 1.173 where it gives 1.6, and **q_(k,2m) comes out 27% low, 2.028 instead of 2.767 kN/m²** (q_(k,11) and q_(k,12) happen to be unaffected, the hindered-sliding branch flooring both at 0.8). The repair is in Excel and was not made here — nothing writes to the engineer's workbook: each SL sheet needs μ formulas reading its own `H23`/`H24`, and μ₂m in `K35`. Verified three ways, since the real workbook cannot get past the guard: the reader against `SL-Dach1`/`SL-Dach2`'s actual cached cells, every value bit-identical to Excel's (q_(k,2m) = 2.0283768650884357 from `I39`); the whole registry + validation + render against a simulated repair (μ₁₁ = 0, μ₁₂ = 0.8, μ₂m = 1.6 from `K35`, q_(k,2m) = 2.767 kN/m²), all three levels laying out as intended with μ₂m joining the Dachformbeiwerte row; and both guards fired deliberately, on the real `Dropdowns!F64` and on a planted `N37`.

- **v0.23** (2026-08-31) — The file tree becomes an office project folder, and the PDF is written one level above the `.typ` that produces it (§2, §11, §12). At the top of the folder: `Positionen.xlsx` (was `input/project.xlsx`), `Belastungsannahmen.xlsm`, the built `Statischer Rapport.pdf`, and `build.ps1`. Below it, `000-TypstRapport/` holds the report (`Statischer Rapport.typ`, was `main.typ`, plus `config.typ`, `Sections/`, `Images/`) and `000-TypstRapport/000-Python/` everything generated and everything that generates it (`calc/`, `data/`, `schemas/`, `lib/`, `docs/`, `out/`, `Makefile`). `input/` is gone. **The one path change that would have failed silently**: `lib/calc-engine.typ` loaded its JSON through *absolute* Typst paths (`json("/data/...")`), which resolve from the Typst root — now `000-TypstRapport/`, which has no `data/`. They are relative (`../data/...`) now, resolved from the file that reads them, so they no longer depend on where `--root` points. On the Python side only the two workbooks actually moved relative to the code: `export_all.py` keeps `ROOT` (`000-Python/`) for `data/`/`schemas/`/`docs/`/`lib/` and adds `PROJECT_ROOT = ROOT.parent.parent` for them; `validation.py` and `build_reference.py` needed no change at all, their `ROOT` still landing on `000-Python/`. `registry.py`'s `WORKBOOK` and `bending.py`'s `excel:project.xlsx!Positions` are declared provenance (§2), so they were renamed too and `data/` regenerated — the only two lines of `data/calculations.json` that changed. New: **`build.ps1`**, the build that needs neither `make` (not on PATH here) nor VS Code — every path derived from the script's own location, and it refuses to compile if the export exits non-zero. It passes **no** `--input level=` unless asked, so the deliverable's base level is the document's own (§4) and the report is built as written, the per-call `level:` overrides in `Sections/` applying on top; `-Level 1|2|3` forces a base for one run, `-AllLevels` adds the three uniform variants in `out/`. The first draft forced level 3 by default, which made every override invisible and the deliverable a byte-for-byte duplicate of `out/report-3.pdf` — the level mechanism producing exactly what it exists to prevent. Caught by the engineer on first use; and **`.vscode/settings.json`**, setting `tinymist.outputPath` to `$dir/../$name` so the editor's on-save export lands on the same PDF as the other two routes. The `Makefile` was kept for whoever has `make`, retargeted at `../../Statischer Rapport.pdf` with its paths quoted (the name has a space), and given the `.PHONY` line it always needed: `data` and `out` are real directories, so `make summary` had been quietly skipping the export for as long as the file existed. Verified: `python calc/export_all.py` passes from the project root, `.\build.ps1 -AllLevels` writes the root PDF and all three levels, and the level-3 PDF is byte-identical to the pre-move build except for its `/CreationDate`, `/ModDate` and XMP document id — 108 bytes of metadata, no content stream touched. The scratch copy of the reorganized tree (`000-NewStructureOfTheFiles/`) was deleted once the real tree matched it. Finally, `--input uniform=N` and the `uniform-level` state (§4): `out/report-N.pdf` must be level N *throughout*, and a per-call `level:` override written in `Sections/` is a literal argument that no outside state — and no separate entry file — could disarm, so the engine had to be told. `show-input` and `show-calc` each carried their own copy of the level resolution; both now call one `resolve-level(level)`, which checks the uniform level first. `build.ps1 -AllLevels` switched from `level=N` to `uniform=N` accordingly, and gained `-Uniform N` (mutually exclusive with `-Level N`, refused with a message that says why). The difference is not cosmetic: with the dead-load loop forcing level 3, `out/report-1.pdf` went from 7 pages to 5.

- **v0.22** (2026-08-31) — `dead-load`'s level 3, reworked to the engineer's layout (§3.3, §3.3ter, §10bis). The `Schichtaufbau` table is now printed **above** the values instead of under them (it is the working, and the values are what it ends on), and the two Σ formulas for `g_k` and `a_k` are gone: the sums are two bold `totals` rows of the table itself, set under a heavier rule, each standing in the `Last` column it adds up — a Σ in letters above a table restating it row by row is the same fact stated twice. The per-layer arithmetic that used to be footnoted on those formulas is now a `note` under the table, so nothing was lost with them. The table proper: `Nr.` removed; `Art` renamed `Type` and its cells rendered as real math (`g_k`/`a_k`, not the sheet's `gk`/`ak`); `spez. Gewicht` renamed `Gewicht`, its unit carrying a real exponent (`500 kg/m³`, lifted in `_weight_text`); `Material` widened at the numeric columns' expense — the last by giving a column its **`unit`** as a field of its own (`{"label": "Dicke", "unit": "m"}`) and breaking the header between the two, so `Dicke`/`Breite`/`Dist.`/`Last` are each as narrow as their own name instead of as wide as `Dicke [m]`. The widths are not hand-tuned — each column now declares a **`kind`** (`math`, `wide`, `text`, `number`), and `render-table` gives the one `wide` column `1fr` and every other its natural width. That is the whole fix: a table of all-auto columns that overflows is shrunk *proportionally*, which is why `Dicke [m]` kept space it had no use for while `Weichfaserplatte Thermoflex` wrapped three deep. `schemas/calculations.schema.json` types the new column objects, `totals` and `note`; `validation.py` rejects an unknown `kind` and checks a `totals` row against the column count like any other row — one cell short would slide a sum under the wrong header. Verified by rendering every `EL+AL-*` sheet at all three levels and the full report at level 3 on A4: level 1 and level 2 unchanged (the four values, no table), level 3 the table above them with both totals, no column wrapping mid-word and no overflow.

- **v0.21** (2026-08-31) — `dead-load`'s level 2 shows only the four values, on the engineer's request: `g_k`, `a_k`, `g_k + a_k` and `(g_k + a_k)_"gew"`, one plain row each (§3.3ter). No display code changed — the two sum steps in `calc/domains/dead_load.py` moved from level 2 to level 3, their vars staying at level 2, so `show-calc`'s existing rule (a var whose step sits above the level prints as the value alone) does the rest; the Σ formulas and their footnoted symbol note now appear at level 3 beside the `Schichtaufbau` table they explain. `Sections/004-einwirkungen.typ`'s dead-load loop dropped its hard-coded `level:` argument and follows the global report level again, like the snow loop beside it. Verified by rendering every `EL+AL-*` sheet at all three levels: level 1 unchanged (the gewählt value alone), level 2 exactly the four rows requested, level 3 the formulas plus the build-up table.

- **v0.20** (2026-08-30) — The snow load's presentation, reworked to the engineer's layout (§3.3bis, §10bis, §13). Every calculated value is now printed **twice over** — its formula in letters, then the same formula with the numbers put in their place and the answer set bold — and the second line is generated from the first by substituting the operands the step's own `uses` names, so it cannot disagree with the formula above it (`substituted-rhs`/`substituted-line` in `lib/calc-engine.typ`). A signed text value loses a leading `+` and parenthesises a leading `-`, so `h_ref = h_0 + Delta h` substitutes to `438 + 200`, not `438 + +200`; operands are replaced longest symbol first, so `mu_1` cannot eat the `mu_11` it prefixes; an operand the formula never mentions (`abgleiten`, which picks the branch rather than appearing in it) replaces nothing. `show-calc` stopped printing every input and then every step: it now walks a calculation **var by var in data order**, rendering a var some step produces as that step, so the printed sequence is decided in the domain module rather than here — the horizontal rule that used to divide inputs from steps went with it, the bold answer lines now carrying the rhythm. Two rules stop a value being set out twice: an *intermediate* step's operands get no row of their own, since its substituted formula already shows them (`h_0` and `Δh` appear only inside `h_ref = 438 + 200`), while a *result* step's operands keep their row with the norm reference and the choices footnote a checker reads them for; and a formula stating a **bound** rather than an equality — s_k's `... >= 0.9 kN/m²` — is not closed with `= result` (that would print a chain of two relations, only one of which happened) but keeps the compact `mit h_ref = 638 m` note above a plain result row, detected from the formula by `is-bounded` rather than declared in the data. On the data side `calc/domains/snow.py` adds its vars in the order the report prints them instead of in cell order, and levels a var **separately from the step that produces it**: `h_ref` and `s_k` are level 2 with level-3 steps, every coefficient and choice is level 3, the q_k vars and steps stay level 1. That is the whole of the requested split — level 1: the q_k formula and its numbers; level 2: h_ref, s_k, then q_k; level 3: h_ref's formula and numbers, C_e, C_T, the building geometry, Grosse Höhensprünge, Abgleiten, Lastmodell, the Dachneigungen, the Dachformbeiwerte, then s_k and q_k. `F21` is read before the vars are added, since it decides whether `H23`/`H24` are read as L/B or as α₁/α₂, and added later at the place the report shows it. Refinements the engineer asked for on seeing the first render, folded in here rather than released separately: the `mit h_ref = ...` note now trails the s_k formula on its own row instead of sitting under it; `h_ref`'s numbers line is no longer bold, since bold is now reserved for a step producing one of the `results` (`step-rows`/`substituted-line` take a `bold:` flag, and `show-calc` passes `name in results`); and a var may carry an optional **`group`** so that values the calculation states together are printed together — `C_e = 1; C_T = 1`, `L = 20.18 m; B = 7.8 m`, and likewise `alpha_1`/`alpha_2` and `mu_11`/`mu_12` on `SL-Dach2` — plus an optional **`group_label`** on the var opening the run, which names that row (`Gebäude Geometrie: L = ...; B = ...`, the one group here that needs saying what it is). Both are threaded through `engine.var()`, `snow.py`'s `add()` and `schemas/calculations.schema.json`; `show-calc` merges only *consecutive* same-group input vars, so a step between two of them breaks the run. Two Typst traps met on the way, both worth remembering: a semicolon written straight after a `#expression` in markup terminates that expression and never prints, so the rows are joined in code mode with `+ [;] +`; and `var-row`'s content was built as a multi-line markup block, whose trailing space showed up as a gap before the semicolon the moment two rows were joined — it is set on one line now, which also puts the footnote marker flush against its value. Verified by rendering both sheets at all three levels: `SL-Dach1` matches the requested layout line for line, `SL-Dach2` prints both q_(k,11) and q_(k,12) as formula-and-numbers pairs, and `dead-load` is unregressed (its `uses`-less sums fall back to `symbol = result`, now bold). ⚠️ `data/` could **not** be regenerated: `input/Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` was saved at 19:26 without its formula cache, so `H12`, `H14`, `H17`, `H26`, `H27`, `I37` and `I38` read empty on both SL sheets, and `F29` has picked up a trailing space that `_require_one_of` rejects. The verification therefore ran against a scratch copy of the last good export, reordered and relevelled exactly as `snow.py` now emits it, and `data/calculations.json` was restored untouched afterwards — reopen the workbook in Excel, save it, trim `F29`, and rerun `python calc/export_all.py`.

- **v0.19** (2026-08-30) — Prose stops being hard-wrapped (§1ter, new). The whole repository was written folded at ~72 columns — 706 of `CLAUDE.md`'s prose lines sat in the 70-79 bucket and 67 of its 93 Python comment lines likewise — which bought nothing, since every editor already wraps, and cost a readable diff: changing one word in a paragraph rewrapped the five lines after it, so `git diff` and `git blame` pointed at lines nobody had touched. One paragraph is now one line, in `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, every Python comment and docstring, every Typst `//` and `///` comment, and the German report text in `Sections/*.typ`; Markdown tables, fenced and indented code blocks, and list-item boundaries keep their line structure, and a `///` parameter line stays one item so Tinymist and `tidy` still read it (§10bis). Applied to all 23 prose-carrying files in one pass by a throwaway script deliberately not added to the repository — the convention is the thing worth keeping, not the tool. Verified per file by comparing the text with all whitespace and every comment marker normalized away, identical before and after, so nothing but line breaks moved; `calc/` still byte-compiles and Typst reports exactly the diagnostics it reported before. Untouched because unrelated: the working tree's in-progress `lib/calc-engine.typ` does not compile (`round-value` undefined, `eval` handed content), and `python calc/export_all.py` stops on `SL-Dach1!H12` holding an uncached formula cell.

- **v0.18** (2026-08-30) — Loads stop being a flat registry and become **domains with instances**, addressed `(domain, instance)` (§3.3, §8, §13). `data/loads.json`, `schemas/loads.schema.json` and `calc/loads/registry.py` are gone, replaced by `data/calculations.json`, `schemas/calculations.schema.json` and `calc/registry.py`: a domain declares a sheet prefix, and every sheet of `input/Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` carrying it is one instance. Copying a sheet in Excel (`SL-Dach3`) now adds that case to the report with no code edit — which is what the workbook's own `Objekt` sheet already told the engineer to do. An instance carries `results` (a **list**, so `SL-Dach2`'s two design loads are both first-class) and an optional `table`. Two domains ship. **`snow-load`** (prefix `SL`) is v0.17's reader generalized from the single hardcoded `Schnee` sheet — which no longer existed, so the export was broken — to every `SL-*` sheet, still with no arithmetic (§3.3bis); its printed q_k formula now follows the sheet's own `IF(F29=...)` branch (`max(mu_11, 0.8) dot ...` when the sliding is hindered) instead of a product that does not produce the number beside it. **`dead-load`** (prefix `EL+AL`) is new and goes the other way (§3.3ter): Excel holds the layer build-up, `domains/ dead_load.py` computes `(1/e) dot rho dot d dot b / 100` per layer and sums `gk`/`ak`, reading only the *Gewählt* entry below the table, which becomes the level-1 result. Verified against all five sheets' cached Excel totals — identical to 1e-12, including `EL+AL-Dach2.ohnePV`, whose own material lookups point at a missing external workbook (`[5]!Tabelle2`); the material lookup is keyed `(Ebene, Material)` and refuses to guess where the name alone is ambiguous (`Gartenplatten` is 2200 kg/m³ as a *Beschichtung*, 2000 as a *Beschwerung*), while library rows nobody uses can no longer block an export. Nothing in the EL+AL reader is pinned to a row number: the header, the `Total` line and the *Ständige Lasten* line are found by label (`io_excel`'s new `find_row()`, beside `sheet_names()` and `rows()`). On the Typst side, **`show-calc(domain, instance, level:, title:)` renders a whole calculation** and no longer takes a step name (§10bis): inputs first, then each step's formula and the value it produced, the result last — the order requested, and the order the source performs. Level 3 adds a compact `mit x = ..., y = ...` substitution line under each formula plus the instance's `table`. `show-input`/`show-check` take the same `(domain, instance, ...)` order, all three resolving through a rewritten `resolve-source()`; `inline-value(domain, instance, name)` is new, the inline counterpart to `show-input` for quoting a value inside a running sentence (`show-input` is a grid row, so it breaks one); `var-of`/`value-of` expose the raw number so a one-off calculation can be done in Typst and bound to a reusable `#let` (§1, finally implemented), a one-off calculation is then written as an ordinary Typst equation in math mode (there is deliberately no `show-*` for it), with `fmt` keeping it rounding like a generated one — untraced, and explicitly not a route around §3.5; `number-digits` makes the decimal count one document-wide setting, at most N decimals with trailing zeros dropped (§4); `domain-ids()`/`instance-ids(domain)` join `position-ids()` as data loaders, and `Sections/004-einwirkungen.typ` is now two `for` loops over them. A var's `description` and `choices` merge into **one** footnote (two adjacent markers read as a two-digit number), and a `step` may carry a `description` of its own, footnoted on its formula — a result var is never printed with notes, so an explanation of the working had nowhere to go. Neither Excel carries loads any more: `project.xlsx`'s `Loads` sheet is no longer read (a warning on every export until it is deleted by hand) and `typst-reference.xlsx` lost its `Loads` sheet, gaining `Calculations` and `Results` instead, every sheet now leading with `domain`/`instance` (§10ter). Verified: export passes with 2 domains and 7 instances, all three levels compile and render in the intended order, and each deliberate error (unknown domain, unknown instance, unknown var) reports actionably.

- **v0.17** (2026-08-30) — The snow load moves out of Python entirely (§3.3bis, §13). `calc/domains/snow.py`'s `compute_snow(s_k, mu, ce)`, which multiplied the three Excel-entered numbers, is replaced by `read_snow_load(cells)`, which contains no arithmetic at all: it reads the `Schnee` sheet of `input/Belastungsannahmen.xlsm` cell by cell into the ordinary load-registry record, `snow_roof`. Read and displayable: `h_0`, `korrektur`, `h_ref`, `ce`, `ct`, `s_k`, `hoehenspruenge`, `lastmodell`, `L`/`B`, `mu_11` and `q_k11`, plus `abgleiten` (which drives the sheet's μ ≥ 0.8 rule, so the printed q_k formula is honest) and `mu_12`/`q_k12` for a load model that has a second one. Three steps carry the sheet's formulas — Bezugshöhe, s_k, q_k. `io_excel.py` gained the `CellReader` behind `open_cells()` (the form-shaped counterpart to `read_workbook()`), which reports an empty cell, an Excel error cell, a missing sheet or a missing workbook as a blocking export error naming the cell; `domains/snow.py` adds the same treatment for a dropdown value outside its allowed set and for `SheddachLM2`'s unread third result. `choices` for the exposure and the height correction are read from the workbook's own `Dropdowns` sheet rather than restated in Python. To display a formula that hangs off a *load*, `show-calc` and `show-check` now take `(source, domain-or-none, name)` exactly as `show-input` always did, all three resolving through one new `resolve-source()` helper in `lib/calc-engine.typ` (§10bis); no call site broke, since `Sections/005`/`/006` still show no calculation. A var's `value` may now be text as well as a number (`lastmodell`, `hoehenspruenge`); `schemas/loads.schema.json` states the whole var shape explicitly, `validation.py`'s `_check_type` learned to accept a list of types, and the loads `schema_version` went `1.0` → `1.1`. `build_reference.py`'s `Domains`/`Steps`/`Checks` sheets gained `source_type`/`source` so a load's steps are catalogued like a position's (§10ter). `Sections/004-einwirkungen.typ` rewritten around the snow values in sheet order, under `== Eigengewicht` / `== Schneelast`, with no `calc-block`: both loads there are project-wide (§8). Not done, because writing to the engineer's workbook was refused: `input/project.xlsx`'s `Loads` sheet still holds the now-dead `snow_roof_A` row and its `s_k`/`mu`/`ce` columns — `loads/registry.py` skips any `snow` row there with a warning naming it, and `tools/build_example_input.py` no longer seeds either. Verified: export passes, `snow_roof` holds q_k = 1.383 kN/m² from `Schnee!I37`, and all three levels compile and render (h₀ = 438 m, Δh = +200 m, h_ref = 638 m, s_k = 1.729 kN/m², μ₁₁ = 0.8).

- **v0.16** (2026-08-30) — Three engineer-requested changes to the position display. (1) `show-position-summary` renamed **`show-position`** (call sites: `Sections/003-baustoffe.typ` and `calc-block`'s header loop). (2) It gained a `description: false` flag: the row's free text is printed only when the caller asks for it, instead of appearing automatically at `level >= 2` — so `level` is now accepted and validated but unused, like `show-check`'s. `calc-block` passes the flag through to its header summaries. `Sections/003-baustoffe.typ` opts in (`description: true`), the material inventory being where that text belongs; block headers keep the compact default, so §4's snow-load header no longer repeats "Unterzug". (3) The `Positions` sheet's `material` column is facultative — `_column()` gained an `optional=True` mode (also used for `description`) returning `""` for a blank cell or an absent column, the non-empty business rule was dropped, and the summary joins only the filled-in parts, so a row without a grade prints `D001: CLT, 180 mm` rather than `D001: CLT, , 180 mm`. Verified against a scratch position with an empty material: the default/flagged/axes forms and both `calc-block` header variants render as intended.

- **v0.15** (2026-08-30) — Added the `Positions` sheet's `units` column (`mm`), carried into `data/positions/<id>.json` as a plain string next to `dimension` and printed right after it by `show-position-summary`, so the Baustoffe list reads `T001: Träger, GL24h, 140/400 mm — Unterzug` again after v0.14 stopped synthesizing a unit. Required by `schemas/position.schema.json` but allowed to be empty, in which case nothing is printed after the dimension — no business rule forces a unit onto a row whose dimension text does not want one.

- **v0.14** (2026-08-30) — `python calc/export_all.py` crashed on the reworked workbook: the cross-section column had been renamed `dimension (b/h)` → `dimension` (a bare `KeyError`, since `io_excel.py` keys each row by the header exactly as typed), and a new CLT deck row (`D001`, dimension `180`) had no `"<b>/<h>"` pair for `_parse_dimension` to split. Both had the same root cause — a descriptive column being parsed as calculation input — so the parsing was removed rather than extended (§7, §13): `dimension` is now free text carried through as typed (`"140/400"`, `"180"`), `DIMENSION_COLUMN` and `_parse_dimension` are gone, `schemas/position.schema.json` types `dimension` as a string, `validate_positions_business` only checks it is non-empty, and `show-position-summary` prints it verbatim (so a unit is shown only if typed into the cell). Position `schema_version` bumped `1.0` → `1.1` accordingly. Every `Positions` column now goes through a `_column()` helper that reports a renamed/missing header as a blocking `export failed: Positions: no column '...' -- found: ...` instead of a traceback, and coerces a numeric cell to text. `tools/build_example_input.py` matches the new header and seeds a deck row. Verified: export passes, `D001.json` holds `"dimension": "180"`, all three levels compile, and Baustoffe lists T001/S001/D001.

- **v0.13** (2026-08-30) — `Sections/003-baustoffe.typ` displayed the material/section inventory by naming each position by hand (`#show-position-summary("T001")`, `#show-position-summary("S001")`), so every new `Positions` row needed a matching Typst edit. It now loops over every exported position instead. Because Typst cannot enumerate a directory, `calc/export_all.py` collects the ids in the loop that already writes the position files and exports them as `data/positions-index.json` (§7), validated like every other artifact against the new `schemas/positions-index.schema.json` plus a `validate_positions_index_business` rule rejecting duplicate ids — two rows sharing an id would otherwise silently overwrite each other's `data/positions/<id>.json`. On the Typst side, no new display command: just `position-ids()` next to the existing `load-position`/`load-loads` loaders, and a `for` loop in the section around the unchanged `show-position-summary`. Verified: `python calc/export_all.py` passes, all three levels compile, and the rendered inventory is identical to the hand-listed version.

- **v0.12** (2026-08-30) — Added the **calculation block** (§5), answering the report-side half of §14: `calc-block(positions, title: none, axes: none, level: auto, body)` in `lib/calc-engine.typ` groups every calculation that concerns the same position(s), renders each declared position's summary (plus its axes) as the block header, and frames the body with a left rule so everything inside is visibly tied to those positions. `positions` takes one id or an array; `axes` takes one string for all of them or one entry per position. Blocks nest. Attribution is block → position and presentation only — declaring a position writes nothing to its JSON, and a load's `show-input` inside a block creates no position↔load link (§8), so this does not reopen v0.11. Because the block knows its positions, `show-calc`/`show-check`/`show-input` (the position form) now scope-check against the enclosing block and `panic()` with an actionable message if they read a position it does not declare — catching a calculation printed under the wrong position's header, which is otherwise invisible in the finished PDF. Removed in exchange: `show-calc`'s `axes:` parameter and the `last-position-shown` banner state (implicit, order-dependent, and unable to express one block covering several positions). `show-position-summary` gained `axes: none` and prints it in parentheses. `Sections/004-einwirkungen.typ` rewritten around the new block — the project-wide roof dead load sits outside any block, the snow-load calculation inside a `calc-block("T001", ...)`. Verified: the block renders as intended at all three levels, nesting and the multi-position/per-position-axes forms work, and a deliberate out-of-scope call fails with the intended message.

- **v0.11** (2026-08-30) — A position is a purely descriptive record and nothing else. Removed `calc/export_all.py`'s `POSITION_INPUTS` dict and its `_linear_load` helper — v0.10's replacement for the deleted assignment layer, which still gave each position a span (`length_m`) and two `load_id`s with influence widths. A position now comes from exactly the columns of `input/project.xlsx`'s `Positions` sheet, plus `"domains": {}` (§7-§8). Accepted consequence, chosen explicitly over keeping the example alive by moving the span into Excel: the ELU/bending worked example no longer renders — `Sections/005-tragwerksanalyse.typ` and `006-nachweise.typ` keep their headings and prose but contain no `show-calc`/`show-check` call, and `calc/domains/bending.py` / `combinations.py` are unchanged but uncalled (a comment at the top of each says so). The surviving end-to-end path is Excel -> `loads.json` + descriptive positions -> `show-input` / `show-position-summary` in `Sections/003`/`004`. Nothing was removed from the schema, the validation rules, or `lib/calc-engine.typ` — `domains` may be empty, so all three still hold for whatever eventually fills it (§14, now the blocking open question). Two fixes to keep the pipeline runnable against the reworked workbook: the `Positions` cross-section column is now `dimension (b/h)` holding `"140/400"` (was `dimension` / `"140x400"`) — pinned in `export_all.py`'s new `DIMENSION_COLUMN` constant and matched in `tools/build_example_input.py`; and `loads/registry.py` now coerces `norm`/`norm_ref` to text via a `_as_text` helper, since Excel types a clause like `5.2` as a number and the loads schema requires a string (this was failing the export outright, before any of the above). Verified: `python calc/export_all.py` passes validation and regenerates `data/` plus `docs/typst-reference.xlsx`; all three report levels compile.

- **v0.10** (2026-08-26) — Two problems raised by the engineer: `input/ project.xlsx` and `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` overlapped (both carried a `Positions` sheet with near-duplicate content, plus a redundant `Sections` sheet on the generated side), and positions must never carry a load assignment. Fixed both. Removed entirely: the shared section catalog (`data/sections.json`, `schemas/sections.schema.json`, `calc/sections/`) and the load-to-position assignment mechanism (`data/assignments.json`, `schemas/assignments.schema.json`, `calc/loads/assignment.py`, `calc/utils/ranges.py` -- its only caller). Positions now carry `type`/`material`/`dimension`/`description` directly (§3.1, §7) -- `material` doubles as the grade/quality, `dimension` is a plain `{b, h, unit}` object parsed from the Excel `dimension` column (e.g. `"140x400"`), no catalog indirection. The elastic section modulus `W` is now a real traced step in `domains/bending.py` (`W = b·h²/6`) instead of a pre-supplied catalog value -- same numbers as before (140×400 → W = 3,733,333.33 mm³), just computed transparently per position. For the one worked example that still needs a design load, the load↔position wiring moved into a small hand-written `POSITION_INPUTS` dict in `export_all.py` (§8) -- no schema, no Excel column, ad hoc per position by design. `input/project.xlsx` gained a reference-only sheet, `data_type` (element-type symbol legend); `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` lost its `Positions` and `Sections` sheets, so the two workbooks no longer overlap at all. Position `B01` renamed `T001`, following the new `<TypeSymbol><NNN>` id convention (`T` = `Träger`, from `data_type`). `show-section` replaced by `show-position-summary(position, level: auto)` in `lib/calc-engine.typ`, which reads a position's own descriptive fields directly (no shared catalog to look up anymore). Verified end-to-end: `data/positions/T001.json`'s calculated values (`M_Ed`, `sigma_m`, `eta`) are bit-for-bit identical to the pre-refactor `B01.json`; all three report levels recompile cleanly.

- **v0.9** (2026-08-26) — Multiplication in `formula` strings now uses Typst's `dot` symbol (renders as a centered dot, like `\cdot` in LaTeX) instead of a literal `*` (which Typst math mode renders as the asterisk-operator glyph, not a multiplication sign): updated the three affected formulas in `domains/snow.py`, `domains/bending.py` (two), and `domains/combinations.py`; documented the convention in §3.1.

- **v0.8** (2026-08-26) — Doc/UX cleanup plus a batch of correctness fixes closing gaps between what §13 already called "settled" and what the code actually did (no calculated values changed for the existing `B01` example; verified end-to-end before/after). `README.md` rewritten to match the real codebase (it still described a pre-`calc-engine.typ` state) and now points to this document instead of duplicating it; fixed this document's own status banner, which had been stuck at v0.5 despite the changelog already being at v0.7; fixed `main.typ`'s `#include` paths from `sections/` to the actually-tracked `Sections/` (a case-sensitive-filesystem landmine, invisible on Windows); `main.typ` now asserts `--input level=` is `1`/`2`/`3` with an actionable message instead of failing deep in the render logic. On the Python side: `loads/assignment.py`'s `build_assignments` no longer looks up `loads` before validation runs, so `validate_assignments_business`'s unknown-`load_id` check (previously unreachable dead code) now actually fires; assigning the same load twice to one position, or two different loads under the same category, now raises a clear `ValidationError` instead of the second one silently overwriting the first (multi-zone *summation* itself remains an explicit future domain-level addition per §13, not implemented here); `validation.py` gained a generic `_check_choices` helper (replacing the `ce`-specific hardcode in `validate_loads_business`, and now applied to position vars too), `validate_sections_business` (`b`/`h`/`W` > 0, previously unchecked), and a check that every step's `result`/`uses` reference real var names in its domain; `engine.py`'s `var`/`step`/`check` now enforce `level ∈ {1,2,3}` at construction, the same way `norm`/`norm_ref` were already enforced; `loads/registry.py`'s permanent-load branch now goes through `engine.Calculation()` like every other domain instead of hand-building its dict, an unrecognized load category now raises a clear error instead of being silently treated as a permanent load, and its var key was renamed `value` → `g_k` to match its own `symbol` (`Sections/004-einwirkungen.typ` updated accordingly).

- **v0.7** (2026-08-26) — Implemented the §10bis conventions in `lib/calc-engine.typ`: `///` doc comments added above every public function, and `assert(..., message: ...)` guards added to `show-section`/`show-input`/`show-calc`/`show-check` so an unknown `level`/`domain`/`step-name`/`check-name`/`var name`/`load_id`/section key raises an immediate, actionable error listing the valid options, instead of a bare Typst dict-lookup (or field-on-none) panic. Filled in the §10bis function reference table's real signatures for `show-check`, `show-section`, and `fmt-value`; corrected `show-calc`'s documented third parameter name from `name` to the real `step-name`; reworded the `show-position-summary` row to state plainly it is not implemented. Added §10ter and `calc/tools/build_reference.py`: a generated Excel workbook, `docs/typst-reference.xlsx`, cataloging every valid `show-*` argument combination (positions, domains, steps, checks, loads, vars, and their discrete `choices`) plus the doc-comment-derived function reference — regenerated by `export_all.py` on every run, never hand-edited, versioned in Git like `data/*.json`.

- **v0.6** (2026-08-26) — Added (§10bis): the `///` doc-comment convention for every public function in `lib/calc-engine.typ` (parsed by Tinymist as editor hover docs, and by the `tidy` package for a generated reference page), plus a `show-*`/`get-*` function reference table. Documented the stable call-shape convention already in use (lookup key, then `domain`, then `name`, positionally; everything else named with a default) and the practice of failing fast with `assert(..., message: ...)` on an invalid `level`/`domain`/`ce`-style argument. `show-check`, `show-section`, `show-position-summary`, and `fmt-value` are named in the reference table but flagged *(to confirm)*: this document does not fully specify their signatures, so they still need to be filled in — and given a `///` header — directly from `lib/calc-engine.typ`.

- **v0.5** (2026-08-25) — Document translated to English. First working MWE built end-to-end (§13): `calc/` Python pipeline, `schemas/`, `lib/calc-engine.typ`, position `B01` wired into `Sections/003` through `006`. Naming aligned to English throughout the codebase (`snow.py`, `bending.py`, `combinations.py`, `actions`/`bending` domains, `--input level=`), matching CLAUDE.md's own examples. Norm references now render as `SIA 265 §4.2.1` (no comma, no brackets) in a small right-hand column, level with their equation (§10). Symbols render as real Typst math via `eval(..., mode: "math")` instead of literal text (§6bis).

- **v0.4** (2026-08-20) — Added: mandatory explicit, documented JSON schema for every file (`positions`, `sections`, `loads`, `assignments`), a dedicated `schemas/` folder; mandatory `schema_version` field in every exported JSON file; mandatory explicit unit on every numeric value; mandatory three-level validation (structure, type, business) on the Python side before export, via `validation.py`; clarified Excel's role as the primary source of input (the JSON files must never be hand-edited); load combinations (ULS, SLS) moved exclusively into Python, new `calc/domains/combinaisons.py` module; introduced `get-value`/`get-unit` abstraction functions for all JSON data access in Typst; plan axes no longer stored in position JSON files, now passed as an argument to `show-calc()` for display only. Overall architecture unchanged.

- **v0.3** (2026-08-18) — Decisions taken (§13): confirmed one JSON file per position (projects <= ~200 positions); influence widths always displayed separately, combination handled explicitly in `domains/flexion.py`/ `fleche.py` rather than automatically in the assignment step. No prototype (MWE) written at this stage, by explicit request.

- **v0.2** (2026-08-18) — Added: plan axes (X/Y) displayed automatically, global level with per-calculation override, shared section catalog (B001-B032), uniquely-identified load registry (avoids snow/wind duplicates), load assignment to a position with influence width(s) and automatic surface->line conversion, mandatory norm+clause reference on every step/var/check, description/choices for input parameters (e.g. Ce), detailed Python layout with a `norms/` folder for the SIA 265-1 tables.

- **v0.1** (2026-08-18) — First version: indexing by position, strict separation of `vars`/`steps`, report level as a compile argument, unification of Python and direct-Typst calculations.
